# Inventory AI — Claude Code Project Instructions

## 1. Project

This repository contains an AI-powered Inventory Management System for Android and iOS.

The complete system requirements are defined in:

`docs/SYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md`

That document is the primary source of truth for the system.

---

## 2. Technology Stack

### Mobile

- React Native
- Expo
- Expo Router
- TypeScript

### Backend

- Node.js
- Express.js
- TypeScript
- REST API

The backend is **TypeScript-first and has no build step**. It runs its `.ts` sources
directly through Node's native type stripping — `npm start` is `node src/index.ts` — so
Node **24**, pinned by `.nvmrc` and `engines.node`, is both the development and the runtime
target, and an older runtime cannot start the server. `tsconfig.json` sets `noEmit` and
`erasableSyntaxOnly`, so `tsc` acts purely as a checker and any syntax Node could not strip
is a compile error rather than a runtime `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_TYPESCRIPT_SYNTAX`.

The server TypeScript migration campaign (**TS-1** … **TS-2F-p**) is **complete and
closed**. `server/src` holds **117** `.ts` modules (re-counted 2026-10-10; the figure was 94 at the campaign close-out) and exactly one intentionally deferred `.js`
module, `server/src/services/entitlement.service.js`. `.ts` is canonical: new and modified
server code is written in TypeScript. `docs/TS-MIGRATION-MANIFEST.md` records the campaign
history, the deferred exception and the verification baseline.

### Database

- MongoDB Atlas
- Mongoose

### Email

- **Brevo** — the transactional mail provider in development and production, reached through the
  provider-agnostic boundary in `server/src/services/mail/` (selected by `MAIL_PROVIDER`, credential
  `BREVO_API_KEY`) and **the only built-in transport**. It delivers the AUTH-04 six-digit verification
  codes and the Phase 26B password-reset links. Mail is server-side only; no provider credential
  reaches the mobile app.

### Authentication

- Firebase Authentication — the **identity provider** (answers "who is this person?")
- JWT — the Inventory AI **application session**, exchanged from a verified Firebase ID token
- bcrypt — legacy password hashes, retained for accounts that predate Firebase, and **on a retirement path** (locked direction 2026-09-29 — design §14C)
- Server-side session records named by a mandatory `jti` (Phase 26A)

Firebase identity and Inventory AI authorization are **separate systems**. A verified Firebase ID
token is exchanged **once** at `POST /api/auth/firebase/exchange` for an ordinary Inventory AI
access token; it never becomes the API bearer credential. `firebaseUid` is an **identity linkage
field, not an authorization source** — `businessId`, `role`, `status` and permissions always come
from the MongoDB `User`, loaded per request. The Firebase exchange is **UID-authoritative**: it
resolves a user by `firebaseUid` only and never by email.

Provider architecture (Google / Facebook / Apple) and the account-linking security policy are
documented in `docs/SYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md` §29 and in the design authority
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-firebase-auth-design.md` §14/§14A/§14C. **Facebook
Login and Apple Sign-In are NOT implemented.** *(Corrected 2026-09-29: **Google Sign-In** and the server-side **link** boundary are implemented — Phases C and 32H respectively. Provider linking itself is performed by the Firebase **client** SDK and has no server-side equivalent. Corrected 2026-10-02: the server-side **unlink** boundary does **not** exist — it was removed at HEAD `6e2252f` (Phase F-1), which deleted `server/src/services/firebaseUnlink.service.ts` and re-scoped Firebase disconnect into a password change.)* They are
planned and require an owner-approved phase each.

### Firebase-only authentication — the locked direction (design only; nothing removed)

**Firebase Authentication becomes the sole authentication authority**: email/password, Google, password
reset/recovery, email verification and password changes are all Firebase's. **The Inventory AI JWT and
server-side session remain**, and **MongoDB remains authoritative** for `businessId`, `role`, `status`,
permissions, business lifecycle and tenant isolation. **Business authorization must not move into
Firebase custom claims as the primary authority**, and no custom claim is introduced, read or trusted.
Email/password users must have a **verified email before entering the main app**; a Google user is
verified according to Firebase's signed claim, not because the provider is Google.

**The registration and email-verification flow is DECIDED (2026-09-29, Phase 32O).** The gate is the
**registration ordering** — the verification email is sent first and **no MongoDB `User`, no
`Business`, no Inventory AI JWT and no server session exist until `reload()` confirms
`emailVerified === true`** — plus a **client verify screen** for the signed-in-but-unverified
identity, so **`requireAuth` is deliberately not modified** and the Phase 23/24 authorization order and
status codes are untouched. The **Firebase UID remains the only** Firebase→MongoDB identity join key
(**email is never one**); `User.businessId` stays required and `User` + `Business` stay fused; resend
is **native Firebase Auth**; and the legacy `/api/auth/register` path stays **transitional** until it is
retired. The **verification-link return is selected as the Inventory AI deep-link flow**, with the
Firebase-hosted page recorded as the fallback, and it inherits the open L-17 gate. **None of it is
implemented.** Authoritative record: design **§14C.9** (decisions **RG-1…RG-15**, audit gaps
**D-5…D-9**, **OD-3 resolved**); roadmap **§27V**; checklist **§39**.

> **AUTH-04 supersession (2026-10-02).** Email verification is now a **custom 6-digit Inventory AI code
delivered through the provider-agnostic mail boundary** (Brevo, the provider used in development and
production and for the live acceptance), **not** a Firebase verification link,
and the client resends through Inventory AI
rather than native Firebase Auth. A successful confirmation sets Firebase's own `email_verified` state
through the Admin SDK (`updateUser(uid, { emailVerified: true })`). The registration **ordering** gate,
the **Firebase-UID-only** join, MongoDB authority and the unmodified **`requireAuth`** are preserved, and
the server-side **`EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED`** enforcement remains. This supersedes the link/resend and
verification-link-return text above. See design **§14C.10**, checklist **§40**, roadmap **§27Y**.
>
> **AUTH-04 status (2026-10-04).** AUTH-04-A through AUTH-04-F are **shipped**; **AUTH-04-G PASSED its
> live end-to-end acceptance gate on Brevo** (request HTTP 200 with the exact success envelope, Brevo
> `sent` → `delivered`, confirmation HTTP 200 with `Email verified.`, Firebase `emailVerified` false →
> true, Mongo challenge consumed, safe-log checks passed); **AUTH-04-H** is the documentation close-out, **complete** — commit `0972948`, 2026-10-03.
> The request path enforces a **60-second per-UID resend cooldown**; restart-while-unverified is
> **resolved by the shipped AUTH-04-F read-only probe** (unverified → verification screen, verified →
> Login, probe failure → unreachable state; no automatic sign-in or session restoration).

**Nothing has been implemented or removed by this decision.** The application-owned password path —
bcrypt login/register, the custom reset-token flow and the mail layer, and admin-created
password accounts — is on a **retirement path**, with **invitation-based onboarding a hard prerequisite**
of removing legacy login (because `users.service.create` writes a password hash and no `firebaseUid`).
A Firebase-native credential change completes client-side and would therefore revoke **nothing**, so a
**credential-change revocation bridge** is required before native recovery or change-password ships.
The action-link return leg must be verified on a real device against a live Firebase project before
recovery is called complete, and iOS remains `[EXTERNAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED]`.

Authority for all of the above: `docs/SYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md` §29 · design authority
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-firebase-auth-design.md` **§14C** (locked decisions L-1…L-19, the
bridge, the order plan) · security record `docs/AUTH_SECURITY_MASTER_CHECKLIST.md` **§39** · shipped
record `docs/DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md` **§27U**.

### AI

- AI provider abstraction (the interface and the shared Anthropic wire layer stay)
- **One shipped provider: OpenRouter** — an Anthropic-compatible gateway, selected server-side by
  `AI_PROVIDER` and requiring `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` + `OPENROUTER_BASE_URL` + `AI_MODEL_OPENROUTER`, with
  **no default model**. **Claude and Gemini models are reachable through it** by changing
  `AI_MODEL_OPENROUTER`. The **direct Claude and Gemini provider implementations were removed**
  (owner decision, **AI-25**, 2026-10-19) — they needed their own keys, model variables and billing and
  were unexercised in production — so `AI_PROVIDER=claude|gemini` is now refused as unsupported, while
  the abstraction means a direct provider can return as one factory plus one registry entry. The
  retired AgentRouter gateway has been removed and must not be reintroduced. See
  `docs/DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md` §27S and §27DR.
- AI credentials must remain server-side

## 3. Repository Structure

```text
/
├── CLAUDE.md
├── docs/
├── mobile/
├── server/
└── README.md
```

Do not place backend code inside `mobile/`.

Do not place mobile code inside `server/`.

Keep concerns separated.

---

# 4. Mandatory Architecture Rules

These rules must always be respected.

### Database

MongoDB is the source of truth.

The mobile application must NEVER connect directly to MongoDB.

Correct:

```text
Mobile → Backend → MongoDB
```

Incorrect:

```text
Mobile → MongoDB
```

### AI

The AI must NEVER connect directly to MongoDB.

Correct:

```text
Mobile
  ↓
Backend
  ↓
AI Service
  ↓
Controlled Tool
  ↓
MongoDB
```

Never provide MongoDB credentials or unrestricted database access to an AI provider.

### Authorization

The backend is the security boundary.

Always enforce:

- Authentication
- Authorization
- Role permissions
- Business isolation

on the server.

Do not rely on the mobile UI to enforce security.

### Business Isolation

Every business-owned resource must be isolated by `businessId`.

Never trust a client-provided `businessId`.

Determine business context from the authenticated user/session.

### Inventory

Inventory changes must follow the inventory business rules defined in:

`docs/SYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md`

Inventory changes must create appropriate inventory transactions.

Do not allow negative inventory unless explicitly specified.

Products follow an archive-only lifecycle: `ACTIVE` <-> `ARCHIVED`, with `ARCHIVED`
terminal. Products are never hard-deleted by the application — no delete route,
service method, or cascade exists, and none may be added without explicit
specification. Archiving retains the product's inventory records and its full
transaction history.

Referential integrity between inventory and products is a responsibility of
application lifecycle rules and write-path validation, not of MongoDB. Mongoose
`ref` does not enforce anything; read-path filters are defensive query hygiene,
not integrity enforcement.

### AI V1

The AI assistant is READ-ONLY.

It may retrieve, summarize, explain, and analyze authorized inventory information.

It must NOT modify:

- Products
- Inventory
- Transactions
- Users
- Roles

unless a future specification explicitly enables these capabilities.

---

# 5. Security Rules

Never:

- Hardcode secrets
- Commit `.env`
- Expose API keys to the mobile application
- Expose MongoDB credentials
- Trust client authorization
- Trust AI authorization
- Return sensitive internal errors to clients

Use environment variables for secrets.

Maintain an `.env.example` containing variable names but never real credentials.

---

# 6. Development Rules

Before implementing a feature:

1. Read the relevant specification.
2. Inspect the existing implementation.
3. Understand the current architecture.
4. Create a short implementation plan.
5. Implement only the requested scope.

Do not blindly rewrite existing code.

Do not introduce unnecessary dependencies.

Prefer simple and maintainable solutions.

---

# 7. Phase Discipline

The project is developed in phases.

```text
Phase 00 — Planning & Inspection
Phase 01 — Project Foundation
Phase 02 — Backend + Database
Phase 03 — Authentication + Authorization
Phase 04 — Inventory Core
Phase 05 — Mobile Application
Phase 06 — AI Assistant
Phase 07 — Testing + Production Hardening
Phase 08 — Owner-Directed Hardening (lettered increments)
            08A — Structured request logging + correlation IDs
            08B — Backend ESLint baseline
Phase 10 — Commercialization Foundation (lettered increments)
            10A — SaaS plan state + centralized entitlement checks
            10B — Documentation close-out
Phase 11 — Inventory Intelligence & Alerts (lettered increments)
            11A — Inventory health counts + reorder list + read-only restock tool
            11B — Documentation close-out
Phase 14A — User Management Foundation (OWNER/ADMIN users.manage API: list/create/role+status; no OWNER, no self-change, ADMIN↔ADMIN restricted)
Phase 16 — Audit Trail Viewer (read-only GET /api/audit: OWNER/ADMIN governance trail; action/entityType/user/date/q filters; no write routes)
Phase 17 — Mobile Supplier & Purchase Order Management (suppliers CRUD/archive + PO create/edit/issue/cancel on mobile; zero backend changes; STAFF view-only)
Phase 19 — Stock Transfers Between Locations (POST /api/inventory/transfer: two-leg TRANSFER movement + Phase 07A compensation; existing INVENTORY_MANAGE permission; MANAGER+)
Phase 21 — Product Archive-Only Lifecycle & Inventory Invariant (documentation + regression guards only; no production behavior change)
Phase 22 — Authorization Contract Enforcement & RBAC Coverage Guards (read routes bound to their view permissions; `CATALOGUE_ONLY_PERMISSIONS` declared; no role grant changed)
Phase 23 — Authorization Contract Completion: Route Classification & RBAC Mirror Guards (every route classified into a declared authorization bucket; mobile RBAC mirror pinned to the server matrix; `requireRole` inertness and client error-code contract; no production behavior change)
Phase 24 — Runtime Authorization Matrix & Tenant-Isolation Verification (runtime role × real-route matrix derived from `ROLE_PERMISSIONS`; role-change effect proven on an existing JWT in both directions; empty OWNER-only runtime tier asserted; no production behavior change)
Phase 25 — Design-Token Contrast Close-Out & Mobile Known-Issue Reconciliation (two light-theme tokens raised to WCAG AA on every surface they are used on; measured, not eyeballed; no production behavior change)
Phase 26A — Session Hardening & Real Logout (server-side session records named by a mandatory `jti`; real server-side sign-out, per-session revocation and sign-out-everywhere; no new permission, no refresh-token rotation; every pre-26A token is rejected once)
Phase 26B — Password Recovery (email reset link: provider-agnostic mail layer — Brevo is the transport in use — with single-use hashed reset tokens; `POST /api/auth/forgot-password` and `POST /api/auth/reset-password` added as two PUBLIC routes; no new permission, no new dependency, no mobile change; the request path answers one identical 202 to every caller)
Phase 27 — Mobile Password Recovery (`/forgot-password` and `/reset-password` public screens consuming the existing Phase 26B endpoints; custom-scheme reset link `inventoryai://reset-password`; a successful reset clears the client session before returning to Login; ZERO backend changes and no new dependency; Universal Links / Android App Links deferred; iOS verification export-only)
Phase 28 — Password Reset Mail Delivery Timing Hardening (B1 ONLY — the outbound password-reset email send is detached from the HTTP request path, so the 202 no longer waits on a mail provider: the send is dispatched unawaited after the durable token and audit writes, with an explicit rejection handler attached in the same synchronous turn; the pre-lookup `MAIL_NOT_CONFIGURED` 503, the uniform 202, token semantics, audit ordering, rate limiting and mobile are unchanged; PARTIALLY MITIGATED, NOT CLOSED — the residual database-work timing term is NOT eliminated; B2 was NOT implemented and remains open)
Phase 29 — Residual Security-Risk Decision Record & Documentation Reconciliation (documentation only; records the owner's dispositions of the three remaining open security items — B1's residual database-work timing term ACCEPTED FOR V1 and DEFERRED, still PARTIALLY MITIGATED and NOT CLOSED; B2 and E3 DEFERRED TO V4 as related shared-infrastructure work, both still open — and reconciles the roadmap Phase Overview, the missing README Phase 26B entry, and the specification's §48 future-roadmap list with what has already shipped; no production code, test, dependency, configuration, limiter or password-reset behavior changed, no shared store introduced, and no historical verification snapshot back-updated)
Phase 30A — EAS Development Build & iOS Readiness Foundation (EAS foundation only: the Expo Continuous Native Generation architecture is retained and no `ios/` or `android/` directory exists or is committed; the project is linked to EAS project `@jeffernqwes-team/inventory-ai` — `607eca05-1175-43ed-86a0-1829e91c9531`; `mobile/eas.json` defines `development`, `ios-simulator`, `preview` and `production` with `cli.appVersionSource: "remote"` and no update channel; `expo-dev-client` added and `expo-updates` deliberately not installed; bundle/package identifier `com.inventoryai.app` on both platforms; NO EAS build, submit or prebuild has been executed, so the phase is statically validated only and NOT runtime-verified; F-1 — the Phase 30A **PDF-sharing** finding, unrelated to the much later registration required-field validation fix recorded in the unnumbered entry below — **is FIXED in code as of 2026-10-11 (§27AM: both print actions pass the same label geometry, and two of the 8 guard cases fail if the fix is reverted), with only its print → scan round trip left unverified on hardware**; Phase 30B has NOT started, and is now **defined** in the entry below)

Phase 30B — Native & Device Acceptance (defined 2026-10-11 by owner decision; **NOT started**): the work that is unreachable from JavaScript-only CI, and the only home for the items that need a native build. Scope — **ISS-02** the RNFirebase Auth PII log re-checked in logcat on a `development` build made **after** the `patch-package` patch (the client installed on the phone predates it, F-26); **ISS-06** the **generated** `minSdk`/`compileSdk`/`targetSdk` read out of the build and recorded, with `expo-build-properties` entering only if the app's own levels need raising; **ISS-13** the PDF label printed and **scanned back**; the **authenticated device run** — Firebase-first sign-in, AUTH-04 verification, tenant creation, then dashboard/inventory/locations/stock in-out/transfers/suppliers/purchase orders/AI assistant/audit trail; and **iOS**, which is **environment-blocked** (no macOS on this host). Definition of done — every item carries a **device-measured observation** with its date, blocked items are recorded as blocked and **never** as passing, **no item is closed from a code read alone**, and the phase adds **no** dependency, route, permission or schema change. It is **not** the SDK 58 train (§27AI) and **not** Phase 30A's EAS foundation, which is complete; the one item once assigned here, ISS-13, was already fixed in code (§27AM). Roadmap §27AW.)
Phase 32B — Firebase Admin SDK Foundation (server-only verify boundary: Admin SDK dependency, `config/firebase.ts`, ID-token verifier wrapper, environment validation + production fail-fast, `.env.example` names, and three additive `users` fields — `firebaseUid` String `sparse: true` unique, `emailVerified` Boolean persisted but NOT enforced, `passwordHash.required: false`; NO routes)
Phase 32C — Firebase Authentication Session Exchange (two PUBLIC routes `POST /api/auth/firebase/exchange` and `POST /api/auth/firebase/register`, their service and rate limiters; the Firebase ID token is exchanged ONCE for an existing Inventory AI access token and never becomes the API bearer credential; `REGISTRATION_REQUIRED` for a verified identity with no MongoDB user; a colliding email is refused with `EMAIL_IN_USE` — linking is REFUSED, not implemented; `requireAuth`, RBAC and the legacy path unchanged)
Phase 32D — Firebase JS SDK Boundary (mobile `lib/firebase.ts` ONLY: SDK version, initialization order, the `inMemoryPersistence` decision and the `EXPO_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` gate that leaves the SDK uninitialized; infrastructure only — no screen reaches Firebase, no API call is made, no authentication state is touched)
Phase 32E — Firebase Email/Password Authentication Flow (mobile Firebase-first sign-in exchanging its ID token through the 32C endpoint, with the legacy password route reached ONLY for the two codes that mean "no Firebase identity answers to these credentials" — `auth/invalid-credential` and `auth/user-not-found` — never for a Firebase or backend failure, so an outage cannot become a downgrade; plus the server-side `firebaseUid` guard refusing the legacy route with 403 `FIREBASE_SIGN_IN_REQUIRED`; Google Sign-In NOT implemented; account linking and migration NOT implemented)
Phase 32H — Server-Side Account Linking Boundary & Authentication Ownership (ONE authenticated route `POST /api/auth/firebase/link` — `requireAuth` + controller, no permission — attaching a verified Firebase identity to the CALLER'S OWN account; the account is `req.user.id` and the identity is the verified token's uid, so no client-supplied `userId`/`businessId`/`firebaseUid`/`role`/`status` can steer it; `validateFirebaseLink` returns only `{problems, idToken}`; a link writes only `firebaseUid` + `emailVerified`, so it can never move a tenant or change a role; CASE A idempotent, CASE B and CASE D refused with ONE 409 `FIREBASE_IDENTITY_CONFLICT` that never names an account, tenant, role or uid, never merged or re-pointed; a rejected Firebase token answers 400 not 401 so a bad provider credential cannot sign the user out; NO schema change, NO new dependency, NO session-model change, and the Phase 32E guard untouched; Google Sign-In, Facebook Login, Apple Sign-In, provider SDKs, OAuth redirects, unlinking and invitations all NOT implemented — provider linking is performed by the Firebase CLIENT SDK and no server-side equivalent was faked)
Phase 32J — Mobile Firebase Registration Onboarding (an identity Firebase has already created but the exchange refuses with `403 REGISTRATION_REQUIRED` is routed into a registration/onboarding screen that completes the Inventory AI account; Google and email/password converge on the same route; roadmap §27L)
Phase 32K — Firebase Password Authority Boundary & Timing-Equalizer Credential Fix (the Phase 32H-escalated O-2 defect is made true in code for every `firebaseUid` account, with a timing-equalizer credential fix; roadmap §27M)
Phase 32L — Firebase Identity Unlink (32L-A server `POST /api/auth/firebase/unlink` under **Policy B** — an account may leave Firebase authority only by setting a new local password in the same transition — and 32L-B the mobile flow; no new permission; server-side last-authentication-method invariant made structural; roadmap §27Q) — **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-02 (Phase F-1, HEAD `6e2252f`): the unlink route and `server/src/services/firebaseUnlink.service.ts` were REMOVED and Firebase disconnect was re-scoped into a password change; no unlink capability exists in the committed tree.** **Removal identifier, disambiguated — the repository also carries unrelated `F-1`/`F1` labels (the Phase F1 SDK retirement, roadmap §27N, and the Phase 30A PDF-sharing finding): `Phase A / F-1 — 6e2252f4c63a7bd98e1d63a4703f21ab236c93a9`.** See `docs/AUTH_SECURITY_MASTER_CHECKLIST.md` §8.
Phase F1 — Retire the Firebase JS SDK and the Browser OAuth Path (`mobile/lib/firebase.ts`, the `firebase` dependency and the `expo-auth-session` browser path removed; imports redirected to the SDK-free `lib/config.ts` first; pure JavaScript, no new build; roadmap §27N)
Phase F2 — Retire the Browser Dependencies, the Plugin, the Second Scheme and the Native-Intent Hook (`expo-auth-session`, `expo-web-browser` + its plugin and `expo-crypto` removed; `expo.scheme` is exactly `["inventoryai"]`; `app/+native-intent.ts` deleted; roadmap §27O)
Phase F2.9 — EAS Development Build & Android Runtime/Native Verification of F2 (a `development` Android build installed and run; the retained `inventoryai://reset-password` deep link renders and the retired callback is unresolvable at the OS level; authentication E2E remains environmentally unverified; roadmap §27P)
Mail provider consolidation (2026-10-20; server + documentation; **not** an application phase) — the unused **Resend transport is removed**: `server/src/services/mail/providers/resendProvider.ts` deleted, the registry's `BUILTINS` table left with `brevo` alone (`MAIL_PROVIDER=resend` is refused as unsupported, naming Brevo), `RESEND_API_KEY` gone from `.env.example`, the mobile build-env guard, the deployment warning and every test scrub list, and **Brevo — the provider in development and production — untouched**. The ten Resend wire-format tests went with the module; everything provider-agnostic stayed, and two new guards fail if `resend` is ever re-registered. No route, permission, role, session, schema, index, dependency, EAS or mobile change; server **993 unit tests / 0 failures**, the DB recovery suite **31 pass / 0 fail**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean. Roadmap: the dated *Mail provider consolidation* record after §27Y)
Registration required-field validation fix (mobile; **not** the Phase 30A "F-1" PDF-sharing finding and **not** a "Phase F1/F2" cleanup phase) — `mobile/app/register.tsx` `submit()` now checks that business name, name, email, password and confirmation are each non-empty before it calls `signUp()`, so an empty register form is rejected client-side instead of reaching the Firebase sign-up path; the pre-existing non-empty confirm-mismatch rule is preserved (an empty confirmation reports "required", a differing non-empty one still reports "Passwords do not match"); guarded by the new focused suite `mobile/__tests__/register-validation.test.tsx` (6 cases); no backend, dependency, permission, route or session change; roadmap §27R)

AUTH-05 — The Reset Email's Link Returns to the App (2026-10-20; **not** an application phase) — Firebase will not mail a link that opens a custom scheme (`ActionCodeSettings.url` must be on an **Authorized domain**; the live project refused the send with `auth/unauthorized-domain` and generated no mail), so the send now passes **no `ActionCodeSettings`** and the emailed link's destination is the project's password-reset template, which points at a new **public `GET /auth/reset`** page (`server/src/pages/resetPasswordLink.ts`, mounted **outside `/api`** because it is HTML read by a phone's browser, not JSON). The page validates the one-time `oobCode` against an allow-list (refused, never escaped and forwarded), forwards it to `inventoryai://reset-password`, and offers Firebase's hosted page as the fallback for a phone without the app; the only navigation targets are constants, so a crafted `continueUrl` is inert, the inline script and style are authorized by **CSP hash computed from the bytes served**, and every response is `no-store`/`no-referrer`/`noindex`/`nosniff` with the access log recording the **path and never the query** (proved over the real app — neither the code nor the `apiKey` can reach a log line). The two-shape send recovery, its code pattern and its "contact your administrator" sentence were **deleted with the request that needed them** (one send, never two). The route inventory moves **72 → 73 (public 12 → 13; gated stays 47)**; server **1685 tests / 0 failures** (10 new), mobile **78 suites / 979 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean. **Device-verified on the OPPO CPH1969** over `adb reverse`: Chrome was pointed at the page on the laptop and the app opened directly on *Set a new password*; the reset was then **completed on the phone** with a throwaway `auth05.probe@example.invalid` identity — *Password changed* on screen, the **new** password signed in as far as the server's `REGISTRATION_REQUIRED` banner, the **old** one answered *Invalid email or password*, and the probe was deleted afterwards, which also proves the code reaches the app **unconsumed** (the hosted page would have spent it); and a truncated query rendered the 400 *This reset link is not usable* page. **The one step left is console configuration** — Templates → Password reset → *Customize action URL* = `https://ai-inventory-l1mi.onrender.com/auth/reset`, with that host in Authorized domains — which no code can set, so **L-17 stays OPEN** until a real production reset email opens the app; iOS remains unverified, and a reset completed on the hosted fallback page still revokes nothing (the D2/OD-9 gap). Roadmap §27DX, design §14C.4 / §2.6, checklist F-14)

AUTH-06 — Password Reset by Six-Digit Code, Inside the App (2026-10-20; **not** an application phase) — the browser half of password recovery is replaced by the experience account creation already has: the person types their address, the **server** mints a six-digit code and mails it through **Brevo**, and the app takes the code *and* the new password on its own screen. It exists for two measured reasons: the Firebase console refuses every email-template write (`EMAIL_TEMPLATE_UPDATE_NOT_ALLOWED`, Google issue 520781452), so a reset email whose destination **we** choose could not be configured at all — and even the template Firebase does control lands on a **browser form that revokes nothing**, which is the recorded D2/OD-9 gap. Two **public** routes, `POST /api/auth/password-reset/code` (202) and `POST /api/auth/password-reset/confirm` (200); a `password_reset_challenges` collection keyed by a keyed digest of the address (`reset-email:`) and holding the code's digest (`reset-code:`), so **no new secret and no new environment variable** — it reuses `EMAIL_VERIFICATION_CODE_PEPPER` and is separated from the verification flow by **digest domain labels**, not by a second pepper. Six digits, a 10-minute lifetime, 5 attempts and a 60-second resend cooldown, all enforced server-side and **pinned to the mobile copy by a test that reads the server's constants out of its source**. The request path answers **one 202 and one sentence** for a known address, an unknown one and a mail failure alike (the send is detached with its rejection handled, so the 202 never waits on Brevo), and `MAIL_NOT_CONFIGURED` is a **pre-lookup** 503. The confirm path resolves the account from the **challenge** — never from the submitted address — sets the password through the Admin SDK, stamps `passwordChangedAt`, calls `revokeAllSessions` (making this the **first** reset flow that actually revokes), revokes Firebase refresh tokens best-effort, consumes the challenge and writes a `PASSWORD_RESET_COMPLETED` audit row; a wrong code spends exactly one attempt, and a malformed payload spends none. Mobile: `forgot-password` posts the address and navigates, `reset-password-code.tsx` is the new screen, and the Firebase **send** path is **removed** (`sendFirebasePasswordResetEmail`, its outcome union, its enumeration-resistance code list and its native verb) while the **redemption** half stays, so a `?oobCode=` link already in an inbox still works. Route inventory **73 → 75** (public **13 → 15**; gated stays 47); server **1742 tests** — `test:unit` **981 pass / 0 fail**, `test:db` **761 pass** with **one** failure in `phase-ai-usage-report` that is an Atlas `MongoNetworkError` (`ai:usage` exited 2, "NOT VERIFIED"), passed in the other DB run and touches nothing here — mobile **79 suites / 977 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean. **Not device-verified**, and **L-17 stays OPEN** — it now gates only the AUTH-05 hosted-link path, which this flow does not depend on and does not repair; iOS remains unverified. **The owner's *Send Reset Code* error was diagnosed on the device and fixed, 2026-10-21 — it was never this flow.** The screen reported `Cannot reach the server` + `server: NETWORK_ERROR`: the dev build resolves its API base to `http://localhost:4000` and `adb reverse --list` was **empty**, so the request never left the handset — and the listener that owned port 4000 was a **day-old process from before AUTH-06**, which would have answered `404` even with the tunnel up. Independently, **nothing was deployed**: the live host answered both new routes `404`. The stale listener was restarted on current source, `adb reverse tcp:4000 tcp:4000` established, and the same tap on the OPPO CPH1969 then answered **202 in 440.3 ms** with `a reset code was issued` in the log and the device on *Reset your password*; the work is committed as `b9d6ec9` and the live host now answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` on both routes, `400` (invalid-link page) on `GET /auth/reset`, **202 in 0.21 s** for an address that cannot exist, and **400 INVALID_PASSWORD_RESET_CODE in 0.32 s** for an unknown address on the confirm route. **The inbox half is now measured (owner report, 2026-10-21):** the six-digit code reached the mailbox, the new password was set on the phone, and it signed in without error — so Brevo delivery and the credential it authorized are both live facts, not inferences from a 202. The **old** password's refusal was not re-typed by the owner and remains server-suite-backed. Two client fixes followed in the same pass: the code screen now calls `clearSession` on success (without it *Go to sign in* reached `/login`, which redirected into `(app)` on a session the reset had already ended — `reset-password.tsx` had always done this and said why), and both six-digit fields plus Login now declare `autoComplete`/`textContentType` (`one-time-code`/`oneTimeCode`, `email`/`emailAddress`, `current-password`/`password`) so the platform can offer the code and the saved credential; mobile **80 suites / 983 tests / 0 failures**, no server change. Roadmap §27DY, design §2.6 / §14C.4, checklist §49)

AUTH-06 two-step recovery (2026-10-22; **not** an application phase; one new public route and one new mobile screen — no new dependency, no new environment variable, no permission, no change to the six-digit code, the mail boundary or the Firebase credential write) — the recovery journey no longer asks for the code and the new password on one form. **Step one** is `POST /api/auth/password-reset/verify` (`{ email, code }` → 200 `{ resetToken, expiresInSeconds }`): it spends one attempt, writes no credential, issues no session, and on a correct code mints a **32-byte CSPRNG reset authorization** whose only persisted form is a keyed digest (`reset-authorize:` domain, the same pepper, so **no new secret**) on the challenge row under a **sparse unique** index. **Step two** is the existing `POST /api/auth/password-reset/confirm`, now `{ resetToken, newPassword, confirmPassword }` and **no code and no email**: the challenge is found by the authorization's digest, so the account is the one recorded when the code was issued and a `userId`, `businessId` or address in the body has no field to travel through — navigating to the password screen therefore yields nothing the server accepts. The code is **single-use from verification** (`verifiedAt` is part of the verify predicate, so replaying the six digits is refused); the authorization is single-use from a successful password write, gets its own **15-minute** window, and the row's `expiresAt` is pushed forward so the TTL index cannot delete it. It is **not** consumed by a failed Admin write, so an outage does not force a new email. A new code request unsets both. Refusals stay generic (`INVALID_PASSWORD_RESET_CODE`, new `INVALID_PASSWORD_RESET_AUTHORIZATION`), the verify path answers the same 400 for an unknown address as for a wrong code, and both routes share the existing per-IP confirm limiter. **Mobile**: `reset-password-code.tsx` is code-only (one field, resend with the server-stated cooldown, and a note that checking changes nothing yet); on success it stores the authorization in `lib/passwordResetSession.ts` — **memory, never a route param, never persisted** — and `router.replace`s to the new `reset-password-new.tsx`, which applies the shared password policy, posts only the token and the password, and clears both the authorization and this device's session before *Password changed* → Login. With no authorization in memory the password screen renders a *Verify your code first* state instead of a form; a definitive `INVALID_PASSWORD_RESET_AUTHORIZATION` drops the form and offers *Start again*. Route inventory **75 → 76** (public **15 → 16**; gated stays 47), recorded in design §2.6, the Phase 23 public register and the Phase 26B recovery-surface guard. Both six-digit screens — this one and Create Account's email verification — now share **`mobile/components/CodeInput.tsx`**, six boxes over one invisible-but-real `TextInput` (so auto-advance is the caret position, paste is a native single `onChangeText`, and backspace is a shorter value; `one-time-code`/`oneTimeCode` autofill is preserved). Server `test:unit` **1264 / 1211 pass / 0 fail / 53 skipped**, `test:db` **864 / 774 pass / 0 fail / 90 skipped**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean; mobile **82 suites / 1010 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc` clean. **Device-verified 2026-10-22** on an EAS `development` build on the OPPO CPH1969 against the local server: a wrong code spent exactly one attempt and minted nothing, an expired one answered the same generic 400, the correct one opened the password step, the OS paste filled all six boxes in one action, and after *Password changed* the old password was refused while the new one signed in on the phone — with a session issued **before** the reset answering **401 `SESSION_INVALIDATED`**. **Create Account** was driven to a new tenant the same session. Codes were minted by a throwaway script for that run (a probe address has no inbox). **The production journey was then completed with a real inbox** on the same build pointed at `https://ai-inventory-l1mi.onrender.com`: a controlled account on a disposable inbox was registered through Create Account (its verification mail arrived from Brevo, and the row was later found in `inventoryai_prod_db` and absent from the dev database), the reset mail *Your Inventory AI password reset code* arrived, its code was typed into the six boxes, the password step opened and answered **Password changed**; afterwards the **new** password was accepted (and signed in on the phone), the **old** one was refused `INVALID_LOGIN_CREDENTIALS`, a session issued **before** the reset answered **401 `SESSION_INVALIDATED`**, `passwordChangedAt` was stamped, and a wrong code for a known address produced a body **byte-identical** to the same wrong code for an unknown one. The probe user, tenant, challenges and Firebase identity, and the disposable inbox, were all deleted afterwards. **iOS remains unverified.** Roadmap §27EI)

Security audit — authentication, verification and rate limiting (2026-10-23; **not an application phase**; server + documentation — **no route, permission, role, schema, index, dependency, session-model or mobile change**) — an owner-requested audit of the whole authentication surface (registration, the AUTH-04 code, AUTH-06 recovery, login, sessions and renewal, enumeration, error hygiene, validation, every auth limiter, the production proxy configuration) and of the wider API (authorization, tenant isolation, query construction, headers, CORS, logging, error handling) **found five problems and fixed all five**: `POST /api/auth/change-password` had **no limiter at all** (High — `currentPassword` is its only re-authentication, so a live session could guess the account password at machine speed and install a new credential, and every attempt cost a bcrypt comparison), and `POST /api/auth/firebase/link` and `POST /api/auth/credentials-changed` were unlimited (Medium — each spends an outbound Firebase `checkRevoked` and an audit write, the second also a `revokeAllSessions` update); these three now have limiters **after `requireAuth`** (account+IP 10/15 min then IP 30/15 min for the password route; account 10/15 min for link; account 20/15 min for credentials-changed) answering 429 `CHANGE_PASSWORD_RATE_LIMITED` / `FIREBASE_LINK_RATE_LIMITED` / `CREDENTIALS_CHANGED_RATE_LIMITED`. A production process behind a proxy with `TRUST_PROXY` unset silently collapsed **every per-IP bucket into one global bucket** (Medium, operational — 60 failed logins from anywhere would 429 every member for 15 minutes), so `proxyTrustNote()` in `config/deploymentReadiness.ts` now makes `startServer` log a `[config] warning:` naming the variable and the consequence — a warning, not a refusal, because a degraded deployment must keep serving. `/api/auth` responses now carry `Cache-Control: no-store` + `Pragma: no-cache` (Low — a token or reset authorization could otherwise be cached), scoped to that mount and asserted not to leak. Everything else was checked and left alone with its evidence recorded (CSPRNG codes stored only as keyed domain-separated HMACs and compared timing-safely; atomic expiry/attempt/single-use predicates; uniform 202/400 on both recovery surfaces and timing-equalized login; uid-only Firebase joins; `jti` sessions with the 14-day cap and `passwordChangedAt`; claim-free HS256 tokens; server-derived tenant filters everywhere including AI conversations by business **and** user; escaped `$regex`, capped pagination, 100 kb body cap; fail-closed stack traces; path-only access logs; default-deny CORS + helmet; the mobile client's 429/`Retry-After` handling). Eight new optional `AUTH_*` names are documented in `server/.env.example` and the README (now **thirty** variables / fifteen pairs). **12 new cases, 4 existing guards updated** (each still asserting `requireAuth` first and no role gate); server `test:unit` **1269 / 1216 pass / 0 fail / 53 skipped**, `test:db` **871 / 781 pass / 0 fail / 90 skipped**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean (486 modules); mobile **83 suites / 1016 tests / 0 failures** (`tsc` clean), including `mobile/__tests__/change-password-rate-limit.test.tsx` — 4 cases pinning the client half the audit recorded as unverified: a 429 shows the server's sentence and **keeps** the session, the form stays usable after the refusal, a 400 field error lands under its input, and a 401 ends the session. **Device-verified 2026-10-23** on the OPPO CPH1969 over `adb reverse` (local server, documented demo tenant, a disposable legacy account): the change-password **429** rendered the server's own sentence in 177 ms and the next `GET /api/dashboard` still answered 304, so **the session survived the refusal**; a server-side `passwordChangedAt` bump made the next requests answer **401** and the app **cleared the session back to Login**; a real change answered **200** with its follow-up `logout` **401** (already invalidated) while the app still returned to Login cleanly and the **new** password signed in; and the demo dashboard rendered **6 / 3 / 3 / 1 / 112** exactly. Residual V1 risks are recorded in checklist §52: in-process limiter state, distributed stuffing, the shared-address lockout window (an attacker holding a session plus N addresses gets N×10 change-password guesses; the per-address bucket still bounds one source at 30 / 15 min), per-IP rather than per-recipient email bounds, and no per-user session cap. Checklist §52; roadmap §27EJ.

Firebase email-template review (2026-10-23; **not** an application phase; mobile only — **no route, permission, schema, dependency, session, environment or Firebase-console change, and no behaviour change**) — an owner review of whether Firebase's password-reset and email-verification machinery is still needed now that both journeys are this application's own. **Everything Firebase does as the identity provider stays**: native email/password and Google sign-in, the forced ID token, `reload()`/`emailVerified`, and on the server the Admin SDK's `verifyIdToken`, the `emailVerified` and password writes and `revokeRefreshTokens`. **No code asks Firebase to send an email** — AUTH-06 removed `sendPasswordResetEmail`, and AUTH-04 replaced the verification message with the server's six-digit code — so `nativeSendEmailVerification`, exported with **no caller in `app/` or `lib/`**, is **removed** together with its two boundary cases (replaced by the equivalent pair for `nativeUpdatePassword`, which had none) and the `sendEmailVerification` entry in the test's SDK mock. **Deliberately NOT removed:** `GET /auth/reset`, the `?oobCode=` redemption in `reset-password.tsx` and the console's *Customize action URL*, because Firebase's own `accounts:sendOobCode` API (the project key ships in the binary) and the console's per-user *Reset password* action can still compose that mail, and our page is the only destination at which such a reset **revokes sessions** — Firebase's hosted form revokes nothing (D2/OD-9). The earlier "a link already in somebody's inbox" justification was corrected in place. Mobile `tsc` clean; **83 suites / 1016 tests / 0 failures**; the Phase 1 and Phase 16 guards pass unchanged; no server change. Roadmap §27EK)

Auth screen continuity (2026-10-23; **not** an application phase; mobile only — **no route, permission, schema, dependency, session-model, environment or server change**) — five behaviours on the screens before the app, each fixing a real moment of friction. **Create Account declared nothing about what its fields hold**, so a password manager could not fill the address it already had or offer to generate a password on the one screen where that matters most; it now declares `email`/`emailAddress`, `new-password`/`newPassword` (not `current-password`) and `name`, and capitalises the name fields. **A field's red message outlived the value it described** on Login, Create Account, Forgot Password and the reset step; editing a field now clears that field's message and nothing else. **Both six-digit screens printed "expires in 10 minutes" as fixed text**, so an expired code was indistinguishable from a wrong one until the server refused it; each now counts down from its own send instant (`verificationCodeIssuedAt`, the reset screen's `sentAt`, both reset by a successful resend) and says *That code has expired. Send a new one to continue.* when the window closes. **The code screens now name the inbox** — the reset screen always (the address is what the previous screen collected), the verify screen whenever this run knows it. **A cold start no longer strands a verified identity**: a `REGISTRATION_REQUIRED` refusal arms the business-name form from in-memory state only, so someone refused who then closed the app arrived at Login unable to reach it without retyping a password Firebase had already accepted; the read-only boot probe now reports `hasResumableFirebaseIdentity`, Login offers **Continue with saved account**, and the tap performs the ordinary exchange. **The rule is intact:** a launch still mints and exchanges nothing, only a tap opens a session, and the action re-checks `emailVerified` at the moment of use instead of trusting a launch-time snapshot; `REGISTRATION_REQUIRED` still arms onboarding. No persisted flag, token, uid or address; no new native or provider boundary; no visual redesign and no limiter, lifetime, attempt-budget or session-rule change. Mobile `tsc`/`eslint` clean, **88 suites / 1063 tests / 0 failures** (14 cases in `auth-flow-continuity.test.tsx`, 7 in `auth-resume-identity.test.tsx`); server baselines unchanged. **Not device-verified**; iOS unverified (no macOS). Roadmap §27EN)

The password rule is stated while it is typed, and six digits verify themselves (2026-10-23; **not** an application phase; mobile only — **no route, permission, schema, dependency, session-model, environment or server change**) — three follow-ups from the auth review above. **The four screens that choose a password now report distance from the server's ONE policy while it is typed** (`passwordRuleHint`): *3 of 8 characters — 5 more to go.*, *12 characters — within the limit.*, *Too long by 3 characters.* — instead of a static "Min 8 characters" that let a 5- or 73-character value reach the server and come back in a developer's words; it deliberately invents **no complexity rule** the server does not have, and its numbers are the constants already pinned to the server's source. **Both six-digit screens verify themselves 400 ms after the sixth digit**, so the common path costs no tap; pressing Verify or Resend **cancels** the pending request, an in-flight guard prevents a second, nothing is scheduled for a partial code (five attempts per challenge) or for a code the screen knows has expired, and a phone whose clock disagrees can still press. **The two helpers every auth screen had copied now have one home** — `mobile/lib/authCopy.ts` holds `fieldErrorsFrom` (nine copies removed) and `formatRemaining` (three), with a source scan that fails if a screen defines its own again; this is the mechanical half of one failure vocabulary, and screens keep the sentences they genuinely own. Mobile `tsc`/`eslint` clean, **89 suites / 1082 tests / 0 failures** (19 new cases); no server file touched, so its baselines stand. **Device-measured 2026-10-23** on the OPPO CPH1969 via `scrcpy` against the local server: the hint counted up as characters were typed, the reset screen named the inbox and counted *Code expires in 8m 48s* from its own send instant, typing `123456` produced **exactly one** `POST /api/auth/password-reset/verify` (400) **with no press** and rendered the refusal, and §27EN's **Continue with saved account** resumed the demo tenant on one tap and disappeared after sign-out. The success half of auto-verify and the verify-email screen's identical path were **not** driven on device in that run (a live code and an unverified identity were needed) and are suite-backed. **The success half was closed on the device, 2026-10-24** (same handset, same local server, a disposable unverified Firebase identity): the app made the reset request itself, so the real route minted the code, and the mailed digits were read back out of the stored keyed digest with the server's own `hashResetCode` and pepper — so what was typed was what was sent. Six digits, **no press**, opened *Set a new password — Your code was verified for auth.probe.…@example.com* — a step reached this way twice in the run (frames at 12:32 and 12:43); the screen named the inbox and counted *Code expires in 6m 27s*; the password step reported *28 characters — within the limit.* as it was typed and put *Confirm password is required* under the empty confirmation without submitting; and the credential that code authorized signed in **on the phone** and rendered the tenant's dashboard. **Two limits recorded rather than papered over in that pass:** the password step's own button was not carried to *Password changed*, and the **verify-email** screen's identical auto-verify was still not reached — both suite-backed. **Both were then closed on the device the same day, in one pass from the app's own screens:** Create Account filled and submitted on the phone created the Firebase identity and asked for the code (`/email-verification/request` **200**); the six mailed digits typed with **no press of Verify** produced `confirm` **200** → `firebase/register` **201** → `firebase/exchange` **200** and the **new tenant's dashboard** (so the registration ordering gate is measured end to end); the same account was signed out and reset from Forgot password — code requested (**202**), digits opening the password step with no press (`verify` **200**), both fields filled (*13 characters — within the limit.*), and **Set new password pressed on the phone** → `password-reset/confirm` **200** → *Password changed*, after which the app refused the **old** password (*Invalid email or password*) and the **new** one signed in to the same tenant (`exchange` **200**) with no credential write by hand. Not proven: a pre-reset session being revoked on the device (the account was signed out first; suite-backed, device-measured 2026-10-22 §27EI), mail arriving in a real inbox (digits came from the stored digests), a production build. The probe tenant and identity were deleted afterwards and re-counted to 0 users / 0 businesses / 0 sessions / 0 audit rows / 0 challenges; the dev build also lost Metro mid-run (`ConnectException … :8081`) and needed a reload, and `adb shell input text` goes to whatever holds focus — both recorded in the evidence, together with the trap that `uiautomator dump` fails silently onto the previous screen's file while a countdown repaints. iOS unverified (no macOS). Still open: a per-user session cap, a new-device sign-in email, invitation-based onboarding. Evidence: `docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-23-auth-polish/`, `docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-24-auth-auto-verify/` and `docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-24-auth-end-to-end/`.Roadmap §27EO)

Over-the-air updates, and a launch without a network that stays signed in (2026-10-24; **not an application phase**; mobile + documentation — **no route, permission, schema, index, session-model or server change**; one SDK-pinned dependency, `expo-updates`) — an installed build can now receive JavaScript **without being rebuilt and reinstalled**, and the same run exposed a session defect that was fixed and shipped **through that channel**. `expo-updates` is SDK-pinned and in Dependabot's ignore list; `runtimeVersion` is `{ policy: "appVersion" }` (the `fingerprint` attempt **failed a real build**, `71d63d16-…`, with the EAS hash `6217b878…` against the laptop's `e32953a7…`, because `app.config.js` resolves Firebase file paths that exist only on the builder); each profile has its own channel, so preview and production cannot cross; `ON_LOAD` + `fallbackToCacheTimeout: 0` keeps every launch offline-capable; `ota-update-config.test.ts` pins all of it (10 cases). **Delivered on the phone:** preview build `ea941bb7-…` on the production API was updated over the air (group `9e3936aa…`, republished as `6e14b317…`, runtime `1.0.0`); the published bundle was grepped before upload — production URL present, `localhost:4000` absent, the new snapshot key present. **The first publish was refused by the device for 19+ minutes** (`CheckCompleteUnavailable`) while `curl` received the same manifest; `eas update:republish` was picked up on the next launch. The cause is not established and is recorded as an observation with its one-command remedy. `eas update` **requires `--platform android`** here — without it the web export fails.

The update carried a real fix: **a cold start with no network threw the session away.** The boot restore treated a failed `api.me` as "not signed in" — the rule AUTH-03 had already removed from `refresh()` — so aeroplane mode landed on Login with a valid stored token. `mobile/lib/auth.tsx` now stores the **server-confirmed profile** beside the token (`inventoryai.auth.userSnapshot`), restores it with status `signedIn` and phase `degraded` on a **non-session** boot failure, and still signs out and deletes it when the server **refuses** the session; a missing or corrupt profile falls back to the old behaviour rather than inventing an account. `auth-offline-boot.test.ts` adds 6 cases; two existing guards now count token writes by key. **Measured after the update, in aeroplane mode:** the cold start showed the signed-in shell (*Demo Tester · Owner*) with the dashboard's offline state instead of Login, and *Try again* returned the production figures (3 / 3 / 1 / 6 / 112) once the network returned — one frame proving delivery and behaviour together. The production demo tenant was seeded for the run and removed (`cleanup:demo-tenant`, 0 leftovers). Also: `seed:demo-tenant` now sets `DEMO_ACCOUNT_PASSWORD` on an **existing** identity too, instead of silently keeping the first run's password. Mobile **91 suites / 1098 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint`/`check-build-env` clean; the APK's own manifest confirms runtime `1.0.0`, channel `preview` and the project's update URL. **Not verified:** a store-signed build, iOS, code signing and the rollback commands. Guide `docs/guides/7-over-the-air-updates.md`; evidence `docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-24-preview-ota/`. Roadmap §27EP)

Preview build against production, channel isolation and the account journeys (2026-10-24; **not an
application phase**; one EAS environment variable added, **no code, route, permission, schema,
dependency or mobile change**) — the installed `preview` build was proved **unable to receive a
production-channel update**: only the `preview` channel exists, `production`/`development` manifest
requests at runtime `1.0.0` answered **404**, and a *visible marker* published to `production`
(newer than the running preview manifest) was ignored across two cold launches (real Login tagline
kept, **zero** logcat references), after which the production channel and branch were deleted and
the 404 returned. **A latent defect was found and fixed on the way:** the EAS **production**
environment had no `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL`, and `mobile/lib/config.ts` falls back to
`http://localhost:4000`, so a production update published with `--environment production` would
have reached no server — the variable was added, and the next production publish produced the same
asset key as the preview one (whose bytes were grepped for the production host). On that build,
against the production API: **Create Account → six-digit verification (no Verify press) → new
tenant dashboard**, **aeroplane-mode cold start stayed signed in** (dashboard offline state, then
*Try again* recovered), and **password reset → Password changed → old password refused → new
password signed in**, with production rows checked (`OWNER`/`ACTIVE`/`emailVerified`, challenge
attempts/`usedAt`/`verifiedAt`) and the probe tenant, sessions, challenges and Firebase identity
removed (**0 leftovers**). Codes came from the stored keyed digests (the probe address has no
inbox); helper scripts deleted. Mobile **91 suites / 1098 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint`
clean; server `test:unit` **1330 / 1276 pass / 0 fail / 54 skipped**, `tsc`/`eslint` clean.
**Not verified:** a store-signed build, iOS, a real inbox in this run, an induced 5xx. Evidence
`docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-24-preview-journeys/`; guide §7; roadmap §27EQ)

Release-build rehearsal on the production profile (2026-10-24; **not an application phase**; mobile
build configuration temporarily changed and restored, **one mobile test case added — no route,
permission, schema, server, session-model or dependency change**) — the **production profile** was
built (`12829e5d…`, production keystore `84JNqqnITp`, `versionName 1.0.0`, `versionCode 1`,
`minSdk 24`/`targetSdk 36`, runtime `1.0.0`, channel `production`) and installed over the preview
APK; `android.buildType` was `apk` **for the run only**, because a Play artifact is an AAB and cannot
be installed with `adb`, and `autoIncrement` was off so the version matched the publishes — **the AAB
path itself was not built**, so Play packaging and Play App Signing remain unproven. On it, against
production: **Create Account → six digits with no Verify press → new tenant**, **aeroplane-mode cold
start stayed signed in** (dashboard offline state; with 20 s for the radio to settle the **first**
*Try again* worked), and **reset → Password changed → old password refused → new password signed
in**. The old "first Try again failed" finding is **explained**: budgets are **15 s warm / 60 s
cold**, and the earlier failure was screenshotted **12 s** after the tap, so it was an instant network
failure (radio not yet attached), not a timeout. **Rollback was measured for the first time:** a
marker published to `production` (`197d0367…`) was applied by the phone, then
`update:roll-back-to-embedded --channel production --platform android --runtime-version 1.0.0`
(`5516d353…`) returned it to the embedded tagline after two cold launches — the CLI **requires
`--runtime-version`** non-interactively. The channel and branch were then deleted (404 restored), the
probe tenant/user/sessions/challenges/5 audit rows/Firebase identity removed (**0 leftovers**), and
the production profile restored (`autoIncrement: true`, no `buildType`). The 5xx gap is closed at the
client rule only: `auth-offline-boot.test.tsx` adds a **500 at boot keeps the session** case; a live
injected 5xx was not performed. Mobile **91 suites / 1099 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint` clean;
`check-build-env --profile production --live` clean (17 controls). **Not verified:** the AAB and Play
signing, iOS, a live 5xx, a real inbox in this run, `update:rollback <group-id>`. Evidence
`docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-24-release-build/`; guide §7; roadmap §27ER)

Production AAB built with store packaging and inspected before any upload (2026-10-24; **not an
application phase**; documentation and artifact verification only — **no code, route, permission,
schema, server, session-model or dependency change, and nothing uploaded**) — the previous entry
proved the production profile's *code path* by packaging it as an **APK**, so the store artifact
itself was still unbuilt. It now exists: EAS `09d77d3d…`, profile `production` with
**`android.buildType` absent** (default packaging) and `autoIncrement` on, an **AAB** of 80,230,212
bytes (SHA-256 `dcbc6323…`), `versionCode` **2**. Read out of the artifact, not the build log: 1,500
entries with the manifest protobuf, `resources.pb`, `classes.dex` and four ABIs; `com.inventoryai.app`,
`versionName 1.0.0`, `minSdk 24`/`targetSdk 36`; a JAR signature from the production keystore;
`EXPO_UPDATE_URL` with `{"expo-channel-name":"production"}`; `expo_runtime_version 1.0.0` with
`runtimeVersion: { policy: "appVersion" }`; `ON_LOAD` + `fallbackToCacheTimeout: 0`; 60 Play upload
debug-symbol files; no dev-menu, dev-launcher, `react-native-web`, `.env` or secret-named entry; and
a Hermes bundle containing `ai-inventory-l1mi.onrender.com` **once**, `localhost:4000` **zero** times
and the offline-boot key `inventoryai.auth.userSnapshot` — the production API **and** the fixed
launch behaviour are in the store artifact.**Twelve `uses-permission` declarations** are recorded with their sources rather than silently
accepted — the dangerous one is `CAMERA`, used by the scanner; the storage pair is capped at
`maxSdkVersion 32`; `DUMP` and the app's own signature permission never reach a user; and
`BIND_JOB_SERVICE` is an `android:permission` requirement on a component rather than a request
(the first version of this record miscounted that, corrected in §27ET). The check is reproducible:
`docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-24-release-aab/verify.py` (standard library only — no Java,
`bundletool` or Android SDK) re-derives every row and its output is stored beside it in
`aab-verification.txt`; the guide now states the AAB-vs-APK distinction, the `versionCode` behaviour
and the command. The artifact was **later validated with Google's own tooling** — Android Studio's
bundled JetBrains Runtime (OpenJDK 25) runs `bundletool` 1.18.3, and both `validate` and
`dump manifest` were run on it without any keystore (see §27ET). **Not verified:** the AAB has
**never been installed** (an AAB cannot be, and a locally re-signed rebuild would break the installed
app's signature lineage), so behaviour still rests on the production-profile APK built from
the same source, keystore, channel and environment; **nothing has been uploaded**, so Play App Signing,
the upload-key handover and Play's own review have not run; signing is verified as *present*, not
cryptographically; iOS remains unverified (no macOS). Evidence
`docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-24-release-aab/`; guide §7; roadmap §27ES)

Android permission review and trim (2026-10-24; **not an application phase**; mobile config plus one
test suite — **no route, permission grant, schema, server, session-model or dependency change, and
no upload**) — every declaration in the store artifact was attributed and one was removed. The
correction first: `BIND_JOB_SERVICE` had been reported as *requested* and `READ_GSERVICES` missed,
because permission-shaped strings were matched instead of declaration elements; read properly, the 12
declarations are `CAMERA` (expo-camera; `app/(app)/scan.tsx` renders `CameraView`), `INTERNET` and
`VIBRATE` (Expo's manifest template), `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` and `WAKE_LOCK` (Expo Updates / Firebase),
`USE_BIOMETRIC` + `USE_FINGERPRINT` (`androidx.biometric`, a dependency of `expo-secure-store`),
`READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (template + `expo-file-system`, **capped at `maxSdkVersion 32`**),
`DUMP` (`androidx.profileinstaller`), `READ_GSERVICES` (Google Play services) and the app's own
`DYNAMIC_RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED_PERMISSION`; `BIND_JOB_SERVICE` and GMS's `REVOCATION_NOTIFICATION`
are only `android:permission` attributes requiring callers to hold them. **`CAMERA` is the only
dangerous permission and it is genuinely used.** Removed: **`SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`**, which no
dependency and no `app.json` entry asks for — it is one of the "optional permissions" in Expo's
generated main-manifest template, inherited by every prebuild, and nothing here draws an overlay;
`mobile/app.json` now sets `android.blockedPermissions: ["android.permission.SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW"]`
(applied as `tools:node="remove"`, no dependency, plugin or native code added), pinned by
`mobile/__tests__/android-permissions.test.ts` (4 cases: the list is exactly that one entry, nothing
used is in it, `expo-camera`/`CameraView` still exist, and the camera permission sentence survives).
**Verified on the rebuilt artifact:** EAS `4b133d62…`, profile `production`, default packaging,
`versionCode` **3**, 80,230,207 bytes, SHA-256 `76740691…` — `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW` absent, declarations
**12 → 11**, storage caps now printed per permission by `verify.py`, and the **JavaScript byte-identical
to §27ES's artifact** (`index.android.bundle` 2,847,364 bytes, SHA-256 `1156e960…` in both), so only
the manifest and embedded config moved. **Google's own tooling validated it too:** Android Studio's
bundled JetBrains Runtime (OpenJDK **25.0.3**) runs **bundletool 1.18.3**, so `bundletool validate`
(exit **0**) and `bundletool dump manifest --module=base` were run on both artifacts — the dump prints
`versionCode 3`, `minSdk 24`, `targetSdk 36`, `compileSdk 37` and exactly the 11 permissions with
`maxSdkVersion="32"` on the storage pair, while the previous build's dump lists 12 and includes
`SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`. Neither command signs, so **no keystore or password was read, printed or
committed**. Mobile **92 suites / 1,103 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint`
clean, `check-build-env --profile production` clean (17 controls). **Not verified:** a build carrying
this manifest was never installed — the device runs rest on the byte-identical earlier build, so the
permission change itself and `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW` were never exercised either way — and nothing is
uploaded, so Play App Signing, the upload-key handover and Play's review remain unexercised; signing
is present, not cryptographically checked; iOS unverified (no macOS). Evidence
`docs/evidence/device-runs/2026-10-24-permission-trim/`; roadmap §27ET)

AI-28 — The Assistant's Model Is Checked, Not Assumed (2026-10-21; **not** an application phase; server only — no route, permission, schema, dependency, session or mobile change, and **no prompt change**, so a turn's token cost is untouched by construction) — on 2026-10-21 every assistant turn answered `502 AI_UNAVAILABLE` while the log said only `upstream HTTP 404` and `model=unknown`, so neither the answer nor the log could name the model at fault. **Why the previous model stopped working, measured:** the id was never withdrawn — `GET /v1/models` still lists `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b:free`, but `GET /v1/models/{id}/endpoints` reports its single Nvidia endpoint at `status: -5` with `uptime_last_5m: 0`, `uptime_last_30m: 0`, `uptime_last_1d: 60`, while the replacement `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning:free` reports `status: 0` and `100 / 100 / 99.93`; a correct id pointing at an unhealthy upstream is the whole failure, and **nothing on our side is misconfigured** — it is a provider outage, not a bad id, and it clears only when Nvidia's endpoint returns. A **second, independent** cause of "free models are occasionally unavailable" is the account's own `429 free-models-per-day` cap — **50 requests per day, shared by every free model**, measured at `X-RateLimit-Remaining: 0` after a day's turns, which takes **all** free models down together until 00:00 UTC. Shipped: `server/src/services/ai/modelCheck.ts` with two questions kept apart — `checkModelCatalogue` (is the configured id one the gateway lists; sends **no completion**, so it spends **no quota**) and `probeModel` (one tool-shaped request; the check that would have caught this incident, because the gateway still **listed** the broken model) — neither retrying, none mutating a model, none throwing, and **429/402 reported as `quota`, never `unusable`**; `npm run ai:check` with exit **0** VERIFIED / **1** WRONG / **2** NOT VERIFIED (so a pipeline cannot read "could not check" as "checked and fine"), `--probe` adding the one-request proof; a **boot warning** fired unawaited that logs **only** a `missing` model and names it (silent on `unreachable`, because a line that fires when the check could not run trains an operator to ignore it), never able to stop the server, and doing nothing under `NODE_ENV=test`; and `AIProvider.model` (a **getter**, so it reads the environment at call time) seeded into `runAiChat`, so a turn whose every call failed now reports **its model instead of `unknown`**. Verified: **6 of 6 live turns grounded and numerically exact** on `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning:free` (`perf:turns`, 200-product seeded tenant — `200 / 32 / 8`, reorder `32`, a SKU lookup, `8 ÷ 200`, `49,217` units, a per-location table; tools `getReorderNeededProducts`, `getProduct`, `getInventorySummary`, `getLocationBreakdown`; 5.3–15.7 s, no `degraded` mark), and once the allowance ran out the same route answered **429 `AI_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` in 704 ms** with nothing persisted and the turn line naming the model — the fix observed on the failing path it exists for, with mobile's `AI_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` copy still naming the **08:00 Manila** reset. `npm run ai:check` → `ok` exit **0**; `--probe` → `quota (HTTP 429)` exit **2** NOT VERIFIED. Server **1048 tests / 997 pass / 0 fail / 51 skipped** (`AI28-ai-model-config` added, 16 cases; `AI5` strengthened), `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean, mobile assistant-copy suites **57/57**. **Not re-observed live: the model-down 404 itself** (with the allowance spent the gateway answers 429 before it looks at the model) **and a successful in-app turn** (the 6/6 run spent the last free request; the app's success render is covered by §27BG). Roadmap §27DZ)

AI-29 — A Verified, Bounded Fallback Chain (2026-10-21; **not** an application phase; server only — no route, permission, schema, dependency, session or mobile change, and no prompt change) — AI-28 proved that a dead model could be *seen*; this makes it *survivable*. **`AI_FALLBACK_MODELS_OPENROUTER`** is a comma-separated, ordered, deduplicated, **capped-at-5** candidate list (empty = no failover, the old behaviour); the configured **primary is skipped**, so changing it in one variable cannot leave a duplicate. On a **model-specific** failure (`MODEL_FAILOVER_STATUSES` = **404/410/500/502/503/504/529**, carried as `AiProviderError.status`) `ai.service` walks the candidates **once each, in order**, and the model that answered is **sticky for the rest of the turn** — so a tool round does not re-pay the dead model, and the walk happens **at most once per turn**, which is what makes "no infinite retry loop" structural. **Everything else stops the walk, deliberately:** **402/429 never reach it** (they are `AiQuotaExceeded`, AI-6, because the daily free allowance is per ACCOUNT and shared by every free model — measured again with five candidates configured and the allowance spent: **one** provider call, `failovers=0`), **400/401/403** are our request or key, and a **transport failure carries no status** so another model would travel the same broken network. A candidate is attempted only with ≥ **5 000 ms** left of the turn's single deadline (AI-1). **Tool calling is required and checked for free:** `modelCapabilities.ts` reads the provider catalogue at boot (**no completion, no quota**), caches declared `tools` support, and the loop **skips** a candidate that declares it cannot — before a request is spent — while an **unread** catalogue is `unknown`, never `false` (a free check that did not run must not become an outage of its own); a boot line names any configured candidate that will be skipped. Observation without secrets: one `[ai] failover attempt=N model=<from> -> <to> reason=HTTP <status> remaining=<ms>ms` line per attempt, skipped candidates named once, and **`failovers=<n>`** added to the AI-5 turn line. **The list is operator-owned configuration, not code:** `npm run ai:models` prints the primary and chain exactly as the turn loop reads them (marking skipped candidates and why) plus the free ids that declare tool support, and `--check=<id>` proves one id answers a tool-shaped request for **one request**; reordering or adding a model is editing `AI_FALLBACK_MODELS_OPENROUTER` and restarting (the server reads `.env` at boot), documented in `docs/guides/6-scripts-and-commands.md` under *Changing the AI models yourself*. `npm run ai:check` now lists the chain with declared tool support and, with `--probe`, sends one tool-shaped request to the primary **and each candidate** (6 for a 5-model chain), exiting **1** if any cannot call tools or answer. **Configured order, measured 2026-10-21** (all five declare `tools`): lightning (status 0, 100 %) · ultra-550b (0, 100 %) · super-vl (**-2, 96.7 %**, degraded) · `liquid/lfm-2.5-2.6b:free` (**last**: 100 % uptime but a 2.6 B model, so it is reached only after three better candidates) · super-120b (**last**, still down at **-5, 0 %**). Tools are read-only, so a failover cannot duplicate an inventory change; business isolation, permissions and the prompt are untouched. **26 new cases** in `AI29-model-failover.test.mjs`; server **1073 tests / 1021 pass / 0 fail / 52 skipped**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean. **A real failover was not observed live today** — the allowance was spent by the §27DZ measurement, so every candidate answers 429 before the model is consulted, which is exactly the case that must not fail over (and did not: 429 in 545 ms, `calls=1`); the walk is proven by the DB-gated wire test and unit cases, and `npm run ai:check -- --probe` is owed once the allowance resets at **08:00 Manila**. Roadmap §27EA)

AI-30 — A Model That Just Failed Five Times Is Not Tried First on the Next Turn (2026-10-21; **not** an application phase; server only — no route, permission, schema, dependency, session or mobile change, and no prompt change) — AI-29 made a dead model survivable, but the configured primary was still tried **first on every new turn**, so a model the deployment already knew was broken cost one failed request (and a share of the free tier's shared allowance) per turn until an operator edited `.env`. `server/src/services/ai/modelBreaker.ts` is a process-local, in-memory breaker per model id: fewer than **5** consecutive model-specific failures = closed; the **5th** opens a **30-second** cooldown; when it expires the model is **half-open** — a failed trial **re-opens it immediately** (the count is not reset at expiry, so a permanently dead model costs **one request per 30 s, not one per turn**) and a single 2xx clears the streak and the cooldown, returning the model to normal selection by itself. The **primary is skipped like any other model** — the turn starts directly on the first eligible candidate and logs `[ai] model cooling down, skipped: <id> (<n>s left)` — while configuration is untouched, so the primary is first in the chain again the moment its cooldown ends; the opening moment is its own line (`model cooling down for 30s after 5 consecutive failure(s): <id>`). If **every** configured model is cooling the turn **fails fast with zero provider calls** and the existing 502 `AI_UNAVAILABLE` sentence. **Only `MODEL_FAILOVER_STATUSES` (404/410/500/502/503/504/529) is recorded** — quota 429/402 (the shared account allowance), auth/bad request 400/401/403 and a configuration error are not evidence about a model and can never make a healthy model's next turn skip it. *(AI-31 later makes a CLASSIFIED transport failure count; see below.)* **18 new cases** in `AI30-model-cooldown.test.mjs` (threshold at five, self-expiring window, failed half-open trial re-opens, success resets, per-model isolation, primary skipped with config untouched, cooling candidate skipped in the walk, all-cooling zero calls, and every non-model failure left uncounted), plus `AI29-model-failover.test.mjs` passing in full with a breaker reset between cases because the state is process-wide by design; server **1093 tests / 1041 pass / 0 fail / 52 skipped**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean (467 modules). **Not device-verified for the cooldown itself** — it is proven by the fake-provider cases, while the failover it builds on was measured live on the phone in the same session (§27EA); the breaker is **per process and not persisted**, so a restart forgets every cooldown (deliberately: a restart should re-test the whole chain) and two instances keep independent breakers. Roadmap §27EB)

AI-31 — The Model That Answers Becomes the Model the Deployment Uses (2026-10-21; **not** an application phase; server only — no route, permission, schema, dependency, session or mobile change, and no prompt change) — AI-29 rescued one turn and AI-30 stopped a dead primary being tried first, but the fallback was still only a fallback: the next request started on the configured primary again, so a dead primary cost one refusal per cooldown window until an operator edited `.env`. **`server/src/services/ai/modelSelection.ts`** now holds a process-local **promoted** model (`null` = the configured default is active); a turn's chain becomes `[active, ...configured order without it]`. **Promotion is threshold-gated, not eager:** a fallback that answers one transient failure still serves that turn but does not seize the deployment; `maybePromote` requires the active model to be **cooling** (five consecutive availability failures, `AI_MODEL_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` default **5**), and then the model that answered becomes the active one and every later turn starts there (`[ai] promoted model: <old> -> <responder>`).
```
