A promoted model that later fails five times is replaced the same way, by whichever configured model answers next. **Configuration is never overwritten:** `AI_MODEL_OPENROUTER` stays the operator's default, `npm run ai:models` prints it, and a restart deliberately returns to it. **Concurrency is a compare-and-set** (`promoteModel` accepts a candidate only while the caller's `expectedActive` still matches), so two requests cannot overwrite one another; counters are updated synchronously, so concurrent turns neither lose an increment nor reset one another. **Transport failures now count toward the breaker, and still never fail over:** `AiProviderError` carries a declared `kind` (`network`, `timeout`, `invalid_response`) set only in `providers/http.ts`; five consecutive ones cool the active model (the assistant cannot serve, and paying for the same outage every turn is the cost the breaker exists to stop), while the turn ends instead of walking the chain because another model would travel the same broken path. A 400/401/403, a quota refusal (402/429), a configuration error and an `AiProviderError` with neither a status nor a kind still count for **nothing**. **19 cases** in `AI31-model-promotion.test.mjs` (healthy active stays; 1–4 failures do not promote; the fifth does; the next request starts on the promoted model; a promoted model failing five times is replaced; a cooled model rejoins; environment order; configured threshold/cooldown; all cooling fails fast with zero calls; quota/auth/config never count; a tool is not executed twice; concurrent counters, one promotion, a stale promotion refused; five network failures then the first answer promotes). `AI29` and `AI30` pass in full, with AI-30's transport case split into “classified counts, never fails over” and “unclassified counts for nothing”. Server **`test:unit` 1115 tests / 1063 pass / 0 fail / 52 skipped**, **`test:db` 852 tests / 764 pass / 0 fail / 88 skipped**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean (469 modules). **Verified LIVE on the real provider (2026-10-21)**, not only on the fake: a one-off script (not committed) set the configured primary to the valid-but-dead `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b:free` (`ai:models -- --check=…` → `unusable (HTTP 404)`) with `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning:free` as the candidate, and six real turns gave 404 → failover → `OK` on turns 1–4 (`failures=1..4`), the fifth opening `model cooling down for 30s` and logging `promoted model: …super-120b… -> …lightning…`, and turn 6 starting on lightning with `calls=1 failovers=0`. A bogus id tried first answered **HTTP 400** and was correctly **not** counted or failed over. The day's allowance read `44 of 50` before and after, so the check did not exhaust it. **State of the owner's requested order when measured** (`/v1/models/:author/:slug/endpoints`, no completions): super-vl **-2** (100 % / 91.7 %), liquid 2.6B **-5** (100 % / 72.8 %), lightning **0** (100 % / 100 %), ultra-550b **0** (100 % / 100 %), super-120b **-5** (0 % / 3.75 %, 404 on a completion probe) — the first two candidates are currently the weakest, which is a reason to reorder the variable, not to edit code. **Reordered 2026-10-21 on that measurement, configuration only:** primary `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning:free` (100 % uptime; the only model with 6/6 grounded turns on a real tenant), then `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b:free`, `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-super-vl:free`, `liquid/lfm-2.5-2.6b:free`, and `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b:free` last; `server/.env`, `server/.env.example` and the operator guide carry the same order, and no code changed. **The in-app half was not re-run** at that point — the response shape and prompt are unchanged and the turn path was measured in §27EA — so that was live server verification, not a phone run. **Production/phone verification followed on 2026-10-21:** commit `7f3e6a1` was auto-deployed, the production environment was set to the lightning-first order (and 5 / 30000 explicitly), and the OPPO CPH1969 against `https://ai-inventory-l1mi.onrender.com` answered **112 units**, the three low/out products and **one out-of-stock product** from a seeded six-product demo tenant; promotion was exercised live by temporarily making the dead `super-120b` primary — every failed primary attempt was followed by a successful `lightning` answer, the fifth consecutive failure opened the 30 s cooldown and promoted `lightning`, and the next turn used `calls=1 failovers=0` — before the lightning-first configuration was restored and redeployed (final turn `model=nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning:free`). A Render **restart does not apply a changed environment variable; the next deploy does** (measured), which the guide now states. The demo tenant was removed (leftovers 0) and the Firebase identity retained; 11 requests were spent (42 → 31 of 50). Roadmap §27EC)

AI-32 / AI-33 — Live endpoint health, and a measuring instrument that cannot lie (2026-10-21; **not application phases**; server tooling/tests only — no route, permission, schema, dependency, session, mobile or prompt change) — `npm run ai:models` now reads the provider's own `GET /v1/models/{author}/{slug}/endpoints` for the configured primary and chain and prints each endpoint's raw `status` plus 5-minute / 30-minute / 1-day uptime. It is a GET, not a completion, so it spends no request quota; a 404 is `not_found`, an empty list is `no_endpoints`, and any other refusal or transport error is `unreachable` — never a verdict that the model is unhealthy, which remains `probeModel`'s job. **10 cases** in `AI32-model-health.test.mjs`. The turn probe's fact check was also wrong in both directions (`none are out of stock` was reported `MISSING 0`; an expected `2` matched `1281`), so `scripts/perf/factCheck.mjs` now compares numbers whole and accepts the words for zero; **9 cases** in `AI33-turn-fact-check.test.mjs`. **Ultra and Lightning were measured on the same 10k seeded tenant, same five questions:** both **5/5 grounded** after the fix — dashboard **6 products / 1,281 units / 2 low / 0 out**, `SEEDSKU-0` **64 units**, locations **433 / 424 / 424**; Ultra 2,504–8,142 ms, Lightning 2,962–14,528 ms. The order did **not** change: Lightning remains primary on the larger evidence base (6/6 earlier + 5/5 here), Ultra remains first fallback with its own 5/5. The comparison spent 21 of 31 available requests, 10 left. Scale: 10k/50k seeded tenants measured through `executeTool` with **zero model calls** — 50k (100,143 inventory rows) heavy-tool medians 733–1,019 ms, dashboard HTTP median 1,042 ms, no tool payload over the 12,000-character cap; 64 indexes / 16 collections / 0 database disagreements; `test:db` 854 / 764 pass / 0 fail / 90 skipped; 50k tenant removed, 0 leftovers at that point. *(Annotated 2026-10-21: a capacity audit found a **50,626-product** tenant present again in the dev database — a later seed, not a failed cleanup — with the database at **224.1 MB of the M0 tier's 512 MB**. Measured footprint is **~1.6 KB per product with two stock rows** before history, so a 1,000,000-product tenant needs **~1.6 GB** and is a paid-tier provisioning task, not something this cluster can validate; the current cluster fits roughly **180,000 more products**. The next large seed must clean up first. Roadmap §27EF capacity addendum.)* The four open Dependabot alerts are mobile transitive: `decode-uri-component@0.2.2` (runtime via `expo-router` → CJS `query-string@7`, its patched `0.5.0` is ESM-only, so no safe override), and `node-forge` / `braces` / `sprintf-js` (build/dev only, no patched release). No dependency was changed; the alerts remain open and visible. Roadmap §27ED, §27EE, §27EF)

AI-34 — A dead model is found by a timer, not by a member's failed question (2026-10-21; **not an application phase**; server only — no route, permission, schema, dependency, session, mobile or prompt change) — every earlier reliability increment was **pulled**: AI-28/29/30/31 keep a turn alive and AI-32 prints health when an operator asks, but the deployment learned its model was dead on 2026-10-21 from a member's `502 AI_UNAVAILABLE`. `server/src/services/ai/healthWatch.ts` now runs the quota-free `GET /v1/models/{author}/{slug}/endpoints` read for the configured primary and every fallback — **no completion, no request from the free allowance** — and classifies each as **ok** (a current status-0 endpoint, best 5m ≥ 90% and 30m ≥ 95%), **degraded** (an endpoint down while another is up, an uptime window below threshold, or **no status-0 endpoint but recent uptime still measurable**), **unavailable** (nothing published, or no status-0 and no measurable 5m uptime), or **unknown** (the record could not be read — never called an outage; two consecutive unreadable checks alert that the check cannot run). The first draft called every non-zero status unavailable; the **live `npm run ai:health` run corrected it**, because the provider published **status -2 with 100% 5m uptime** for a model that was still serving. Alerts are **transition-only** (new problem, worsening/improvement, one recovery, silence while unchanged), and an unreadable check never clears a known outage; the state is **process-local**, so a restart re-alerts once, like the AI-30 breaker, with no new collection. `sendOperationalAlertEmail` uses the existing provider-agnostic mail boundary to `AI_HEALTH_ALERT_EMAIL`; with no address/mail the same report is logged at error level. It names model ids, statuses and uptime only — **no key, base URL, tenant or user**. `startServer` starts it in production (only with an OpenRouter model configured), first check **60 s**, then every **30 min**, timers **unref'd**, stopped before the shutdown drain; `npm run ai:health` runs the same check once and exits **0** healthy / **1** unavailable-or-degraded / **2** could not check, `-- --alert` sends too. **Live 2026-10-21, five GETs, zero completions:** lightning / ultra / super-vl **ok**, `liquid` **degraded** (status -2, 5m 100%, 30m 76.7%), `super-120b` **unavailable** (status -5, 5m 0%), exit **1**. **29 cases** in `AI34-ai-health-watchdog.test.mjs`; server `test:unit` **1168 / 1116 pass / 0 fail / 52 skipped** (re-run after the two template cases were added; the earlier 1166 / 1114 reading predates them), `test:db` **854 / 764 pass / 0 fail / 90 skipped**, typecheck, lint and both module-graph checks clean (476 modules). Limits: process-local state, a sleeping free host does not check (the CLI is the external-scheduler answer), and delivery was proven with the in-process fake provider, **not** a real mailbox — the recipient must be set on the host and the first real alert observed. Roadmap §27EG)

AI-35 / AI-36 / AI-37 / AI-38 / AI-39 — The model the provider says is down is not tried, the allowance is visible, a promotion ends by itself, and the deployment states its own configuration (2026-10-23; **not application phases**; server + documentation, no route, permission, schema, index, dependency, session or mobile change, and no prompt change) — five increments on top of AI-29/30/31's failover, all built from **free** provider data (endpoint-health GETs and response headers), never a paid service or a completion bought to check a model. **AI-35** — `server/src/services/ai/modelHealthGate.ts` holds a model the provider's own `GET /v1/models/{author}/{slug}/endpoints` reports **unavailable** out of selection, before a request is spent on it; the read runs at boot (fired unawaited by `startServer`, silent on failure) and on every watchdog cycle. Only `unavailable` marks: `degraded` is still serving and `unknown` is a check that could not run. A mark lasts until the next reading (`AI_MODEL_HEALTH_TTL_MS`, default **31 min** = one interval + grace), an answer clears it immediately, and a reading that would remove **every** model releases the configured primary — so a wrong health reading can cost requests, never refuse service outright. **AI-36** — `quota.ts` parses `x-ratelimit-remaining` / `x-ratelimit-limit` (plus the unprefixed forms) from every response, reports `quota=<remaining>/<limit>` (or `quota=unknown`) on the AI-5 turn line, and turns a refusal that reports **zero** remaining into a bounded fail-fast (`AI_QUOTA_BLOCK_MS`, default **30 s**) that answers 429 with **0 provider calls**; a 429 without that reading changes nothing, and any success lifts the block. `postJson` now returns `{ data, quota }` and `AiQuotaExceeded` carries the reading. **AI-37** — the watchdog ends a promotion after **two consecutive** healthy readings of the configured model (one is a sample, two is a recovery), logging `returning to it: <id>` once and clearing the health mark at the same moment; the promotion stays process-local and `.env` is never rewritten. **AI-38** — optional `AI_ATTEMPT_MAX_MS` (**0 = off**, the default) bounds one attempt while another candidate remains, so a model that hangs rather than refuses cannot consume the turn; the **last** attempt always keeps everything left, and a capped expiry is model-specific (fails over, counts toward the breaker) while a transport failure still does **not** fail over. **AI-39** — a fallback list longer than the cap now names the ids it ignored instead of truncating silently, the effective chain is logged once at boot, and the watchdog's run line carries `active=… (configured=…) cooling=… quota=…` so a promotion and the allowance are visible without grepping. **Context handling was verified and deliberately NOT changed**: `npm run ai:models` measured every configured model at **≥66k** context (primary 1000k) against a worst realistic prompt of **~40–58k tokens**, so a per-model trim would be machinery without a present fault. **47 new cases** across `AI35`–`AI39`; server `test:unit` **1316 / 1263 pass / 0 fail / 53 skipped**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean (494 modules). Device verification and residual limits are in the roadmap entry. Roadmap §27EL)

AI-40 — The answer arrives while it is being written (2026-10-23; **not an application phase**; server + mobile + documentation — **no new route, permission, schema, index, dependency, session or environment change, and no prompt change**) — the assistant's measured latency is 5–19 s per turn, and the screen used to show a spinner for all of it. **`POST /api/ai/chat` is negotiated on `Accept`**: `text/event-stream` gets the streamed form, anything else gets **the exact JSON response it always got**, which is why the route table, the `ai.use` gate, the rate limiter, the Phase 23 classification and the route inventory needed no change. `prepareTurn` was extracted so validation, conversation ownership (always `businessId` + `userId` from the session), the AI-24 asked-at stamp and the AI-1 abort listener happen once **before any header**, so a bad request is still the ordinary JSON `400` on both paths. The wire contract is three events — `delta { text }`, `done { conversation, userMessage, assistantMessage, degraded }`, `error { status, code, message }` mapped by the same `mapAiFailure` — and **`done` is the authority**: the server rewrites status constants and trims a cut-off unit before storing, so the client replaces what it rendered. Persistence is unchanged and still happens only after the provider answers (a failed turn writes nothing), a `: ping` goes out every 15 s, and the response carries `no-cache, no-transform` + `X-Accel-Buffering: no` because a buffering proxy defeats the feature while still answering 200. **Provider layer:** `providers/http.ts` reads the upstream SSE and `providers/anthropicWire.ts` assembles text deltas and tool-call JSON fragments into the **same** response shape, so the turn loop, tools, breaker and failover did not change; a `2xx` with no event is `invalid_response` (never an empty answer), a gateway that ignores `stream: true` gets **one** ordinary fallback request but **never** after text has been delivered, the empty-completion retry is untouched, and `ai.service` counts **delivered characters** and stops the failover walk once any exist — text on screen cannot be taken back, so a second model would show the answer twice. **Mobile:** new `mobile/lib/sse.ts` (incremental framing, one space stripped after `data:`, comments skipped, **one stateful `TextDecoder`** so a character split across chunks survives, and CRLF normalised **on the buffer** — normalising per chunk left a split `\r`/`\n` unmatched and delivered the answer as one payload, which the split-CRLF case now pins); `aiApi.chatStream` cannot use `apiFetch`, so it takes its token from the newly exported **`resolveRequestToken`** — the streamed request is the one that would otherwise let a nearly-expired token 401 and sign the member out — keeps `AI_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, keeps the caller's abort apart from its own (`REQUEST_CANCELLED` vs `NETWORK_TIMEOUT`), parses a pre-stream refusal into the same `ApiError`, and imports `expo/fetch` lazily. `AiChat` renders the arriving text, keeps the thinking row only until the first character, replaces it with the `done` payload, and falls back to the ordinary route **only** when no text was shown and the failure is a transport one — a quota refusal, an ended session, a stop and a partly-read answer are never retried. **Two defects were found before release.** The upstream transport normalised CRLF **per chunk**, so a `\r\n` split across writes delivered **no events** until EOF (fixed on the buffer; the `sse-crlf` case reports 1 event instead of 7 if reverted). And the phone showed that a stream going **silent without closing** left `reader.read()` parked, so even the 75 s budget could not settle the call — the spinner stayed for minutes; `readSseStream` now has a **20 s idle window** plus an abort that settles the read even when the body ignores `cancel()`, `chatStream` races its own call as a last defence, and `fetchImpl` is injectable for the test that pins it. The same review put the JSON path back onto `prepareTurn` (it had been duplicated). **18 server cases** in `AI40-ai-streaming.test.mjs` (MIXED), **25 mobile cases** (`ai40-sse-parser` 10, `ai40-assistant-streaming` 9, `ai40-chat-stream-transport` 6); server `test:unit` **1330 / 1276 pass / 0 fail / 54 skipped**, `test:db` **879 / 786 pass / 0 fail / 93 skipped**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean (499 files), secret guard clean; mobile **86 suites / 1042 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc` and `eslint` clean. **Device-verified 2026-10-23** on the OPPO CPH1969 against the local server over `adb reverse` on the demo fixture: one `POST /api/ai/chat` **200 in 13 088.9 ms** (`calls=2 toolRuns=1 tools=getLowStockProducts failovers=0 model=nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning:free`), the answer naming exactly the fixture's restock list, the spinner+**Stop** before any text and the finished answer after it — and a single POST rather than two is what proves the streamed path served the turn, since the client's fallback would have been a second request. Six fault-injected scenarios were measured on the same fixture: cut **before** text (two POSTs 281 ms apart, the JSON retry answering `201` in 4 238 ms), cut **after** text (partial `Three` kept, error banner, one POST, server `AI_ABANDONED`, nothing persisted), **Stop** (*You stopped waiting…*), a **429** (the exact quota sentence, one POST, no provider request) and a **silent stall** (gave up at 20.5 s, retry answered in 9 431 ms). **AI-35 was observed live in the same run** (two models held out before a request was spent, matching `npm run ai:health`); **AI-37's two-check return is proven at the decision level**, not live, because it needs a promotion plus two 30-minute watchdog cycles. **Production, authenticated (2026-10-23)** — measured separately from the local server: the demo tenant seeded into `inventoryai_prod_db` by naming the database, a real Firebase sign-in exchanged at `POST /api/auth/firebase/exchange` (200), and one streamed turn through Render that answered **200 `text/event-stream`** and delivered its body as **36 network chunks carrying 98 `delta` events over 1 044 ms** before one `done` — so the Cloudflare edge in front of Render does not buffer it — with the fixture's answer (112 units, the three restock products, primary model, two tools), stored **once** (1 conversation, 2 messages). On the same deployment an unauthenticated streamed request answered **401** JSON with no SSE header, an empty message answered **400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`** as the ordinary envelope (proving `prepareTurn` precedes the headers), and a client abort at 2 180 ms wrote nothing; counters afterwards **77 responses / 0 5xx / 0 server errors**, the tenant torn down with **0 leftovers** and the database back to 3 users / 3 businesses / 1 product / 0 conversations. **Render's own log stream was not readable from this host**, so that review used the counters, the rows and the request telemetry — recorded as a narrower instrument, not as a log review. It was then **repeated on the phone** the same evening: a `development` client's JS comes from Metro, so `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=https://ai-inventory-l1mi.onrender.com` (bundle verified to contain it, `adb reverse tcp:4000` removed) pointed the installed build at production without a new APK; signed in as the seeded demo OWNER (dashboard 3/3/1/6 and **112** on hand, plus a production session row), four turns answered correctly (112 units, movement history, low stock with 6 and 5 unit shortfalls), one captured at ~0.74 s intervals — **12.9 s** partial answer with Stop still up, **15.2 s** a fourth bullet trailing at `on 2026-`, then the complete answer — and stored **once** (3 conversations, 2 / 4 / 2 messages, primary model, no duplicates, 0 5xx). `X-Accel-Buffering` was not visible to the client through Cloudflare — a proxy hint, with delivery measured instead. **Not proven on hardware: a dropped stream** (fallback rules are suite-proven), and **iOS remains unverified**. **A teardown defect was found while deleting the production demo tenant and fixed:** `clearTenantData` removed every tenant collection except the assistant's, so the command printed `leftovers: 0` while **3 conversations and 8 messages** stayed in `ai_conversations` / `ai_messages` — a message has no `businessId` (only its conversation does), so it cannot join the collection list. `scripts/perf/lib.mjs` now resolves the conversations, deletes their messages, then the conversations, and `countTenantLeftovers` uses the same two steps; re-seeding the demo tenant therefore resets its AI history too. The same audit found two more gaps and closed them: `audit_logs` **does** carry `businessId` and was missing from the list (now in `TENANT_DATA_COLLECTIONS`, so a reset and a removal both take a fixture tenant's own governance trail), and `sessions` / `password_reset_tokens` / `password_reset_challenges` carry only `userId` — `removeTenant` now resolves the tenant's user ids **before** deleting the users and removes those three by owner, because deleting the users first would strand them with nothing left to tie them to a tenant. The demo cleanup's closing line was corrected to say what it actually verified: every collection carrying the businessId, plus assistant messages through their conversation — a `userId`-only row cannot be found by that predicate. `email_verification_challenges` stay: no user or tenant id, and their own TTL expires them. **5 DB cases** in `DEMO2-tenant-ai-teardown.test.mjs` (AI history, audit trail, a whole tenant's session and reset rows, the empty-tenant no-op, and a positive control). Artifacts: `docs/evidence/ai-streaming-2026-10-23/`. Roadmap §27EM)

Expo SDK 58 device acceptance (2026-10-21; **not an application phase**; documentation only — no code, dependency, route, permission, schema, session or environment change) — the SDK 58 upgrade's one open sentence (*"Not verified yet: the Android build and the on-device pass"*) is answered on hardware: EAS build `b1fecde3-fa6f-4235-829d-5bc35a72ea58` (`development`, Android, built from commit `93f9130`) installed on the owner's **OPPO CPH1969, Android 11 (API 30)**, against the local server over `adb reverse`. **Every screen** opened and **every write path** executed — Stock In `201 / 627 ms` (`0 → 5`, history `+5`, audit row), Transfer `201 / 1,084 ms` (both legs: `416 → 413`, `0 → 3`), Stock Out `200 / 576 ms` (`5 → 3`), Adjust `200 / 856 ms` (`3 → 4`) — and the **AI assistant answered on the phone**: *12,540,196 units across 50,626 active products*, `getInventorySummary`, with a **live failover** in the same turn (primary `…super-120b…` **HTTP 404** → `…ultra-550b…` answered, `failovers=1`), the first time AI-29/31 were observed on a device rather than a fake provider. **Label print is now a decoded artifact:** *Save as PDF* produced a 9,451-byte file whose bars decode to the product's EAN-13 `2000053337540` at exactly 95 modules × 4.0 pt, so the printed geometry is complete (the F-1/ISS-13 question) **without paper**; share sheet and the scanner (a real barcode in the room decoded, answering *Product not found* for an unregistered code) both work. The decode is now a **permanent CI check** — `scripts/verify-label-barcode.mjs` (`--html` decodes the generator *and* checks its geometry against the page at module 4 and 8; `--pdf` decodes two real device fixtures) — and building it found **two real geometry defects, both fixed**: the printed SVG had an 11-module quiet zone on the left and **none on the right** (the on-screen preview always padded both), and `barcodeLabelHtml` honoured `moduleWidth` while keeping the page at 595 pt, so module 6 produced **702 pt of label on a 595 pt page** — the F-1 clipping failure one argument away. The page now grows to the label; the fixed label was re-printed from the phone and its clip box measures **468 pt = 117 modules** (pre-fix 424 pt = 106), still decoding at 95 modules × 4.0 pt. **Registered barcode scan verified on the device** — the `2000053337540` label scanned from the laptop answered `GET /api/products/barcode` **200 / 326.6 ms** then the stock record (`GET /api/inventory` 471 ms later), with the unregistered code's `404` measured the same session. **Purchase orders executed end to end on the phone**: create **201 / 950.9 ms** (Draft PO-0001), issue **200 / 625.9 ms** (Draft → Open), receive **200 / 1,290.4 ms** (`Received 24 unit(s) across 1 line`), read back as Shop Front stock **0 → 24**, history `STOCK_IN 24 — reference PO-0001`, order `RECEIVED`. And the **429 quota copy was observed** on screen — *the assistant has used today's request allowance… resets at midnight UTC (8AM in Manila)* — produced by a local pass-through proxy that intercepts only `POST /api/ai/chat`, so no free-model allowance was spent. **ISS-02's device half is clean** — zero occurrences of the signed-in email or Mongo id in the whole `adb logcat` buffer after a full sign-in, with the fix now **upstream in RNFirebase 27** (the v26 patch and its `postinstall` were deleted by the upgrade) — and **ISS-06 is recorded**: `minSdk 24` / `targetSdk 36` read from the installed APK, `compileSdk 37` from RN 0.88's own catalog; no `FATAL EXCEPTION` or ANR that day. **One real guard failure found and fixed:** `expo-sdk-pins` caught `expo-build-properties` (now a direct dependency, because `app.json` declares `minSdk 24` and Kotlin 2.4.0) missing from `.github/dependabot.yml`'s ignore list — added with its reason — and one **load-sensitive Jest flake** (`po-form-create-navigation` timing out at 5 s alongside the mirror and a build; 3.0 s alone) is recorded rather than smoothed over; afterwards **80 suites / 978 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc` and `eslint` clean. **Not verified, and recorded as such:** iOS (no macOS on this host) and **print → scan on paper** (the PDF decode rules out clipped bars but is not a scan of paper); a *genuine* end-of-allowance day, since the 429 copy came from a controlled refusal rather than a spent allowance. Artifacts: `docs/evidence/expo-sdk-58-2026-10-21/` (screenshots, the PDF, the decoder). Roadmap §27EH.

Device-acceptance harness (2026-10-21; **not an application phase**; adds `scripts/device/` and `server/test/device-harness.test.mjs` — no route, permission, schema, index, dependency, environment, EAS or mobile change) — the device pass is now a **script** instead of a hand-written list of `adb input tap` coordinates. `node scripts/device/run.mjs` drives the app **by route** (`inventoryai://dashboard`) and **by the label a person reads**, asserts each screen from the `uiautomator` tree, writes `report.md`/`report.json`/screenshots into `docs/evidence/device-runs/`, and exits **0 / 1 / 2** (passed / failed / **could not check**) so a signed-out session, a missing debuggable build or a missing artifact is never a pass. Three journeys — **`smoke`** (every main screen opens and names itself, read-only), **`label`** (reads the 13-digit code off the screen, taps *Share PDF*, decodes the PDF the phone wrote and fails if the two disagree) and **`draft`** (one draft purchase order, opt-in behind `--allow-writes`). **Measured on the OPPO CPH1969, Android 11 (API 30), SDK 58 development build over the USB tunnels, 2026-10-21:** smoke **17/17**, label **7/7** (screen `2000053300018`; PDF decoded `2000053300018`, **95 modules, 30 bars, 4 pt module**), draft **14/14** (`Draft PO-0002`, its own confirmation dismissed). It found **four defects in itself, each a wrong verdict about the app**: (1) `adb shell cat` rewrote every LF as CRLF, so an **11,225-byte PDF arrived as 11,472** and a correct label was reported *"contains no decodable EAN-13"* — binaries now go through **`exec-out`**, with a source guard that has a must-trip control; (2) a **mid-scroll dump** reported `Create draft order` at `[48,1863][1032,1443]`, so the computed centre landed **inside the quantity field** — `parseDump` marks `brokenBounds`, `findTapTarget` refuses them, `readScreen` retries an unstable dump like a blank one; (3) `type` now takes **`{ value, into: '<label>' }`**, finds the box below the label (exact-then-substring, because it reads `Quantity ordered *`), **reads the field back** and fails if the value is not there (with a geometry fallback when an empty field carries no label); (4) the **keyboard** is closed only when `dumpsys input_method` says it is open (a blind `BACK` left the form, then the app), and `draft` **dismisses its own dialog** — a leftover one failed an unrelated journey. `tap` scrolls to find a control below the fold, and `--until=<n>` stops after step `n` with the screen in `state.json`. **Real-world barcode scanning (owner report, same day):** the barcode of a **Clearance Multi-Purpose Wipes** pack was entered, the **physical pack** scanned with the app, and the app opened that record — the camera half proven against real packaging, which no harness here can produce. **Still open and stated as such:** physical **printing on paper** (no printer; the PDF decode rules out clipped bars but is not a scan of paper, so ISS-13's print → *scan* round trip stays open), **iOS** (no macOS; Phase 30B stays open), and the camera in general (one pack, by hand). **Release gate unchanged:** `chore/expo-sdk-58` is **not merged and nothing is released**; **CI is green on it** (workflow `ci` run `39642789958`, dispatched on the branch at head `f33675f`, all four jobs: `mobile`, `server` full suite, `secret-guard`, `attribution-guard` — a branch push does not trigger CI by itself, so the run was dispatched and the head is named with the claim), but a release still needs a `preview`/`production`-profile EAS build (the device ran a `development` one) and the iOS half — **Android is device-tested by a repeatable script, iOS has not been run at all**. Roadmap: the dated *device pass became a script* section before §28.

Device-acceptance harness — smoke asserts **data**, not chrome (2026-10-22; **not an application phase**; `scripts/device/` and `server/test/device-harness.test.mjs` only — no route, permission, schema, index, dependency, environment variable, EAS or mobile change) — the last harness gap, closed. A smoke run reported **17/17 on a phone where every data request was refused `401 SESSION_EXPIRED`**, because all 17 assertions were screen **headings**, and a heading renders over an empty list exactly as well as over a full one; the green report did not cause that defect, it made it invisible. New **`expectData`** step: `judgeDataRows(nodes, { match, what, min })` waits for a label only the server's data can produce — the inventory row's `33 available · 33 on hand`, a product row's `SKU … · Barcode …` — and returns **rows** (pass), **error** (fail), **empty** (**BLOCKED** — this tenant has none, so nothing was proven), **pending**/**absent** (fail at the deadline). A **`Session expired`** refusal **outranks stale rows** on purpose (the app keeps its last page in memory); loading is tested before empty so "still loading" never reads as "nothing to show". The smoke journey carries **two** data steps, and the patterns are **anchored**: `PRODUCT_ROW` first matched the screen's own search box (`Search by name, SKU or barcode…`) and counted a row on an empty list (run `06-41-07`, kept as the defect record), so it is `^(SKU \S|Barcode \d)` now, with a unit case holding a placeholder-only screen to zero. The five screens that may legitimately be **empty** (purchase orders, suppliers, restock, the assistant, the audit trail) are covered by **`expectClean`** / `judgeSettled`: wait for the screen to stop loading, **fail** (exit 1) if it stopped on the app's error state, and report a screen still loading at the deadline as **BLOCKED** (exit 2) — out of time is not a verdict. The smoke walk is **24 steps**: 7 screens, 2 data assertions, 5 "loaded without an error" checks. The preflight also **waits for the list to settle** (up to 10 s) instead of the fixed 2.5 s single read that missed the banner in the AUTH-03 reproduction, and `INVENTORY_ROW` is one exported definition shared by the preflight and the journey. **Measured on the OPPO CPH1969, Android 11 (API 30), SDK 58 development build over the USB tunnels, no sign-in between runs:** `06-46-06` **19/19 pass, exit 0** (`6 row(s), e.g. "… Shop Front, 33 available · 33 on hand …"`, `2 row(s), e.g. "SKU ROLESKU-202 · Household · Barcode 2000053300018"`), then the **must-trip control** with the API stopped (`06-42-53`) — **4/5, exit 1**, *"expected stock rows, but the screen is showing its error state (\"Something went wrong\")"* — then the server restarted and the same still-signed-in app back to **19/19 pass**, a second small proof of the AUTH-03 renewal fix. **`expectClean` was proven the same way, against one endpoint rather than all of them:** the API was moved to `:4001` and a pass-through proxy took `:4000`, refusing **only** `/api/purchase-orders` with `500` — the run (`07-17-43`) **passed the purchase-orders heading**, passed the two data steps on the healthy screens, and **failed** `expect Purchase Orders to load without an error` with *"the screen is showing its error state"*; the proxy was then removed and `07-29-33` is the clean **24/24**. Server **57 cases / 0 failures** in `device-harness.test.mjs` (44 → 57; every verdict paired with the state it must reject, `validateJourneys` refusing a malformed `expectData` five ways and an unnamed `expectClean`, and a journey table with its data or its "loaded cleanly" steps deleted failing), `eslint` and `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Not verified:** the products must-trip on hardware (proving an empty product list would mean archiving the tenant's products; the placeholder case is held by the suite) and the other five list screens, which still assert only their heading — they can be legitimately empty on a fresh tenant, and the failure mode is app-wide. Roadmap: the dated *The smoke journey asserted chrome; now it asserts data* section before §28.

The scripts that write and delete ask the server's environment question (2026-10-22; **not an application phase**; `scripts/perf/lib.mjs`, the two measurement scripts, the runner's classification and an 11-case suite — no route, permission, schema, dependency, mobile or environment-variable change) — the server had stopped booting without a named environment, but every seeder and cleanup asked the same question with `process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production'`, which answers "safe" to an unset value, to `Production`, and to `prod`. `prod` was the real failure: unrecognised everywhere, so `resolveDatabaseName` resolved it to **`inventoryai_dev_db`**, both checks passed, and the seeder wrote to the development database while the guard looked as though it had considered the question. `assertSafeTarget` now asks `classifyNodeEnv` exactly as `startServer` does — `production` refuses, a recognised non-production value goes on to the unchanged target check (URI present, database exactly `inventoryai_dev_db`, confirmation phrase), and anything else refuses as **ambiguous**, never echoing the value. A second guard, `assertReadableTarget`, covers `perf:measure` and `perf:turns`, which had **no** environment check at all while writing conversation rows and spending the shared allowance: recognised non-production environment, URI, and a resolved database that is the development or test one, printed before the first query, with no confirmation phrase because measuring is not destructive. **11 cases** plus source guards — all nine seeders/cleanups must call a target guard (the demo tenant's own `assertDemoTarget` counts, since it requires `--database=` to equal the resolved database), both measurement tools must call the reader guard, and **no script may compare `NODE_ENV` to `"production"` itself**, so the old rule cannot grow back in a tenth file. Measured on the real commands: `cleanup:perf` refused under `NODE_ENV=` and `NODE_ENV=prod` as ambiguous; `seed:perf` still refused at the phrase; `perf:measure` under `NODE_ENV=production` refused before any connection; `perf:turns` printed `database : inventoryai_dev_db (environment development)` and then failed only for a missing tenant. Server `test:unit` **1257 tests / 1205 pass / 0 fail / 52 skipped**, `tsc`, `eslint` and the module-graph checks clean. Limits: the demo tenant seeder is deliberately allowed on production (it populates the preview tenant and names the database on the command line), credentials still come from the operator's `.env`, and `ai-usage` / `verify-demo-data` stay unguarded read-only tools. Roadmap: the dated *The scripts that write and delete now ask the same environment question as the server* section before §28; guide 6 Parts 6 and 9)

A NODE_ENV the server cannot name stops a deployment from booting (2026-10-22; **not an application phase**; `config/env.ts`, `config/deploymentReadiness.ts`, `config/startup.ts` and the suite that pins them — no route, permission, role, schema, index, session, dependency, mobile or new environment variable) — the error-detail fix recorded just below made an unknown `NODE_ENV` harmless to **read**; it did not stop the process from **booting**. On a deployment that still meant `validateProductionConfig` was **skipped** and `resolveDatabaseName` selected **`inventoryai_dev_db`** — real traffic written into a developer's database, with the only visible symptom being the stacks that fix had just withheld, because a warning in a log nobody watches is not a control. `classifyNodeEnv(raw)` answers **five** things — `production`, `development`, `test`, and the two ways of saying nothing, **`missing`** and **`unrecognised`** (a variable nobody set and a value somebody typed are fixed in different places) — and `deploymentNotes()` names which one it is, ending in the one-variable fix, **never echoing the value**. **The rule is host-independent and explicit:** `startServer` accepts `production`, `development` or `test` — or a process that declares `TEST_MODE`, which the database resolver already pins to `inventoryai_test_db` and refuses alongside `NODE_ENV=production` — and **any other state refuses the boot**, logging one line that names the mistake and the fix, **exiting 1 and returning before `connectFn`**, so nothing is connected or served. No platform marker is consulted: a first version made this fatal only where Render's `RENDER`/`RENDER_GIT_COMMIT` were present, which made the same mistake fatal on one host and silent on another, and that predicate was removed the same day. `env.nodeEnv` now reads the **classified** value, so `NODE_ENV=" production "` selects the production checks instead of only the production database, and `GET /api/health` publishes the resolved `environment` beside the commit id so a deployment serving in the wrong posture is visible from outside without reading the host's dashboard. The old `nodeEnvWarning` line and its `TEST_MODE` suppression were removed with the change — the readiness note is the diagnostic and the gate is the decision, because a boot that reports one problem twice teaches operators to skim. **18 cases** in `phase-sec-debug-detail.test.mjs` (12 → 18), five of them real `startServer` boots (refused when nothing named the environment, refused on a typo, **booted** on each of the three recognised postures, booted as a declared test process with no `NODE_ENV` at all), with the refusal asserted to precede any connect or listen; **the control trips** — deleting the gate fails 3 cases and the file was restored byte-identically (md5 `763a4019…`). **Measured on four real boots:** `NODE_ENV=` → `refusing to start: NODE_ENV is not set … Nothing was connected or served.`, exit **1**; `NODE_ENV=prod` → the same refusal naming the accepted values, exit **1**; `NODE_ENV=development` → the gate passes and the boot proceeds; `TEST_MODE=unit` with no `NODE_ENV` → `environment=development listening on …`, exit **0** on kill. **The production question was answered before shipping, not assumed:** `https://ai-inventory-l1mi.onrender.com` publishes `commit: a0969f9…` and answers `POST /api/auth/login {}` with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` and **no `stack`** — and at that deployed commit stacks were appended unless `NODE_ENV === 'production'` **exactly**, so the live service already runs as `production` and this gate is **inert** there, which is the one thing a fatal boot gate has to establish. After the deploy the same endpoint states it outright, in `environment` — **measured live on 2026-10-22**, when `main` reached `0c79450` (CI run `39756616296` green on all four jobs) and `/api/health` answered `{"status":"ok","db":"connected","environment":"production","commit":"0c79450…"}`, with `/ready` and `/live` 200, no `stack` on a 400/401/404 sweep, and `/api/auth/renew` mounted for the first time. Server `test:unit` **1246 tests / 1194 pass / 0 fail / 52 skipped**, `tsc`, `eslint` and the module-graph check clean (479 files). Limits: `npm start` without a `.env` now refuses instead of guessing (that guess is what this removes), `env.nodeEnv` still falls back to `development` for scripts that never boot the server, and no deployed host was made to refuse on purpose — that proof is the measurement above. Also in `server/.env.example` and the README's environment section. Roadmap: the dated *A server must name the environment it serves* section before §28; checklist §51)

Error detail is released only to an explicitly development process (2026-10-22; **not an application phase**; `config/env.ts`, `middlewares/errorHandler.ts`, `config/startup.ts` and one test file — no route, permission, role, session, schema, index, dependency or environment-variable change) — the auth audit probed the live surface first (ten requests: unknown-email login, malformed bodies, a garbage reset code, missing and forged tokens, an unauthenticated session revoke) and nine answered exactly as designed; the tenth showed that **every** error response carried the server's own `stack`, including `file:///C:/Users/…/auth.service.ts:322:11`. The gate was `process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'`, which is **true** for an unset, empty or misspelled value — so `NODE_ENV=Production`, `prod`, `staging` or a host that sets only its platform's own variable would publish absolute local paths and line numbers to any caller who can provoke a 4xx. Now **fail-closed**: `isDebugNodeEnv()` grants stacks to `development` and `test` **only**, `nodeEnvWarning()` names the consequences at boot (production checks skipped, development database selected, stacks withheld) and is silent under `TEST_MODE`, and `KNOWN_NODE_ENVS` is one list so the predicate and the warning cannot drift. Status, `error`, `code`, `fields` and `applied` are identical in both postures — only `stack` differs — and 5xx never carried either. **Verified:** 12 cases in `phase-sec-debug-detail.test.mjs`, with the **must-trip control** that restoring the old condition fails **3** of them; **live at boot**, `NODE_ENV= PORT=4002 node src/index.ts` printed the warning and answered `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with no `stack` while the `development` server still carries one; server **1946 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint` clean. **Also established and left alone, as documented decisions:** JWT verification pins HS256/issuer/audience, `PATCH /api/auth/me` allowlists its fields so email cannot be changed, session revocation is scoped by `userId` in the query filter, `deviceLabel` is clamped, login is timing-equalized, reset/verification reserve attempts atomically; **authenticated routes stay unlimited** (including `change-password`'s bcrypt, an accepted availability consideration) and `change-password`'s strict `iat <` window is the Phase 15 contract. The wider fail-open posture is unchanged and now announced: an unset `NODE_ENV` still skips the production checks and selects the **development database**. Recorded in `docs/AUTH_SECURITY_MASTER_CHECKLIST.md` §50 and the dated roadmap section before §28.

Phase N-3 — Firebase-Native Password Recovery, Additive (mobile only; two React Native Firebase Auth verbs — `sendPasswordResetEmail` with `ActionCodeSettings` pointing at `inventoryai://reset-password`, and `confirmPasswordReset`; the Phase 1 `FORBIDDEN` verb map relaxed for **exactly one verb**, with the reason written in place; `forgot-password` is Firebase-first with the custom 26B endpoint reachable ONLY when the build has no Firebase configuration, so a Firebase outage can never become a downgrade; `reset-password` redeems a Firebase `?oobCode=` while `?token=` still goes to `POST /api/auth/reset-password`; enumeration resistance moved into the client — `auth/user-not-found`/`auth/user-disabled` answer SENT — and a failed redemption can never fall back to the server endpoint; the `auth/*-continue-uri` family answers as a configuration fault rather than "try again"; the 26B/27 routes, controller, service, token model, mail layer and their suites are UNTOUCHED, and there is no server, permission, schema, dependency, environment, EAS or Firebase Console change; guarded by two new suites (36 cases), mobile 36 suites / 509 tests, `tsc`/`eslint` clean; **an on-device run against the live Firebase project WAS then performed** (F2.9 development APK driven by Metro): `?oobCode=` **renders the reset form** (F-8 closed client-side), the legacy `?token=` path still works, `sendPasswordResetEmail` for the dedicated test address reached the project and answered **`onComplete:success`**, and `confirmPasswordReset` with a deliberately fake code produced `FirebaseAuthActionCodeException` → the screen's *"invalid or has expired"* copy — so the project **ACCEPTED the custom-scheme `ActionCodeSettings`** and the gate is **NARROWED**; **the reset email itself was never received or inspected (no mailbox), so delivery and the email's link remain UNVERIFIED, test items 3/6/7 remain BLOCKED, and L-17 stays OPEN** (the preview/release build this device carried cannot be driven by Metro, which is why the F2.9 development APK was installed over it by owner decision); a Firebase-native reset also does not revoke Inventory AI sessions, which is the credential-change bridge's job (N-4, designed and NOT implemented); roadmap §27U)
Phase 32O — Registration & Email-Verification Decision Record (documentation only; the read-only registration/sign-up audit is written down and its open questions are decided — the registration **order** is the mechanism: Firebase Email/Password account → **verification email sent** → the user verifies → `reload()` confirms `emailVerified === true` → **only then** Inventory AI creates the MongoDB `User` **and** `Business` together (OWNER, `User.businessId` still required) → Inventory AI JWT + server session → main app; **no `User`, no `Business`, no JWT and no session exist before verification**, so there is no half-registered tenant and a signed-in-but-unverified identity is held on a **client verify screen** that can resend via **native Firebase Auth**; **`requireAuth` is deliberately NOT modified** — a server-side check would change the frozen Phase 23/24 order and strand every already-shipped client — so the Phase 23/24 authorization order and status codes are preserved; the **Firebase UID stays the only** Firebase→MongoDB identity join key and **email is never one**; MongoDB stays authoritative for `businessId`/`role`/`status`/permissions/lifecycle; an unverified **Google** identity is **refused** per Firebase's signed `email_verified` claim, not the provider name; `User.emailVerified` is persisted with **no per-request Firebase call**; the legacy `/api/auth/register` path stays **transitional** (N-6 retires it) and **universal** enforcement becomes possible only then; the **verification-link return is SELECTED as the Inventory AI deep-link flow** with the **Firebase-hosted page recorded as the fallback**, inheriting the open L-17 gate so the delivery half must be measured live; **OD-3 resolved** and audit gaps **D-5…D-9** closed; three source-recorded findings — `nativeCurrentIdToken()` passes no argument to `getIdToken()` so RG-9 needs a **forced refresh** (boundary change, **not** a guard change), `sendEmailVerification`/`reload` are **not** in the Phase 1 `FORBIDDEN` map so **no guard relaxation is owed**, and **no verify screen or verification gate exists today**; documentation only — no code, test, schema, dependency, environment, EAS or Firebase Console change, and nothing implemented; roadmap §27V, design §14C.9, checklist §39) — **superseded in part 2026-10-02 (AUTH-04):** the **verification-link + native resend** mechanism is replaced by a **custom 6-digit Inventory AI code delivered by Brevo** (design §14C.10, checklist §40, roadmap §27Y). This entry is the historical record and is **not** rewritten.
Phase 32P — N-4 D2 Resolved: the Recovery-Context Bridge Caller (documentation only; closes the previously undocumented **D2** gap between Firebase password recovery and the planned N-4 credential-change bridge — the bridge is **authenticated** and needs a live Inventory AI session, which is correct for the in-app password *change* but **unsatisfiable for the primary reset flow**, since `forgot-password`/`reset-password` are **public** screens, the reset completes inside Firebase, and the reset screen clears the local session before returning to Login, so at the instant the password changes the caller holds **no** session and a Firebase-native reset would have revoked **nothing**; **the approved resolution is to establish the context first, never to relax authentication:** after the reset the user **signs in again with the new password** (which is also what makes the **300-second `auth_time` freshness** check satisfiable) → a **fresh Firebase ID token** → the **existing public exchange** mints a **temporary recovery-context session** → the **bridge** stamps `passwordChangedAt` and calls `revokeAllSessions()`, which revokes **all** sessions **including that temporary one** (`revokeAllSessions` has **no caller exception**) → a **re-exchange** mints the **final** session, valid because its `iat >= changedAtSec`; the recovery-context session is **structurally an ordinary session** — **no new session type, no new field, no "recovery" flag** — so the frozen session surface is untouched, and stopping after the bridge is **loud and recoverable** (sign in again), never silent; **restated as still binding:** `passwordChangedAt` **and** `revokeAllSessions()` remain the N-4 invariant, a Firebase ID token is **never** the application's permanent bearer credential, the **Firebase UID → MongoDB `User`** binding stays authoritative, and **no public unauthenticated `credentials-changed` endpoint** and **no Cloud Function / Admin-SDK trigger** is introduced (§14C.3.6's rejections stand); findings — the 300 s window must **not** be widened for recovery and the `401 SESSION_INVALIDATED`-is-success rule now also covers recovery (still to be pinned by a test), and the one remaining piece is **OD-9**, the trigger, recorded rather than chosen silently; documentation only — no code, test, schema, dependency, environment, EAS or Firebase Console change, and the bridge is still **not implemented**; roadmap §27W, design §14C.3.8, checklist §39) — *(Annotated 2026-10-09: the bridge shipped the same day, in `703a9da` — `server/src/services/credentialsChanged.service.ts` and the authenticated `POST /api/auth/credentials-changed` — so the "still not implemented" clause in the record above is superseded on the implementation question only; `L-17` and `OD-10` remain OPEN. See the §27W annotation.)*
Phase 32Q — OD-9 LOCKED: the Automatic Recovery Marker (documentation only; closes the last piece of **D2** — **OD-9**, how the client knows that a post-reset sign-in must run the bridge — locking it as **M-1…M-10**: a **durable, non-secret recovery marker** in `expo-secure-store` under **`inventoryai.auth.passwordResetPending`** holding **only an account fingerprint**, never a password, an `oobCode`, a Firebase ID token, an Inventory AI JWT, a `userId`, a `businessId`, a `role` or tenant data, so it is a **flow selector, never a credential**; it is created with **`verifyPasswordResetCode(oobCode)`** (present natively on Android and iOS, resolving the account's **email**), whose email is **memory-only** — used for the automatic sign-in and the fingerprint and **never** as an authorization source, the server still deriving the account exclusively from `requireAuth → req.user.id → Mongo User → firebaseUid`; **F1 automatic recovery is approved:** verify → write marker → `confirmPasswordReset` → sign in with the new password → **temporary** public exchange → `credentials-changed` → `revokeAllSessions` → **final** public exchange → `applySession`, where the temporary session is **held only in a local variable** (never persisted, never applied, never navigated with, never in route params) and is revoked with every other active session because `revokeAllSessions()` has **no caller exception**; the marker lifecycle is fixed — created **before** the password-change operation, **cleared** after a successful bridge acknowledgement or a **success-equivalent** `401 SESSION_INVALIDATED`, **kept** on `409 FIREBASE_REAUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` and on transport failure, **never consumed on a fingerprint mismatch**, and the legacy `?token=` recovery **never creates it** because that server flow already revokes; **two limits recorded, not glossed** — the marker is **device-local**, so a reset on one device followed by the first password sign-in on another is **not detectable** and OD-9 is **not** a universal cross-device revocation guarantee, and **Option E** (server-side `passwordUpdatedAt` derivation) is **DEFERRED as a separate owner decision — now **OD-10** — with nothing of it introduced** (no Mongo field, no extra Admin lookup); `requireAuth`, `passwordChangedAt`, `revokeAllSessions()`, the UID→Mongo `User` mapping, Mongo business/role authority and the JWT/session architecture are **unchanged** and **no new session type or field** is introduced; **L-17 stays OPEN** as the end-to-end gate and **gains one requirement** — the live device test must confirm `verifyPasswordResetCode()` returns the correct email **without consuming** the reset code; findings F-19…F-22; documentation only — no code, test, schema, dependency, environment, EAS or Firebase Console change, and the bridge remains **not implemented**; roadmap §27X, design §14C.3.9, checklist §39) — *(Annotated 2026-10-09: the bridge shipped on the same day as this record, in `703a9da`; the "bridge remains not implemented" clause is superseded on the implementation question only, while `L-17` and the deferred `OD-10` remain OPEN exactly as recorded. See the §27W annotation.)*
Phase A / F-1 — Firebase Disconnect Re-scoped into a Credential Change (the unlink capability **REMOVED**, not deprecated: the route `POST /api/auth/firebase/unlink`, its service, controller handler, payload validator, both audit actions and the mobile API wrapper are gone from the committed tree, and the operation moved into **credential-change authority** — Firebase `updatePassword` → the shipped N-4 `credentials-changed` bridge → the session the sequence ends with — so `firebaseUid` is never cleared and no reachable path clears it; the old flow's credential-authority dead end (the OLD password staying valid at Firebase while the server no longer knew the account, resolving to `REGISTRATION_REQUIRED` and then `EMAIL_IN_USE`) is what removing it closes; **`Phase A / F-1 — 6e2252f4c63a7bd98e1d63a4703f21ab236c93a9`**, 2026-10-01, 27 files +1,347/−3,372; the formal record is **roadmap §27AA**, written retrospectively because the commit predates §27Z)
AUTH-01 — Firebase Revocation Checking & Verification-Failure Classification (`checkRevoked: true` on the Admin SDK verification path; a failure classified into three disjoint outcomes — terminal `FirebaseTokenError`, transient `FIREBASE_UNAVAILABLE` → 503, and the configuration fault `FIREBASE_NOT_CONFIGURED`; everything outside the four unambiguous SDK codes, including the overloaded `auth/argument-error`, is transient so an outage cannot be presented as a bad credential; a structural JWT pre-check and an `aud`/`iss` assertion as defence-in-depth on the real Admin SDK path only; a guard fails if the installed `firebase-admin` version changes; no route, schema, session/JWT or mobile change and no `revokeRefreshTokens()`; `b3f1146` 2026-10-02; roadmap §27AB)
AUTH-02 — Short-Lived JWT, Enforced Issuer/Audience & the 14-Day Absolute Session Cap (`JWT_EXPIRES_IN` default `1h`; `JWT_ISSUER`/`JWT_AUDIENCE` minted and validated by `jsonwebtoken` on every request, defaults `inventory-ai`/`inventory-ai-api`, deliberately non-environment-specific; session longevity bounded separately **in code** — `SESSION_ABSOLUTE_CAP_DAYS = 14` with `expiresAt` anchored at creation — so the JWT lifetime can never lengthen a session; `requireAuth` distinguishes the recoverable `SESSION_EXPIRED` from the other 401s; pre-AUTH-02 tokens carry neither claim and are rejected, so every existing user signs in once more; `6a0f41b` 2026-10-02; roadmap §27AC)
AUTH-03 — Inventory Session Renewal (server boundary and mobile client; `POST /api/auth/renew`, authenticated, account from `req.user.id` and session from `req.auth.jti`, body carries only `{ idToken }`; a new token for the SAME session — `jti` unchanged, no session row created, `expiresAt` never written, no audit row per renewal, so the 14-day cap cannot be extended by renewing; uid mismatch → `409 FIREBASE_IDENTITY_CONFLICT`, unverified → `400 EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED`, per-IP limiter → `429 SESSION_RENEW_RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After`; middleware order `requireAuth → limiter → controller`; mobile reports the horizon as `min(tokenExpiresAt, sessionExpiresAt)` but renews proactively only when the **token's** `exp` is inside a 10-minute window and the token, not the session cap, is the earlier boundary (renewal never moves the cap), paced to one per 60 s unless the token is within 20 s of expiry — *fixed 2026-10-22: scheduling from the horizon renewed on every request for the last ten minutes of every session, and with a short `JWT_EXPIRES_IN` on every request at all, measured at 121 renewals / 122 dashboard loads in three minutes; the recovery hook now also receives the token its request was refused with, so a sibling request whose token a renewal already replaced is retried with the current one instead of minting a third — measured as three renewals for one dashboard load* — and on foreground, single-flight with an authentication epoch, classifying failures by application error code rather than HTTP status — one retry for `SESSION_EXPIRED`, never for terminal codes, graceful degradation for transient ones; `3085e3f` + the `5da0692` re-entrancy fix, 2026-10-02; roadmap §27AD)
AI-1 — The AI Turn Time Budget (fixes **ISS-21**, the one defect the authenticated device walkthrough §27BC found; server + mobile, **no new dependency**): the server's ceiling for one chat turn was the *product* of two unrelated knobs (`AI_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` 60 s × `AI_MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS` 6) and was stated nowhere, while the mobile client aborted **every** route at a fixed `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000` — so a slow-but-successful answer (one measured working answer took **13 283 ms**) was killed by the phone and then reported to the user as *"Cannot reach the server"*, naming the wrong component and sending them to check a connection that was working. Now: **one wall-clock deadline per turn** (`AI_TURN_DEADLINE_MS`, default 60 000) that each provider call is clamped to the remaining time of, so the loop can never exceed it; the assistant gets its own client budget (`AI_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, 75 000) with a **guard that reads the server's default out of its source** and fails if the client budget is not above it, because two numbers that were never designed to agree would otherwise drift again; a timeout is its own fact (`504 AI_TIMEOUT`, its own sentence, checked **before** `isNetwork` — the transport always distinguished it and the screen discarded it); and a turn whose caller has gone away is **cancelled** (`499 AI_ABANDONED`) instead of paying for a model call nobody will read, which also makes an abandoned turn visible server-side for the first time. **Two corrections of the record:** §27BC's F-51 wording said the chat request "never arrived" — it said only that it was never **logged**, and `httpLogger` emits on `res.on('finish')` alone, so an aborted request leaves no line at all (probe: **1** line for a completed request, **0** for an aborted one, with a positive control), making the zero occurrences exactly what an abort looks like; and `res.on('close')` was **not** sufficient to detect a caller who left during auth, found by the failing test and fixed with a `res.destroyed` check. Server **1331/1331**, mobile **688/688**, typecheck/lint/both module-graph checks clean on both sides. **VERIFIED ON THE DEVICE 2026-10-12 (§27BE)**, on the same OPPO CPH1969 over the USB tunnels with scrcpy mirroring: four `POST /api/ai/chat` turns returned **201** at **6 185 / 8 212 / 10 302 / 19 388 ms** — the last **4.4 s beyond the old 15 s client abort**, still answering and still rendering on screen, which is exactly the request the old code would have killed — and a forced **2 s** turn deadline returned **504** at **2 224 ms** with the app showing *"The assistant took too long to answer. Please try again."* and *"Cannot reach the server"* absent from the screen. The only 5xx in the whole run is that deliberate 504. **The Task 5 abandoned-turn cancellation — the item this entry left open — is now measured**: §27BE's two inconclusive attempts were superseded by **§27CM (2026-10-17)**, where AI-9's Stop against a held stub produced `outcome=AI_ABANDONED` (`calls=1`, so the stop landed inside an in-flight provider call — bounded, not eliminated) with the stub itself logging *"caller had already gone away; nothing sent"* **12.2 s before its held answer was due**, and measured again in **§27CW** (2026-10-17), which also read the member's own conversation list: an aborted turn leaves **no** conversation and no orphan USER message. The one half still unprovoked is a stop landing *after* the model had already finished, whose storage promise rests on Phase 07E / M7. *(This sentence said "still unverified" for five days after §27CM measured it; §27CW corrects it where it stands.)* Streaming and the model choice are the owner's next decisions and were deliberately left out; roadmap §27BD, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-ai-turn-time-budget.md`)
AI-2 — The Degraded Turn Made Visible, a Bounded Retry, and the Read-Only Invariant Made Provable (fixes **ISS-23** and delivers the code half of **ISS-22**; server + mobile, **no new dependency, no model change, no route, permission or schema change**): the 2026-10-12 device run measured **three defect-class turns in five** from the free-tier gateway (§27BE) — **F-52** (a `200` carrying only a `thinking` block: no text, no tool call), **F-54** (a `200` with a **zero-length body**) and **F-55** (raw Markdown on screen) — and the first two **left no server-side trace whatsoever**: the canned sentence *"I could not generate a response right now. Please try again."* was returned as an ordinary **HTTP 201**, and a repository-wide `grep` for it found exactly one occurrence, its own assignment. Now: a content-less `200` is **retried once** (`AI_EMPTY_RETRY_MAX`, default 1) as an ordinary loop iteration, so the retry **shares `AI_TURN_DEADLINE_MS` and `AI_MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS`** and can neither extend a turn nor run unbounded, and it applies **only** to the empty-completion shape — **a provider failure is never retried**, so an outage cannot be answered by paying for it twice while the cause is hidden; a turn that exhausts the budget is **marked** — `degraded` is `EMPTY_COMPLETION` or `ITERATION_LIMIT`, **absent rather than null on a healthy turn** — and it is still persisted and still a `201`, with one greppable server line (`[ai] degraded turn reason=… attempts=… empty=… model=… tools=…`, where `empty` counts responses **seen** rather than retries **authorised**, so the line cannot overstate what happened), the **cause reported rather than the trigger** because a turn that ran out of steps while the model kept answering nothing is an empty-completion problem and naming it a step-count problem sends an operator to the wrong knob. The **security half**, and the reason this is more than a reliability patch: CLAUDE.md §4's read-only scope was mandatory and yet **nothing in the repository could fail if a write capability were added to the tool table** — the invariant held only because every contributor remembered it — so `server/test/AI2-ai-readonly-tools.test.mjs` now pins every tool's permission to the four **read** capabilities (and asserts those four are not write-shaped), scans the table's **own source** for any service call outside a read-only allow-list, requires **every** tool to refuse a smuggled `businessId`/`$where`/`role` argument, and asserts the nine write-tool names a model actually invents (`createProduct`, `stockOut`, `adjustStock`, …) all answer `TOOL_NOT_FOUND`; **the scan carries a positive control that mutates the source twice**, because a guard that cannot fail is decoration. Plus **ISS-23**: the assistant bubble strips Markdown markers — deliberately **not** a renderer, so no new dependency — with two limits the suite pins: **single underscores are never emphasis** (`SKU_123_AB`, `MIN_STOCK_LEVEL` are data, not formatting) and **a lone asterisk counts only when a non-space follows it**, so `12 * 3 = 36` survives as arithmetic. **Owner decision 2026-10-13: the assistant stays on the free tier** — no paid model, no provider change — so the model is untouched and the residual is accepted for V1: the retry reduces and *counts* the symptom, it does not make a free-tier model reliable. Evidence: server **1353 tests / 192 suites** and mobile **54 suites / 701 tests**, **0 failures** (up from 1331 and 688), `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean on both sides; **every new suite was watched failing first** (the retry suite `1 !== 2` provider calls, `1 !== 3` for a raised budget, `degraded === undefined`, and *"Missing expected rejection"* exactly where today's code resolves instead of timing out; the mobile suite **13/13** on `stripMarkdown is not a function`); and the guard **proven able to fail in both directions** — clean tree exit **0**, a write call injected into an executor **1** (failing both the scan and its positive control), a tool gated on `INVENTORY_MANAGE` **1**, restored **0** and **byte-identical** to the pre-break copy. One transient is recorded rather than smoothed over: the run printed `[db] MongoDB connection lost` once (the known Atlas `ReplicaSetNoPrimary` of §27BC/§27BE) and recovered with **0 failures and 0 skipped**. **NOT device-verified** — the failure rate is not yet re-measured and the Markdown strip's wiring awaits the next run; roadmap §27BF)
AI-3 — The Assistant's Queries at Catalogue Scale (fixes **ISS-24**, the defect the large-dataset stress test found; server only — **no route, permission, schema, dependency, session or mobile change, and no model or provider change**): the owner asked for the assistant to be tested *"under more demanding, real-world conditions"*, so a synthetic tenant was seeded (`npm run seed:perf` — isolated, labelled, disposable, refuses production and the test database, requires `SEED_CONFIRM`) and every tool was timed against it with **zero model calls** (`npm run perf:measure`), because provider quota is the scarce resource (~50 free requests a day, §27BF). Measured at **100 / 1 000 / 10 000 products**, **seven of nine tools grew ~8–9× for 10× the data** — `getInventory` 122 → 659 → **5 665 ms**, `getInventoryHistory` 119 → 605 → **5 520**, `getProductsByLocation` 99 → 408 → **3 555**, and the stock/dashboard family 86–133 → 253–284 → **1 983–2 281** — while `searchProducts` and `getProduct` stayed flat at ~210 ms; **payloads stayed flat** at 3.8–7.4 k characters, so paging was never the problem. `explain('executionStats')` gave the mechanism exactly: each slow pipeline ran a `$lookup` **once per tenant row** *before* the page window — 20 105 rows twice for the inventory page (3.1 s + 3.2 s), 19 944 twice for history (3.0 s + 2.8 s), 10 000 for the availability family (2.0–2.2 s) — each an **index** lookup at ~0.15 ms whose **count** was the cost, because `$skip`/`$limit` sat at the bottom inside a `$facet` and a facet cannot run without everything above it: a 15–25 row page paid for the whole partition, and **no index can fix a fan-out of one query per row**. Fixed in three shapes: the page window moved **above** the joins wherever nothing between them filters (`transactions.list`, `inventory.listRows` — the latter gaining the model's `{businessId, updatedAt:-1, _id:-1}` index so the order is index-provided, both counting with a separate `$count` over the same `$match`), and wherever a stock predicate genuinely needs the join it was **turned around**: one grouped `$unionWith` pass over inventory merged by key with a single `$group` (`$max` ignores missing values, so each accumulator takes the side that carries it, and every filter/sort/`$regex` stays inside MongoDB), plus the dashboard's four counts computed from one grouped inventory read and a projection-only catalogue read instead of 10 000 joins. **9 of 9 tools are now ≤ 650 ms at 10 000 products** (getInventory **75**, history **68**, byLocation 258, out 259, low 265, reorder 268, search 214, getProduct 212, summary 413) — the seven slow ones together, **23.4 s → 1.6 s**. Proven **output-identical rather than merely faster**: `npm run perf:golden` captures the full payload, hash and total of **17** tool/argument combinations — including the ones that change which pipeline branch runs — and **all 17 matched byte-for-byte before and after**; the suite stayed **1353 tests / 192 suites / 0 failures**, with `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks clean. A real stress run over HTTP (`npm run perf:turns` — a session minted the way login mints one, so no credential lives in a script) answered **2 of 3** turns **grounded and numerically exact** (1 492 restock items; 64 units across 3 locations) at **34.9 s / 31.1 s**, and the third returned **504 AI_TIMEOUT at 60.3 s** — provider latency, not query time: at this scale the **free-tier model, not the database, is the bottleneck** (0.3–0.4 s of queries against 30–60 s of model time), which is the measured cost of the owner's *"stay on the free tier"* decision. **NOT device-verified, and NOT measured above 10 000 products** — the merged tools still scan the partition by construction (0.26–0.65 s at 10 k), so a ≥50 k measurement is the recorded next step; the new index builds on the next server boot (`autoIndex`, the same route the Phase 20G/20H indexes took), and `npm run cleanup:perf` removes the seeded tenant. **DEVICE-VERIFIED 2026-10-13 (§27BG)** on the OPPO CPH1969 over the USB tunnels with scrcpy mirroring, against the 10 000-product tenant: the dashboard on the phone showed **8 508 / 620 / 1 492 / 10 000** — every figure equal to the golden ground truth — **three** of four real turns returned **201** at **40.2 / 34.8 / 12.2 s** (tools `getLowStockProducts`, `getInventoryHistory`, `getProduct`; every answer **grounded and numerically exact**, including *64 units across three locations*) and the fourth returned **504 AI_TIMEOUT at 60.5 s**, rendering the AI-1 copy with *"Cannot reach the server"* absent. The comparison to the earlier device runs (6.2–19.4 s at dozens of products) is the point: **the tools are 0.07–0.3 s of each turn and the free-tier model is the rest**, so at this scale the provider — not the database — bounds the assistant, exactly as the HTTP measurement said. Two new findings: **ISS-25** (Markdown *tables* render literally — AI-2's strip never covered them, and a 20-row list is when a model reaches for one) and a **self-contradictory seeded ledger** (every movement was written as an increase regardless of type, so 6 615 of 6 616 STOCK_OUT rows claimed stock went up and the assistant reported them faithfully) — **fixed in `seed:perf` and proven consistent on a re-seeded tenant (0 inconsistent rows)**. Roadmap §27BG.
AI-4 — The Assistant's Answer, Rendered (fixes **ISS-25**, and the defect the owner's three screenshots showed; **mobile only** — no server file, no route, no permission, no schema, no dependency, and **no prompt change**, so the request path and the token cost per turn are untouched by construction): the owner asked for answers that are *presentable, organized and easier to read* and set the constraints — no new dependency, no expensive API calls, no noticeable latency, no unnecessary tokens, no loss of accuracy. The scoping measurement came first, and it decided the design: a census of **every stored assistant answer** found **3 tables, 2 headings, 4 bullet lists, 0 numbered steps**, so the model already writes the structures; nothing needed to be asked of it, and **a formatting instruction in the system prompt was rejected as a permanent per-turn token cost for a benefit rendering already delivers** (recorded as a rejected option, not an oversight). AI-2's approach could not be extended, and this is the whole reason ISS-25 existed: in `| Product | SKU |` the **pipes carry the row/column relationship**, so stripping them destroys the structure and keeping them (what shipped) puts punctuation on the screen — a table has to be **rendered**. `stripMarkdown` was **moved** to `mobile/lib/assistantMarkdown.ts` — the AI-2 suite's import address changed and **not one assertion did** — and given a precise residual role: applied to every span the parser does not model, so a marker nobody taught it about still cannot reach the screen, while the two AI-2 invariants (a single underscore is never emphasis, because `SKU_123_AB` and `MIN_STOCK_LEVEL` are data; `12 * 3 = 36` stays arithmetic) are preserved and re-pinned **through the renderer**. Two presentation calls are stated so they can be rejected: **a wide table becomes one card per row, not a grid** (the observed answers have five and seven columns, and in a ~400 px bubble a seven-column grid leaves each column under 60 px — which is exactly what made the screenshot unreadable — so at ≤ 3 columns a grid is rendered and above it the row's own name becomes a card title with its remaining columns as label/value lines, the threshold being one named constant), and **bold is rendered as bold rather than deleted**, because the owner asked for important information to be highlighted and styling a parsed run is free. **A real defect was found on the way, in shipped code:** `stripMarkdown` had **no word boundary at all**, so it silently rewrote `4*5*6` as `456` and `A*B*C` as `ABC` — a shortened multiplication still reads as an answer, which is the worst class of bug in an assistant that reports quantities, and AI-2's own reasoning ("would silently corrupt the very data the assistant exists to report") always implied the fix; a marker must now **stand apart from a word** to count as markup, in the parser **and** the residual pass so the two agree, which means ambiguous markup is treated as **data** (visible markers are ugly, a wrong number is wrong). Cost was **measured, not argued**: on the largest real stored answer (1 494 chars) `stripMarkdown` cost **0.0075 ms** and `parseAssistantMarkdown` costs **0.1552 ms** — **+0.148 ms** of added CPU work per answer against turns of **12 236–40 226 ms** on the device (~0.001 % of the answer's latency), incurred once per rendered bubble because the parse is memoised on the answer and the component is `memo`ised; scaling was checked for the failure mode that matters (**10 → 160 synthetic rows / 1 189 → 18 639 chars is 0.114 → 1.666 ms, i.e. 16× the data for 14.6× the time — linear**), with a deliberately enormous absolute bound left in the suite as a permanent guard; the one number that went **up** is view count and it is recorded rather than glossed — a table answer renders **355** nodes instead of 1 `<Text>` (a one-line answer renders 3), ordinary for a React Native screen and bounded by `FlatList` virtualisation plus `memo`. Verification: **29 new cases** in `mobile/__tests__/ai4-assistant-answer-render.test.tsx` (the AI-2 suite's 13 cases are re-pointed at the new module with **no assertion changed**), written first and watched fail on a missing module, pinning the parser's structure, the renderer's output, both AI-2 invariants through the new path, both directions of the boundary rule, and as **one property over six realistic inputs including both real answers** that no `|`, `**`, `__` or `---` can reach the screen by any path; the guard was **proven able to fail three times** — wide tables back to a grid → the card assertion, the `---` separator no longer consumed → **8 failures** including both no-markup properties, the boundary removed again → the two arithmetic cases — with the tree then restored **byte-identical**, and **one mutation that appeared to pass in silence was a `sed` whose escaping did not match** (a no-op mutation, not a blind guard), re-run with an assertion that the edit landed and failing exactly as intended, because a mutation that cannot apply is not evidence; `tsc` also earned its keep by catching a genuine **type-design error the tests could not** — a table row is a list of *cell*s and `rows` was one array dimension short — fixed by naming the levels (`Cell`, `Row`) rather than by loosening an assertion.
