**the platform limit is recorded, not assumed** (protection and rulesets `403`, `security_and_analysis: null`, `allow_forking: false` → `422 "only on org-owned private repositories"`), so the policy is **enforced where it can be and observable where it cannot**: **`access-guard.yml`** **ran** daily and hard-failed on a non-private repository (the monitor was **removed 2026-10-09** — see *Workflow simplification* below), a fork/fork network, or a collaborator other than the owner — its **first real run (`37893715498`) measured the collaborators endpoint answering `200` to `GITHUB_TOKEN`** while invitations, deploy keys, webhooks, secrets, the Actions policy, protection and rulesets all answer **`403`**, so owner-only membership is **asserted unattended** and those seven are **printed with their status, never counted as a pass**; the deep audit is **`scripts/audit-access.mjs`** against the owner's own `gh` session (dependency-free, **names only and never a value**, non-zero exit on a violation, run live with **no violations**); the two workflow actions are **pinned to commit SHAs** verified via `git ls-remote` to be what the floating tag already resolved to — **re-pinned from v4.4.0 to v7 on 2026-10-11** (checkout v7.0.1, setup-node v7.1.0), which clears the Node 20 deprecation warning every run had printed (ISS-12) — with a `github-actions` Dependabot entry maintaining the pins; two stale claims were **corrected, not left standing** (`dependabot.yml`'s "protected `main`" and the monitor's own unreadable-collaborator claim); **nothing was revoked, narrowed or disabled**, and Render reaches this repository through a **GitHub App installation — not a webhook — which is why no CLI check here can see it**, making the app list and account **2FA** the two owner-side confirmations; **a private repository is not an uncopyable one**; roadmap §27AF, checklist §42)
Documentation truth pass, invalidated citations & a route-inventory guard (2026-10-12) — a read-only audit of the **39 tracked documents** found four wrong claims and **one false security control**: **branch protection is NOT applied** (the API answers `403 Upgrade to GitHub Pro or make this repository public` on a private repository at this plan level), corrected in place on the claim itself in this file, roadmap §27AE and checklist §42; the Firebase design authority §2.6 said **68 routes** where the mounted set is **72** (**47 permission-gated + 13 authenticated + 12 public + 0 role-gated**) and its auth-surface table omitted four routes — both corrected, and the table now lists all 17; `README.md` called the mobile app "(JavaScript)" and claimed 129 `.ts`/`.tsx` files in `app/ components/ lib/` where **82** are measured — both fixed. Two findings with longer tails: the **2026-10-11 message-only rewrite invalidated the documentation's own evidence citations** (a fresh clone resolves **2 of 27** sampled pre-rewrite SHAs; fetching `refs/pull/*` resolves **25**; CLAUDE.md's "ancestor of `main`" claim for `703a9da` is false) and it left **all 99 trailer-bearing commits still fetchable from `origin`** through the **58 `refs/pull/*` refs** it advertises — so §27AS is corrected, **`refs/original/*` is RETAINED** (it is what makes ~132 cited SHAs resolve locally, and the earlier suggestion to delete it is withdrawn), and the standing convention is now **cite section anchors, file paths and PR numbers as primary evidence, and treat any pre-2026-10-11 SHA as historical**. New DB-free guard `server/test/phase-docs-route-inventory.test.mjs` pins §2.6 to the route declarations and the app registrations (11 tests, break/restore proven: exit 1 broken, exit 0 restored); `server` lint, typecheck and both module-graph checks clean; **no source, route, schema, permission, dependency, workflow or session change, and no Git history operation of any kind**. Roadmap §27AX.
ISS-26's device half — the assistant's LaTeX decoded correctly and was still hiding a different number (mobile only; **no dependency, no renderer, no prompt change**; found on the device 2026-10-14): §27BQ closed ISS-26's *record* gap and ended by stating that **nothing in it had been device-checked**, so the stored answer was re-opened on the OPPO CPH1969 (the F2.9 `development` APK over Metro, USB tunnels) with **no new turn and therefore no provider quota spent at all**. The decoder itself is now confirmed on hardware — not one backslash, brace or command survives on the screen. The same screen, however, showed `17+ 5/113 × 100 ≈ 19.5%`, and read as written that is **17 + (5 ÷ 113) = 17.04**, a different number from the 19.5% printed at the end of it. The message was then read straight back out of `ai_messages` to establish ground truth instead of inferring it: the model had written `\frac{17 + 5}{113}` — *"17 plus 5, over 113"* — so **the renderer, not the model, introduced the wrong reading**. That is the same failure class as AI-4's word boundary (a marker quietly editing the answer) and worse in one respect, because the answer still *looks* like an answer. Now `fractionTerm` in `mobile/lib/assistantMarkdown.ts` keeps the brackets on any fraction side that carries an operator, so `\frac{17 + 5}{113}` decodes to `(17 + 5)/113`, while a single term is untouched and `\frac{620}{10,000}` still decodes to `620/10,000` — which is what keeps the rule away from the answers already stored. **The rule covers the operator family (`+ - − × · ÷ /`, plus the LaTeX spellings `\times`/`\cdot`/`\div`/`\pm`/`\mp`) rather than the two signs one screenshot happened to show, and that came from a measurement:** the fraction bar binds tighter than `×` exactly as it does than `+`, so the same code decoded `\frac{1}{2 \times 3}` — **one sixth** — as `1/2 × 3`, **one and a half**. Verification: **11 cases** in the ISS-26 suite (its count unchanged — the assertions grew inside the cases that already existed) covering the real stored formula, `(17+5)/113` unspaced, both-sides-operator `(n - 1)/(2 + k)`, the four multiplicative forms and the single-term re-pins; the narrowed rule was **watched failing first** through a line-based mutator that prints the before and after line and **exits non-zero if the edit did not apply** (a mutation that cannot apply is not evidence), answering `Expected: "1/(2 × 3)" / Received: "1/2 × 3"` — exactly the wrong number — with the file then restored and re-run green; mobile **59 suites / 802 tests / 0 failures** (up from 801), `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean; and the corrected line was **re-rendered on the phone at zero quota**, checked twice on the raw dump and on a 3× crop because the pre-fix reading had already been an OCR artifact once. The same pass then gave AI-4's rendering its **first device look**, and closed the one question §27BH left to a screen — what a **table** looks like on a phone — with **one real turn** (`201`, 17.6 s, `getOutOfStockProducts`), asked directly for a table and answered with a **4-column, 5-row** one: it renders as **one card per row**, the row's own name as the card title and the header's other columns as label/value lines, the header row **not** drawn as a row, **no `|`, no `------`, no `**` anywhere on the screen**, the closing sentence as ordinary prose, and every value present — including the single-character `0`, which was proven **drawn on all five cards** by pixel measurement (267 ink pixels, bbox `428..445`, the identical glyph signature five times). **That turn is also the first device sighting of AI-5's cost line** — `[ai] turn outcome=ok total=17599ms providerMs=17510ms calls=2 toolMs=89ms toolRuns=1 tools=getOutOfStockProducts empty=0 in=3272 out=228`, i.e. **99.5 % provider time against 0.5 % tools** read off a real member's turn, with a clean `calls=2 / empty=0` that contrasts with the two earlier turns in the same account that needed `calls=3` and an AI-2 retry. **Three false findings are recorded rather than buried**, because each would have been written up as a defect: a database probe that reported "no stored table anywhere" while **two silent bugs** (the database name lives in `MONGODB_DB_NAME`, and `role` is uppercase `ASSISTANT`) made it unable to match anything — so a provider turn was spent on a question whose answer was already in the database, where a **6-column, 20-row** table still sits (in another business, and therefore invisible to the signed-in phone); OCR that read the lone `0` as **absent** on all five cards, which is exactly what a dropped last column would look like; and a pixel scan whose window began at `x=460` while the value column begins at **x≈428**, so a glyph that was correctly drawn measured as zero ink three times. The instruments were fixed and re-controlled before any of it was believed, which is how three apparent defects became zero. **Stated limits:** OCR of a screenshot rather than a DOM read, one turn and one table shape (the **grid** path and the 6-column stored table remain unseen on hardware), and *"does it read well"* is still a judgement for the owner rather than for a test. **Limits stated rather than glossed:** the observation rests on OCR of a 1080-px screenshot (the accessibility tree could not be dumped — `uiautomator` will not idle past a blinking caret and the one digest it returned was stale), only one stored answer contains an operator-carrying fraction, so the `×`/`÷`/`·` branches are proven by test and mutation rather than by anything the model has actually written, and `stripMarkdown`'s `**` rule keeps the limit §27BH recorded. Roadmap §27BS.
ISS-27 — The narrow table collapsed, and the screen looked empty (found **on the device** 2026-10-14, minutes after the verification in the entry above; mobile only — **no server, route, permission, schema, dependency or prompt change**): asked for a **two-column** table, the assistant answered correctly (the stored message is a proper 2×5 Markdown table and the API returned it) and the phone showed **nothing at all** — a blank message area, the previous answer gone with it, only a timestamp drawn. The accessibility tree settled it: the answer's `android.widget.TextView` cells measured **48 px wide and up to 1 452 px tall**, so the whole answer sat in a ~200 px column, its height exploded to ~1 600 px, and scroll-to-end pinned the viewport to the bottom of it, pushing the question off the top of the screen. **The pixel scan had reported zero ink** over a 1080 × 1500 band, and that was the instrument being wrong rather than the screen: the collapsed answer draws only the grid's `border` (`#2A2F3B`) on the bubble's `surface` (`#1B1E26`), a luminance difference of about **20**, under the tool's threshold of 40 — at threshold 20 the same cell showed 516 ink pixels. The four-column **card** path verified minutes earlier had escaped the same defect only because a card is titled with the row's own long name, which gives the bubble a wide intrinsic width — **luck, not design** — which is why the fix covers both paths. Now a table-bearing answer takes the full bubble width (`bubbleWide: { flex: 1 }`, still capped by `maxWidth: '84%'`) so a grid's `flex: 1` columns have a width to share, and `isTableAnswer` is an O(n) `includes('|')` — a superset of the parser's own detection, so the keystroke-driven render path pays nothing and a prose reply keeps its natural bubble. **5 cases** in `mobile/__tests__/iss-27-narrow-table-width.test.tsx` using the real stored answer, the ★ case **watched failing first** (`Expected: 1 / Received: undefined`, then restored byte-identical); mobile **60 suites / 807 tests / 0 failures** (up from 59 / 802), `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean. **DEVICE-VERIFIED at zero provider quota**, because it re-renders a stored answer: the tree now measures **464 × 96** and **256 × 96** for the two columns instead of 48 px wide and 1 452 px tall, with all five rows and every `0` on screen and the card answer unchanged. **Stated limits:** **jest cannot see a layout collapse** — every AI-4 case passed while the phone rendered that 48 px column, so the new suite pins the wiring and the values while the layout stays device-only; one screen and one orientation, with only two- and four-column tables measured, so the seven-column card path and landscape remain unmeasured; and the conversation list's timestamp was seen to go stale after a new turn (**not investigated, not fixed**, recorded so it is not rediscovered). Roadmap §27BT.
The Markdown word-boundary rule gets the guard AI-4 never left behind (mobile only; **no dependency, no prompt, no server and no production behaviour change**): AI-4 found and fixed a real defect — `stripMarkdown` had **no word boundary at all**, so it rewrote `4*5*6` as `456` and `A*B*C` as `ABC`, **two characters removed and a quantity silently changed** — and then left one residual **recorded rather than fixed**; neither the fix nor the residual had a rule-level guard, because the AI-2 and AI-4 suites pin the two named invariants (`SKU_123_AB` is data, `12 * 3 = 36` is arithmetic) rather than the rule that produces them. **The first guard written for it was blind to the very defect it was written for:** it compared **alphanumeric content** before and after and **passed while the bug was present**, because `4*5*6` and `456` keep the same digits in the same order — measured by removing the boundary rule and watching the passing property, not reasoned about. The shipped guard compares **runs** instead, where a removed marker **fuses** two alphanumeric runs and the change is visible: **0** violations on the clean tree, and **1,976** strict plus **2,412** styled violations when the rule is removed from its two code sites, with the edit **asserted to have landed by file hash** before the run and the file restored **byte-identically** afterwards. Scoping is measured, not chosen: over the no-backtick corpus (every string to depth 6 over `* _ # 4 a` — **19,530** inputs) a run may never be fused by either path, while with backticks a boundary **can** be fused and every such case is a code span or a styled span adjacent to a run (**1,492** of them, and **0** once backticks leave the corpus) — the styling carries the separation, so the shipped rule is the general form, *a run boundary may disappear only where a styled span carries it*, measured to hold over both corpora (**75,516** inputs). The residual AI-4 accepted (`4**5**6` → `4*5*6`, operators surviving so no quantity is altered) is **pinned** in both directions so it cannot drift silently. 6 cases in `mobile/__tests__/assistant-markdown-invariant.test.ts`; mobile **63 suites / 839 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean. **Limits stated in the file:** it is a property over generated inputs rather than a proof (a small alphabet at depth 6 cannot see a defect needing a longer string), the styled-span exemption is a proxy (styling is present *at* the fusion, not proven to be *the* boundary), and it says nothing about how an answer reads on a screen. Roadmap §27CB.
The section numbering gets the guard every other index already had (docs plus one test file; **no production, dependency, route, schema or permission change**): **two records claimed the same §27 number on two branches** — the server-bumps record and the Markdown guard above — and **nothing failed**, no test and no build; the collision was found by a human reading a **merge conflict**, and resolving it cost a renumbering across three documents and a rewrite of the record's own justification. In the same afternoon a citation was found sending readers to a `27A` record **that has never existed** (the lettered series starts at `27B`; the plan series begins at §27). Both are one failure — the document numbering is an **index** and nothing checked it — so `server/test/phase-docs-section-integrity.test.mjs` (**4 cases**, registered in `run-tests.mjs`'s `PURE_UNIT` list, because an unclassified file makes `npm test` exit **2**) now asserts that **no plan record is numbered twice** and that **every citation resolves**, scanned across the project's own text (**446** files — docs, sources, tests, scripts, workflows). **The scoping was measured rather than chosen:** records use `## 1. …`-style local sub-headings and **four such numbers repeat today** (`1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, three times each), so a guard over "every numbered heading" would report four false positives on a clean tree and be deleted within a week; the `27…` namespace holds **79** records and that is what is checked. **Proved able to fail in both directions:** a second `# 27CB.` record appended → the duplicate assertion fails with `[['27CB', 2]]`, exactly the defect class that cost the renumbering; a citation to an unresolvable record appended → the citation assertion fails naming the file and the id; each restored **byte-identically**, and the corpus floors (**>70** records, **>400** files) come from that measurement rather than a guess. **The guard applies to itself and needs no self-exclusion:** quoting the bad identifier and planting an unresolvable one in the test file made the first run of the finished suite report **four** new dangling citations, so identifiers are written **without their sigil** here and the unresolvable one is **assembled from parts**, leaving the corpus whole (the issue-record guard took the documented single-file exclusion instead). It also corrected the one real dangling citation in the tree — `docs/PHASE_HISTORY.md`, which now points at §27 — and the fix records why the wrong identifier is written there without its sigil. Server **unit mode 790 tests / 180 suites — 753 pass / 0 fail / 37 deliberately skipped** (from 786 / 179 — 749 / 0 / 37), `npm run test:unit` exit **0**. **Limits:** it cannot see a collision before the two records meet (two branches can each claim a number and both be clean; it fires when the second lands, which is earlier than the human who found it by hand but is not prevention), and it says nothing about whether a record is true, sensibly numbered, or whether a citation names the section the sentence meant. Roadmap §27CD.
The role-boundary fixture — four working logins, and the matrix that proves them (2026-10-17; **server only, three scripts plus one guard suite — no route, permission, schema, session, dependency or mobile change**): the owner asked to *"create test accounts for the Admin, Manager and Staff roles yourself"* so each role could be checked from their side, without a six-digit verification code and without weakening real accounts. Every role guard was already proved, but **no command produced a working login**, so "what does STAFF see?" could only be answered by reading code. Now `npm run seed:roles` creates **one isolated tenant** (`ROLE TEST — role boundary fixture (do not use)`, identified by **both** its name and its owner address) holding **exactly one account per role** and ~900 documents; `npm run verify:roles` signs all four in and checks **96 cells** (18 gated routes and 3 authenticated-only routes × 4 roles, plus **12 user-management rules** through the app's own `POST/PATCH /api/users`); `npm run cleanup:roles` removes that tenant, proves **0 remaining**, and keeps the Firebase identities unless `--delete-firebase` (**the F-50-safe direction**). **No code is skipped to create them:** the identities are written by the Admin SDK with `emailVerified: true` — the *same* write AUTH-04's confirm step performs after a real code — the addresses are four literal **`example.invalid`** ones that can never receive mail, the script never enumerates or deletes identities, and **no route, middleware, environment value or schema changed**, so `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED` still applies to every other account. Safety is inherited rather than re-implemented: `assertSafeTarget` refuses production and any non-development database **before a connection is opened** (dry run included), every query and delete is filtered by the one `businessId`, and `audit_logs`/`ai_conversations` — keyed by business but absent from `TENANT_DATA_COLLECTIONS` — are removed and verified by the fixture itself, because a cleanup reporting "0 remaining" over a shorter list than it deleted from is the failure that hides. **Measured 2026-10-17:** a **real Firebase sign-in + exchange** for all four accounts (`--signin`, HTTP 200, correct role from `/api/auth/me`) and **96/96 cells matching `ROLE_PERMISSIONS` exactly**; 900 documents written and re-counted through the same filter the screens use; cleanup removed **914** and proved **0**; a reseed after cleanup reattached the same four identities. **Two findings:** **ADMIN is route-equivalent to OWNER** — the two capabilities it lacks are catalogue-only (Phase 22), so its real restriction lives inside `/api/users` (`ADMIN_RESTRICTED`, `CANNOT_MODIFY_SELF`, `OWNER_IMMUTABLE`) and is checked separately; and `POST /api/users` **sends no mail and needs no code**, so OWNER-provisioned accounts are usable immediately through the legacy password route, having no `firebaseUid`. A first draft of the suite asserted "every role but OWNER is refused a route" and **failed on ADMIN** — the assertion was wrong, and is now written as the property that holds. Guard `server/test/phase27ct-role-fixture.test.mjs`, **25 cases DB-free**, **proved able to fail** (collapsing `cellAgrees` to `() => true` → **5 failures**, restored byte-identically → 25/25). **Limits:** the verifier proves the **server** boundary, not which buttons a screen hides; **no device run** was performed; the matrix is **18 representative routes of 47** (Phase 23/24 remain the exhaustive proofs); and `--signin` needs the web API key and the fixture password in the environment. Roadmap §27CT.

The four-role phone walkthrough — a door with no handle, and a trail that showed its own enums (2026-10-17; **mobile plus one server guard suite — no route, permission, schema, session, dependency, environment or model change, and no model was called**): the owner signed in as OWNER, ADMIN, MANAGER and STAFF and asked for the screens walked the way a person walks them. Two defects came out of it, both **invisible to every existing guard**. **F-61 — STAFF could not reach Profile at all:** `defaultHomePath` sends STAFF to **Inventory** (correct, they do not hold `dashboard.view`) and Profile is reached from a **Dashboard tile**, so the only header affordance on their landing screen was sign out — and with it they could not change their password, review or end their own sessions, or edit their name. Every screen involved was permission-correct; the gap was a missing link between two screens that both worked. Now `mobile/components/ProfileIcon.tsx` sits **beside sign out on the Inventory header for every role** (one icon, no permission conditional), and `mobile/__tests__/profile-reachability.test.tsx` asserts both halves — an accessible `Profile` button that pushes `/profile`, **and** that the Inventory `Stack.Screen` actually renders it; removing the wiring fails the second assertion (mutation-verified, restored). **F-62 — the audit trail printed its own enum names:** the label table lived inside `audit.tsx`, written at Phase 16A and never extended, so four session-lifecycle actions (26A), two password-recovery actions (26B-2) and two Firebase identity actions (32H) rendered as `SESSION_CREATED` / `PASSWORD_RESET_COMPLETED` / `FIREBASE_IDENTITY_LINK_CONFLICT` beside labels like "Stock in", and the `SESSION` badge read `SESSION`. The table now lives in `mobile/lib/auditLabels.json` with the lookups in `mobile/lib/auditLabels.ts`, and `server/test/audit-label-parity.test.mjs` reads **the same file** and fails when an enum has no label, when a label merely repeats its key, when a label is blank or padded, or when the table holds a value the server cannot emit — because adding `SESSION_CREATED: 'SESSION_CREATED'` would satisfy a presence check while leaving the screen exactly as broken. A **Sessions** filter chip was added to the trail's entity row for the same reason it needed a label. **Why JSON rather than TypeScript:** the guard runs under the *server's* project, and importing the mobile `.ts` module pulled it into `server/tsconfig.json`, where `verbatimModuleSyntax` rejected it with four `TS1287`/`TS1295` errors — `npm run typecheck` red for a presentation file; data cannot collide that way and the app imports the same file, so there is still **exactly one table**, with the lookups covered by `mobile/__tests__/audit-labels.test.ts` (unknown stays visible, missing is an em dash). **F-63 — a denied screen still asked the server:** writing the Audit screen's first render test exposed that both `audit.tsx` and `users/index.tsx` checked their permission **after** their hooks, so a MANAGER or STAFF deep link still mounted `usePagedList` and issued `GET /api/audit` / `GET /api/users` to be answered **403** — no data leaked, but the client asked for what it knew it could not have and the server logged a refusal for a screen already denied. MOD-07 fixed this shape on four screens and missed these two because neither *violates* Rules of Hooks. Both now use the same guard-parent/child split as the AI hub (unchanged denied copy), and `screen-rbac-guard.test.tsx` grew the cases that would have caught it — denied renders copy **and** never calls the API, plus an allowed direction so the guard cannot pass by refusing everyone; reverting the Users guard to a no-op produced **2 failures**, restored → 13/13. A **sweep of every gated screen** then found the same shape on two detail screens — `users/[id].tsx` (a MANAGER/STAFF deep link loaded `GET /api/users/:id` before denying) and `purchase-orders/[id]/edit.tsx` — so the class was fixed rather than the two instances, with **4 more guard cases** (denied + allowed for each) bringing that suite to **17**, and both guards proved failing on mutation. **DEVICE-VERIFIED 2026-10-17**, after the fixes, on the OPPO CPH1969 over the USB tunnels (development build, headless §9b recipe), with the role fixture re-seeded (934 documents removed, 900 written and read back equal, four identities refreshed `emailVerified: true`): as `role-staff@example.invalid` the app landed on **Inventory** and the header carried **Tools · Profile · Sign out**, tapping **Profile** opening *Role Test Staff · Staff · role-staff@example.invalid · Active* — the screen that had no route at all before; as `role-owner@example.invalid` the **Audit Log** read *Signed in · Role Test Owner · Session* / *Signed out · Role Test Staff · Session*, the new **Sessions** chip filtered to exactly those rows while **Products** answered *"No product activity yet."*, and the database independently held exactly `SESSION_CREATED ×2` / `SESSION_LOGGED_OUT ×1`; and as STAFF the deep links `/users`, `/users/<id>` and `/audit` each rendered the denied copy with the server log growing by **0 bytes** across all three — measured by byte offset, so the claim is "no request was made", not "no error was seen". One tooling correction is recorded because it produced a wrong reading first: an ad-hoc mongoose script reported **0 audit rows** while the phone showed three, because it connected to the `test` database — `MONGODB_DB_NAME` is applied by the server's own `resolveDatabaseName` and is not in the URI. **Limits:** one build, one device, one tenant, three role/screen combinations; the Sessions chip is proved by list contents rather than by an inspectable query string (`httpLogger` records no query);and the fixture is **left in place** (cleaned with `npm run cleanup:roles`), its password now in gitignored `server/.env` as `ROLE_TEST_PASSWORD`.

The MANAGER walk — the writes checked against the ledger, and two screens that agree (2026-10-17; **no code changed — a device, a database and the ledger, so no suite was re-run for it**): MANAGER is the role whose daily job is writing (`inventory.manage`, `products.manage`, `suppliers.manage`, `purchase_orders.manage`, `dashboard.view`, `ai.use`; refused `users.manage`/`audit.view`/`categories.manage`/`locations.manage`). **Stock In** on *Everyday Battery 10202* at *Shop Front*, quantity **5**, reference `QA-MANAGER-WALK`: the app said *"33 → 38"*, and the database agrees — Shop Front **38**, Main Warehouse untouched, `inventory_transactions` **366 → 367** with one `STOCK_IN` row, and an `INVENTORY_STOCK_IN` audit row holding `previousQuantity: 33` / `newQuantity: 38`. **Transfer** of the same product *Shop Front → Main Warehouse*, quantity **1**: the app said *"38 → 37 / 32 → 33"*, and the database holds the **two legs** — one `TRANSFER` row per location, each with its own `locationId` and previous/new pair, **one shared automatic reference** — plus an `INVENTORY_TRANSFERRED` audit row carrying both sides and both transaction ids. **Same-location transfer** is refused in three places: the button is disabled with *"Choose two different locations…"*, the validator rejects it, and the service answers `409 TRANSFER_SAME_LOCATION`. **Two screens agree on data that moved under them:** the dashboard read **168 / 35 / 11 / 203 / 46 607** and the restock list **"35 products at or below minimum stock"**, both equal to an independent recomputation (per product, summed availability across locations, `<=` minimum → **35 low / 11 out of 203**) and to the dashboard service called in-process; the dashboard's earlier **36** was not a discrepancy at all — the stock-in had lifted a product from **exactly** its minimum (available 65, `minimumStock` 65, so `65 <= 65` was low) to 70, and the ledger showed exactly three transactions after that reading, both of them mine. The §19 boundary is asserted in `SCALE-dashboard-counts.test.mjs`; this is it on live data. **The mirror, seen not asserted:** Categories and Locations render **read-only** for MANAGER (no create/edit affordance — mutations are gated, reads are open by design), the workspace grid withholds Users and Audit, and `/users`, `/users/:id`, `/audit` refuse with the denied copy and **no request** (F-63 re-confirmed for a third role). **Limits:** one device, one build, one tenant, one role; PO create/issue/receive, adjust, stock out, product create/edit and the assistant were **not** exercised as MANAGER (their routes are covered by the §27CT matrix — what is unverified there is their screens, not their permissions); the destination picker still offers the source location, which is a banner plus a disabled button rather than an impossible choice — **recorded as an observation, not implemented**; and the QA writes are left in the fixture (`npm run seed:roles` resets it, `npm run cleanup:roles` removes it). Roadmap §27CV.
 Verified: mobile **70 suites / 900 tests**, `tsc`/`eslint` clean; server **1 497 tests / 223 suites / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint`/`check:extensions`/`check:specifiers` clean; the parity guard **proved able to fail** on the real table (deleting `SESSION_CREATED` and de-humanising `INVENTORY_ADJUSTED` → **4 failures**, restored to 31 actions / 9 entity types), and the reachability guard likewise (after a first mutation attempt that silently did not apply — CRLF — and was therefore not evidence). **Limits:** no device run after either fix — the missing Profile was *found* on the phone, but the added icon, the new chip and the corrected labels have not been seen on a screen, and "does it read well" is still hardware's judgement; the parity guard covers the **label** table and cannot know that a new action is worth showing at all. Roadmap §27CU.
The purchase-order write chain as MANAGER — create, issue, receive, and the ledger after each step (2026-10-18; **no code changed — a device, a database and the ledger, so no suite was re-run for it**): §27CV walked this role's screens and named what it had **not** exercised; this closes all of that list but `product create/edit`. One product (*Everyday Battery 10202*), one fixture tenant, and **every step checked against the database rather than the screen that reported it** — **PO-0002** created as a DRAFT, issued, then received **2 of 3** and **1 of 3** in two calls. The screen and the store agree at each step: *"Received 2 unit(s) across 1 line."* with **no** "fully received" clause and status still `OPEN`, then *"…This order is now fully received."* with status `RECEIVED`, **two embedded receipts** (`prev 37 → new 39`, then `39 → 40` with their own transaction ids), `committed 3`, and **no leftover claims**. Stock out (`40 → 39`) and adjust (`39 → 41`) followed, each with its own `STOCK_OUT` / `ADJUSTMENT` transaction and `INVENTORY_STOCK_OUT` / `INVENTORY_ADJUSTED` audit row — so **the ledger is continuous across all five movements (37 → 39 → 40 → 39 → 41)**, each row's `previousQuantity` equal to what the step before it left. Both receives write the **same** audit action and are told apart by metadata (`status`, line list), and the form's own reads matched the database at the instant it was used (**AVAILABLE NOW 40**, **CURRENT ON HAND 39**). Terminal states seen only after a real completion: the list's *3 of 3 received · complete*, the detail's **RECEIVED** badge with a two-entry receiving history and the Receive button **replaced** by *"This order is fully received."*, and the receive route answering *"This order is already fully received."* with no form. The dashboard moved by exactly the net — **46,611 → 46,614 → 46,613 → 46,615** — with the four status counts unmoved. One observation recorded rather than changed: after creating a draft the alert dismisses to the screen **underneath the form**, not to the new order. **Limits:** one device, one build, one tenant, one role; `product create/edit` remains unexercised for MANAGER; the QA writes are left in the fixture (`npm run cleanup:roles` removes it). Roadmap §27CX.
The port now proves which process answered — the stale-server trap made impossible instead of documented (2026-10-18; **server only — two new modules, one middleware line, one boot guard, one error handler, one guard suite; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency, environment value or mobile change, and no model was called**): §27CW ended by documenting the trap — four `node src/index.ts` processes alive, the port's owner not the newest one, an `OPENROUTER_BASE_URL` override aimed at a stub looking ignored while four provider requests reached the deployed free tier — and the device guide's remedy was *find the PID*, which depends on remembering to look. **A readiness probe could never have caught it**, because the old server is healthy and writes the same `{"status":"ready"}`: the missing fact was **identity**, not state. So no new logging was added; an identity was. `config/instanceIdentity.ts` creates **one `randomUUID()` per process** and `app.ts` stamps it on **every** response as `x-instance-id` (before any route, so errors and 404s carry it too), and `config/portGuard.ts` makes a boot **ask the port it just bound to name itself** against `/api/health/live` — the one endpoint that never touches MongoDB, so the probe can neither be slow nor fail with the database. Three outcomes, only one fatal: **this process's id** → silent; **a different id** → log the foreign id plus the `netstat`/`lsof` command and **exit 1**, because a process that is listening but not serving is worse than one that refused to start; **unreachable or no id** → say so and **continue**, since a loopback probe can fail for reasons that are not the trap and refusing to boot on an inconclusive answer would break real deployments to catch a hypothetical one. The probe is fired **unawaited** after the socket is listening, and the boot line now carries the same id, so the line an operator just saw can be compared with the header a client is getting. A bind that genuinely fails is now **one line naming the port and the `netstat` command** instead of an unhandled `error` event and a stack trace; **`EADDRINUSE` is fatal whenever it arrives**, while every *other* code is fatal only before listening — a rule the first draft got wrong, and the correction is measured below rather than argued. **Measured with two real boots, not a stub — and the second one found a defect in this increment.** Run 1, `createApp()` twice through the real `startServer` on 4599: the first server kept answering with **its own** id, the second **exited 1** with `port 4599 is already in use — refusing to start …`, the first untouched. Run 2, two real `node src/index.ts` on one port: the second printed `listening on http://localhost:4011 instance=521c…` — **its listening callback really fired** — and `EADDRINUSE` arrived **afterwards**, so the first draft's rule (*an error after listening is logged, not fatal, because killing a serving process would be its own defect*) left a process with **no working listener** alive for **30 s**. The guard did not catch it either: after that error the socket is gone, `address()` returns null, and the port was never asked — a boot that cannot report its address is exactly the boot that most needs the fatal path. Fixed: **`EADDRINUSE` is decisive on its own**, before or after the listening callback, since a port that cannot be taken is never a normal runtime event, while every **other** code stays non-fatal once listening; re-measured on the same port, the second boot now **exits 1** immediately. That is also why the guard **probes** instead of branching on an error code: this host refused a second bind *before* listening in one run and accepted it and failed *afterwards* in the other, on the same afternoon. A real single boot (real `.env`) was checked too — the guard stayed **silent**, `x-instance-id: bfce8573-…` matched the boot line, and it verified **while readiness was `not ready`** (MongoDB unreachable, the known Atlas timeout), which is what `/api/health/live` was chosen for. Guard `server/test/port-ownership-guard.test.mjs`, **13 cases** — both error orderings pinned, the fakes rebuilt on `EventEmitter` because a fake whose `on` is a no-op can never emit — with the probe exercised against **real loopback HTTP servers** (both identity outcomes and the closed-port failure are not mocked) and `startServer` pinned to probe the **bound** port, to skip a server with no address, and to still return its handles when a foreign answer exits; **proved able to fail** — removing the foreign branch's `exit?.(1)` reports **2 failures**, one per layer, and the file was restored **byte-identically** (`diff` + a `grep` proving the mutation text is gone). **The fixture had a defect and it was found by the fixture hanging**: the fake server's `close()` never called its callback, so the shutdown drain awaited a promise nothing would settle — fixed in the fixture, with the reason written into the file, because a fake that swallows a callback turns *"the suite is green"* into *"the suite never ran". Server **unit 884 tests / 198 suites — 844 pass / 0 fail / 40 skipped** (from 871 / 197) and **db 721 / 132 — 656 pass / 0 fail / 65 skipped**, both exit 0; `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean; no mobile file changed, its baseline **70 suites / 904 tests** green. **Limits:** the probe sees IPv4 loopback only, so a stale IPv6-only process could stay invisible; it is a **boot** check, not a poll; the header is **not authentication** and nothing authorizes on it; and the live run is one host on one day. Roadmap §27CY.
Where a new draft lands — and the first test the purchase-order form has ever had (2026-10-18; **mobile only — one navigation branch in `PoForm.submit()` plus one new suite; no server, route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment change**): §27CX created a draft through `inventoryai://purchase-orders/new` and watched the alert dismiss to the **AI hub**, with the order just made nowhere on screen. The cause is one line used for both verbs — `submit()` ended in `router.back()`. For **edit** that is exactly right (the member came from the detail and returns to it, which refetches on focus); for **create** the destination is whatever is underneath, and under a deep link that is whatever screen the link was opened over. The write was correct every time; only the destination was arbitrary. Now a successful create **replaces** the form with `/purchase-orders/<id>` — the id the server already returns with its 201 — so the alert's own advice (*"issue it when you're ready"*) is one tap away and back from a new draft goes to the list rather than to a second copy of the form; edit still pops, and a response with no id still pops as before, because the old behaviour is the safe fallback. **New suite `mobile/__tests__/po-form-create-navigation.test.tsx`, 6 cases** — create→detail with the exact payload, edit→back, no-id fallback, the client-side empty-form refusal before any request, a server field error landing on the field it names, and a server code error becoming its sentence with the code itself absent — and this is **the first machine test `PoForm` has ever had**, on the write path §27CX had only walked by hand. **★ watched failing first:** reverting the create branch to the single `back()` fails **1 of 6**, the ★ case and only it, restored byte-identically. **One finding worth keeping:** the suite's first draft tested nothing and its symptom was a *missing element* — every press was written as `fireEvent.press(...)` without `await`, and in RNTL 14 that call **returns a promise that resolves after the handler's updates flush**, so each press was still pending when the next line read the tree; it failed as *"Unable to find an element with text: Choose a product…"*, which reads like a missing control and is a race, and the same trap failed five of six cases in a full run while each passed alone. Mobile **71 suites / 910 tests / 0 failures** (from 70 / 904), `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean; the server is untouched and its baseline stands at unit **884 / 198 / 0**, db **721 / 132 / 0**. **Limits:** the destination is pinned by mock and has **not** been re-seen on the phone since the change; the same `back()` shape exists on the other create forms (movement, transfer, product) and was deliberately **not** audited, because several of those are meant to return to a list the member was looking at and each needs its own judgement rather than a blanket change. Roadmap §27CZ.
An escaped pipe made data, and a tilde removed — two parser defects found by probing (2026-10-18; **mobile plus the extracted library; no server, route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment change, and no prompt change**): both came from **running the parser on realistic model output** — a probe over eleven shapes a model writes — rather than reading it, which three earlier increments had done without finding either. **`\|` inside a table cell was a column boundary:** `| A \| B | 3 |` is Markdown's escape for a pipe in a cell, and `splitCells` produced three cells (`A`, `B`, `3`), so the row had one more column than its header and every later value sat under the wrong one — worse than untidy, because a table is the one structure where a shifted value is *wrong*: the number read under **Qty** becomes part of a product name while the row still looks normal. The escaped pipe is now protected by a sentinel while the row splits and restored inside the cell, and a trailing `|` is no longer dropped when it is itself escaped; ordinary tables are untouched, pinned by a control case. **`~~strikethrough~~` reached the screen as tildes**, because no rule had ever been written for it — the one thing the residual pass exists to prevent — and it now follows the **same word boundary as every other marker**, so `a~~b~~c` stays data exactly as `SKU_123_AB` does. New suite `mobile/__tests__/assistant-parser-edges.test.ts`, **7 cases** (escaped pipe with alignment, trailing escaped pipe, ordinary-table control, ★ paired tildes, tildes in a word, a lone `~5 minutes`, and strikethrough inside a **cell**, where the two rules meet). **Each fix was reverted separately and its own cases failed** — naive splitter **2 of 7**, strikethrough rule removed **2 of 7** — restored byte-identically each time; the first strikethrough mutation silently did not apply and the suite passed, which is why both mutations now assert they landed before the run is trusted. Mobile **72 suites / 917 tests / 0 failures** (from 71 / 910), `tsc`/`eslint` clean, and the AI-2/AI-4/invariant suites that pin the no-marker property all still pass unchanged. The same fix is published in the extracted **`assistant-markdown`** library (two matching cases, 13 pass, typecheck clean, CI green on Node 22 and 24, PR #2). **Limits, recorded not fixed:** links and images still leak in the plain-text path (`stripMarkdown('[Product A](…)')` is unchanged, and a link rule needs its own decision about the URL); `4**5**6` still becomes `4*5*6`, the residual AI-4 recorded on purpose because operators survive; the escaped-pipe cell is reasoned from the renderer rather than re-seen on a phone; and the app module and the library copy are **two files kept in step by hand**, with no guard, because the app deliberately does not depend on the published package. Roadmap §27DA.
The whole stored corpus replayed — and the one leak it found (2026-10-18; **mobile plus the extracted library, PR #3 there; no server, route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment change, and no prompt change**): §27DA found two defects by *writing* realistic samples, so this increment asked the opposite question — **what does the assistant actually write?** — and replayed **all 50 assistant answers stored in the database** through the renderer, flattening every block back into the text a member would read. **The census decided what not to build as much as what to fix:** tables in **13** answers, bold in **23**, bullets in **11**, numbered lists in **6**, a `#` heading in **1**, `\( … \)` in **1**, inline code in **1** — all already rendered — while **links and images (`[`) occurred in ZERO answers, as did blockquotes, HTML tags, fenced code, `---` rules, `$$` and checkboxes**, so the link rule §27DA left open was **not written**: it would have been code for a shape the model does not produce. **The one leak in real output was a truncated answer's dangling opener**, in the **two** turns that hit their output budget mid-marker — `… (the system has **71,07` and a numbered `11. **2`, so the last thing on screen was a pair of asterisks. `stripMarkdown` now drops a marker run that opens and is never closed (both §27DA's defect class and this one come from the same place: a marker the renderer does not model reaching the screen), under **three conditions that keep it away from data** — a **word boundary before it** (`4**5` between digits is untouched), **a non-space after it** (`** a` is not half-cleaned, because an opener has content after it), and **no later run of the same marker**, which is what makes it safe: in well-formed markup every opener has a closer later and a closer is preceded by a word character, so the only run it can match is a genuinely unpaired one. Only the run goes, never the text — the truncated answer still says `71,07` — and the single `*` is deliberately **not** in the list, because `12 * 3 = 36` must keep its multiplication. **New suite `mobile/__tests__/assistant-real-answers.test.ts`, 6 cases over a committed fixture of 8 verbatim stored answers** (wide table, numbered list, truncated table answer, heading, LaTeX, bullets+bold, longest prose, and a short non-English answer), whose centre is a **property over all eight at once** — no marker of any kind reaches the rendered text — plus a guard-on-the-guard that the fixture still covers those eight reasons, because a property that passes over a trimmed corpus proves nothing; it is a property rather than a snapshot so a Markdown change that is an improvement passes while a marker on screen or a lost value fails. `assistant-parser-edges.test.ts` gained **6 cases** for the rule itself (the two measured strings, a dangling `~~` and an unclosed code span, the arithmetic/identifier controls, and the `** a` case). **The fix was removed and watched failing twice** — edges suite **4 of 6** new cases, corpus suite **2 of 6** (the ★ property and the truncation case) — restored **byte-identically** (`cmp`). Mobile **73 suites / 929 tests / 0 failures** (from 72 / 917), `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean; library **PR #3** merged with `npm test` **15 pass**, typecheck clean, CI green on Node 22 and 24, and the rule's declaration verified **identical in both files** (`diff`). **Limits, recorded not fixed:** a dangling unpaired **single** `*` still reaches the screen in a truncated answer, which is the price of keeping `12 * 3 = 36` correct; `** a **` with spaces inside is left as-is by the `(?=\S)` condition; the cleaned tails have **not** been re-seen on a device; the corpus is **50 answers from seeded demo tenants**, real model output but a small and unusual population; the replay is not committed as 50 blobs (8 are verbatim, the rest were measured once); and the app module and the library copy are still **two files kept in step by hand**. Roadmap §27DB.
The tool result the model reads, capped without breaking it (2026-10-18; **server only — one new pure module, one line in `runAiChat`, one new suite; no route, permission, schema, session, index, dependency, mobile or environment change, and no model was called**): found by **probing**, like the two increments before it — the question nothing had asked was *at each tool's own maximum legal `limit`, how big is the payload, and is it still JSON when the assistant's cap is applied?* On the 50 000-product perf tenant, **five of the nine tools exceed the 12 000-character cap at the maxima their own schemas advertise**: `getInventoryHistory` **24 834** chars, `getReorderNeededProducts` **15 550**, `getProductsByLocation` **14 207**, `getLowStockProducts` **14 109**, `getOutOfStockProducts` **14 082** — and `runAiChat` capped them with `truncate(JSON.stringify(output), max)`, a **slice of a JSON document plus an ellipsis**, so the model was being handed **syntactically invalid JSON** and the probe's `JSON.parse` of the same slice threw on all five. Those are not exotic arguments — they are the limits the tool schemas themselves advertise, so *"show me the last 50 movements"* reached the defect with no misbehaviour anywhere — and a model handed half a payload has to guess at the other half, which is the worst input for an assistant whose one job is reporting exact numbers (prompt rule 3 cannot help: it describes a *short, complete* page, not a *broken* document). `server/src/services/ai/toolPayload.ts` now caps **structurally**: a result that fits is returned **byte-identical** to `JSON.stringify(output)` (asserted, because this must be invisible on an ordinary turn), and an oversized one drops rows **from the end of the longest array** until it fits, adding `truncated: true` and a note naming **how many of how many rows** are included — a **prefix** rather than a sample (every one of these tools is newest-first or worst-first), with `total`/`page` and every other non-array field kept so the model can still say *"41 of 7 717"*. A result too wide to shorten row by row (one enormous row, a bare array, a bare string) becomes a labelled **`preview`** envelope, a **string**, so the JSON stays valid — and that path was itself buggy in the first draft, because escaped JSON text grows faster than the slice it came from (`"` → `\"`) so a budget computed by subtraction overflows; the suite's first run caught it as *`Unterminated string in JSON at position 12000`* and it is now an exact search on slice length. **After the fix, the same probe on the same tenant:** all nine payloads **valid JSON** at or below 12 000, the five oversized ones marked `TRUNCATED` keeping **23–41 of 50 rows**, and the four that fit passed through byte-identically. New suite `server/test/AI13-tool-payload-cap.test.mjs`, **10 cases / 4 suites**, registered in `run-tests.mjs`'s `PURE_UNIT` list, using **the probe's real sizes** (the 24 834-character payload, 50 rows) rather than invented ones: byte-identical passthrough and the inclusive boundary; ★ the measured payload becoming parseable **with the old cut asserted to fail**; the kept rows being a **prefix and whole**; `total`/`page` surviving; ★ the **largest** prefix kept (one more row would not fit); the longest array being the one shortened; ★ one enormous row becoming a labelled preview; a bare array, a bare string and a cap smaller than the envelope; and ★ a **source guard** proving `runAiChat` caps through the module, with a positive control that the same scan finds the old call when it is put back. `tsc`/`eslint`/`check:extensions` (412 modules)/`check:specifiers` (1 974 specifiers, 0 stale) clean. **The same probe produced a second finding, recorded as ISS-31 and NOT fixed:** `getInventory` took **25 346 ms**, and following it showed `q`/`status` force `inventory.listRows` down its **filtered** path — the product `$lookup` once per inventory row before the page window — measured through the service the **Inventory screen itself calls**: **no filter 922 ms**, `q` "a" **25 234 ms**, `q` "pro" **19 088 ms**, `q` "Professional Towel" **14 812 ms** (499 rows), `status` ACTIVE **28 950 ms**; the 499-row case is the tell, because the cost is the **partition**, not the result. That is a member-facing 15–29 s search on the main catalogue screen, the same class AI-3 fixed for the tools, and it needs its own increment (resolve matching product ids first, page the inventory index, count separately, proved output-identical with `perf:golden`) because the ordering contract and the total must survive the rewrite. **Limits:** the kept prefix is rows not characters, so a page of individually huge rows keeps very few and the note says so; the cap is still per tool result rather than per turn; **no model was called**, so how a model reads the new `truncated` note is unverified; and ISS-31 means the Inventory search is still slow, stated plainly rather than implied by a green suite. Roadmap §27DC.
The filtered inventory page, resolved product-first — 15–29 s to 0.5–1.6 s (2026-10-18; **server only — one branch of `inventory.listRows` plus two DB cases; no route, permission, schema, index, dependency, mobile or environment change, and no model was called**): closes the performance half of **ISS-31**, which the previous increment found by following a 25 s `getInventory` tool call to its source — `q` and `status` forced the filtered path, which ran the product `$lookup` **once per inventory row** before the page window. Measured through the service the **Inventory screen itself calls** on the 50 000-product tenant (100 143 inventory rows, `skip: 0, limit: 25`): **no filter 922 → 693 ms**, **`q` "a" 25 234 → 1 561 ms**, **`q` "pro" 19 088 → 758 ms**, **`q` "Professional Towel" 14 812 → 716 ms** (499 rows), **`status` ACTIVE 28 950 → 1 019 ms**, **`q` + ACTIVE 17 516 → 760 ms**, **page 2 28 521 → 831 ms** — and through the assistant's own tool, which is the same code: **`getInventory {q:"a"} 25 346 → 1 342 ms`**, `{status:ACTIVE}` **877 ms**, `{q:"pro"}` **695 ms**, unfiltered **183 ms** — **every total unchanged**. When the filter is on the **product's own fields**, the matching products are now resolved in **one** query and the page is an index read over `productId`, joined only for the rows actually returned — AI-3's shape, applied to the one path it left behind. The order is applied before `$skip`/`$limit` exactly as before, so a page boundary lands on the same row; the `$in` changes which rows *can* appear, not how they are ordered; the total is a `countDocuments` over the **same** scope, so it pays for no joins. **`lowStock`/`outOfStock` are deliberately NOT on this path** — they compare a row's availability with its product's `minimumStock`, a per-row joined value, so the join is inherent; they stay ~19 s at this size, stated beside the branch, and the assistant's low-stock questions use the AI-3 tools (1.1–1.3 s at this size). **Proven output-identical:** `perf:golden` **17/17 byte-for-byte** (payload hash + total) before and after, including the four `getInventory` shapes this moves and the two it does not; and **a prototype was compared against the old implementation before the service was edited** — same rows, same order, same totals on six shapes including a page 2. `phase20-pagination-order.test.mjs` gained **two cases** over the real route: the **three-step ordering/paging contract** now also runs for `status=ACTIVE` **and** `q` (distinct sort values, one shared value falling back to `_id`, paging at limit=1 and 2 reproducing the single-page order), because the `_id` tiebreaker a transfer relies on is exactly what a reordered pipeline could break; and a **selective search** returning only its own rows, a total that agrees, and an empty page with a zero total for a term matching nothing — **13 / 13 pass**. **The guard was proved able to fail:** dropping the `$in` narrowing from the page scope fails the selective-search case (**12 / 13**), file restored **byte-identically** (`cmp`) — the failure a pre-resolved id set built from the wrong scope would produce. `tsc`/`eslint` clean; server **1522 tests / 228 suites / 0 failures** (from 1520 / 228). **Limits:** `lowStock`/`outOfStock` on this path are still ~19 s; the **product scan is now the largest term** of a filtered page (**0.4–0.8 s** at 50 000, because an unanchored case-insensitive `q` cannot use an index — one scan instead of 100 143 joined lookups, but not free and linear in the catalogue); the id list is bounded by BSON (~600 KB of query at 50 000) and a catalogue an order of magnitude larger has not been measured. **DEVICE-VERIFIED 2026-10-18**, on the OPPO CPH1969 over the USB tunnels with a Metro-launched development build against a restarted local backend: signing in to the 50 000-product perf tenant as a throwaway MANAGER (removed afterwards, its script deleted), the Inventory screen's own search typed `pro` and `GET /api/inventory` answered **200 in 996.7 ms** (the unfiltered load before it 267 ms) with the row rendered as *"Professional Kettle 50984, Shop Front, 176 available · 176 on hand"* — the request that used to cost ~19 s on this tenant; the debounce was also confirmed in practice, the first request arriving with the **third** character. Roadmap §27DD.
The join itself was the cost — 9× from a `$lookup` shape (2026-10-18; **server only — two lookups in `inventory.listRows` plus one isolation case; no route, permission, schema, index, dependency, mobile or environment change, and no model was called**): §27DD left `lowStock`/`outOfStock` at **~20 s** because they compare a row's availability with its product's `minimumStock`, which needs the join — and a probe that ran the same full-partition pipeline twice, changing only **how** the product is joined, showed the join was never the problem: **`$lookup` matched with `$expr` cost 19 965 ms on the 50 000-product partition and the simple `localField`/`foreignField` form cost 2 166 ms** — same rows, same order. The `$expr` form is a nested loop with no batching (one index lookup *per row*, so the cost multiplies with the partition); the simple form is index-backed and batched. `productLookup` and `locationLookup` are now the simple form in **all three** paths, and **tenant isolation moved rather than weakened**: the simple form resolves by `_id` alone, so a `$filter` beside each lookup drops any joined document whose `businessId` is not this business, before the unwind and before any DTO — the same guarantee as the old `$expr` match, in a different place. **After:** `lowStock` **20 361 → 3 801 ms**, `outOfStock` **2 612 ms**, `q`+`lowStock` **2 554 ms**; the paged shapes (`q` "a" 1 533 ms, `status` ACTIVE 906 ms) are unchanged within noise, and every total is identical (25 419 / 6 396 / 4 371). **`perf:golden` 17/17 byte-for-byte** again, including the two shapes this changes; `phase20-pagination-order` **13/13** re-run after the change; and `phase04-inventory.test.mjs` gained **one isolation case** that writes a row **directly** with the owner's `businessId` but **another business's** `productId`/`locationId` (the application cannot create one) and asserts it exposes nothing, with the foreign product's name absent from the whole response — **19/19**. **The guard was proved able to fail:** the `$filter` condition set to `true` fails exactly that case (**18/19**), file restored **byte-identically** (`cmp`). `tsc`/`eslint` clean; server **1523 tests / 228 suites / 0 failures** (from 1522 / 228). **Limits:** `lowStock` is still **3.8 s** at 50 000 because the predicate needs every row's availability and its product's minimum — a full-partition pass now, not a per-row fan-out; the assistant's dedicated `getLowStockProducts`/`getReorderNeededProducts` remain 1.1–1.3 s and are what the prompt steers to; the **same `$expr` lookup form exists in the tool pipelines** and was deliberately **not** swept (those tools are 0.1–1.3 s, so the upside is small and the surface wide — named as the next place the 9× might be available). **The same device run produced a real assistant turn on the phone** — *"How much Professional Towel stock do we have, and where?"* — `[ai] turn outcome=ok total=12035ms providerMs=11719ms calls=2 toolMs=316ms toolRuns=1 tools=searchProducts empty=0 iterations=2`, rendered as a disambiguation question with the read-only tool chip, no marker on screen and no degraded mark; the `lowStock` shape was **not** driven from the phone, because the model chose `searchProducts`. Roadmap §27DE.
Five tools broke at 131 000 products — the `$group` payload, not the row count (2026-10-18; **server only — the availability merge in `product.service.ts`, two branches of `inventory.listRows`, one new DB-free guard suite and the runner's classification; no route, permission, schema, index, dependency, mobile or environment change, and no model was called**): the owner asked for stress testing, so the assistant was run against a **131 000-product tenant (261 918 inventory rows)** — where **five tools did not work at all**. `getLowStockProducts`, `getOutOfStockProducts`, `getReorderNeededProducts` and `getProductsByLocation` answered `TOOL_ERROR`, and `GET /api/products?lowStock=true`, the **Restock** screen's own service method, answered 500, with MongoDB's sentence in the log: `Exceeded memory limit for $group, but didn't allow external sort. Pass allowDiskUse:true to opt in.` **The mechanism, and why size never looked like the problem:** AI-3 replaced a per-product `$lookup` fan-out with ONE `$group` over the union of the catalogue and its inventory rows, whose key is the product — so there is one group entry per product, and **`$group` memory is memory PER GROUP**; the first version carried the **product document itself** through it (`__product: '$$ROOT'`), i.e. the whole catalogue in RAM, so a few hundred bytes × 131 000 crossed the 100 MB limit and nothing smaller ever noticed. The merge now publishes **four scalars** (`__product` as a `{ $literal: true }` presence marker — it is what still drops the groups the catalogue `$match` excluded — plus `minimumStock`, `createdAt` and the two inventory sums) and the page, which is at most `limit` rows by construction, is **hydrated afterwards** (`hydrateStockPage`: one indexed `Product.find` for the page's ids, one `Category.find` for their distinct references, both tenant-guarded, with `available`/`hasRow` merged back and the same `category`/`categoryId` normalisation the removed `$lookup` performed); both paged aggregates also pass **`allowDiskUse: true`** as a backstop rather than a mechanism. **The four tools now answer in 2.0–2.2 s** (from `TOOL_ERROR`) and `perf:golden` is **17/17 byte-identical** — payload hash and total, across **three** captures (before, after the merge, after the inventory change). **Two row-local facts the same measurement produced, both fixed and both measured:** `outOfStock` is `available <= 0`, computed from the row alone, and had been routed through the **per-row product join** only because it sits beside `lowStock` in one request — that join is ~55 µs per row, so a 15-row page cost **5 364 ms**, and the same predicate with no join costs **603 ms**; and `lowStock`, which genuinely needs each row's product's `minimumStock`, now **prefilters against the largest `minimumStock` in the business** above the join — exact rather than a heuristic, since a row above the maximum threshold cannot satisfy the comparison for any product — keeping **147 225 of 261 918 rows** out of it (**11 286 → 4 843 ms**). The location join in that path also moved **below the page window** into the facet's data branch, where it cannot change the result set because it augments and `preserveNullAndEmptyArrays`-unwinds. **Proven without changing an answer:** `perf:golden` 17/17 byte-identical; `phase04-inventory.test.mjs`'s own filter assertions (lowStock total 3 with its three ids, outOfStock total 1 with both flags true) pass on the new path; `phase20-pagination-order`'s ordering contract for the same routes passes; `tsc`/`eslint` clean. **New guard `server/test/scale-stock-query-shape.test.mjs`, 6 DB-free cases** (registered in `run-tests.mjs`'s `PURE_UNIT` list, because an unclassified file makes `npm test` exit 2) pinning the merge's presence marker and its **lack of `$$ROOT`**, the post-group hydration, both `allowDiskUse` opt-ins, `outOfStock`'s absence from the join-routing expression and the `lowStock` threshold prefilter — **each checker with an in-memory positive control**, plus a control proving **every mutation actually applied**, because a guard proved by a mutation that missed its target proves nothing. **Scaled again to 523 918 rows (131 000 products × 6 locations): every tool answered, no errors anywhere** — the four former failures 2.9–3.3 s, `{outOfStock}` **869 ms** (flat at twice the rows, which is what the row-local predicate buys), `{lowStock}` **8 873 ms**, `{locationId}` **2 395 ms**, summary 2 548 ms, history 161 ms — and that run produced **two findings recorded rather than fixed**: `lowStock` **grows with the partition** (4.8 s at 262 000 rows, 8.9 s at 523 918) because it must read every row's availability against its product's threshold, and the **location-filtered page** took 2 395 ms with `explain` showing `optimizationTimeMillis: 2052` and a blocking `SORT` over 131 000 rows — **plan-choice instability, not a regression** (the same query measured 98–125 ms earlier that day, and 2 030 vs 2 091 ms with and without `allowDiskUse`), whose named next step is a dedicated `{ businessId, locationId, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1 }` index. **The cluster ceiling is a real finding for the owner:** the growth filled the Atlas free tier — the next write was refused with `you are over your space quota, using 520 MB of 512 MB. Writes are blocked on your cluster.` The scratch tenant (**872 004 documents**: 523 918 inventory rows, 216 106 transactions, 131 000 products, 800 purchase orders, its audit trail, conversations, sessions, users and business row) was removed the same hour, leaving **0 documents** and restoring writes (a probe insert then succeeded; `storageSize` 74.8 MB / `dataSize` 125.1 MB / `indexSize` 328.0 MB after). **No production, acceptance or real tenant was touched at any point.** Testing at **millions** of inventory rows needs a larger or separate test cluster — the 512 MB tier cannot hold the data plus the indexes — and that is recorded as a blocker rather than worked around. **DEVICE-VERIFIED 2026-10-18** on the OPPO CPH1969 over the USB tunnels with a Metro-launched development build against a restarted local backend (its boot line's instance id checked against the port's owner first), signed in on the **50 000-product perf tenant** as a throwaway MANAGER (removed afterwards, its script deleted, `adb reverse` cleared, both servers stopped): the **Restock screen** answered `GET /api/products/reorder` **200 in 1 173 ms** rendering *7,585 products at or below minimum stock, most urgent first* with rows like *Value Torch 50999 · 0 available · minimum 88 · Out* (the id-space merge plus hydration); the **Inventory screen's Low stock filter** answered `GET /api/inventory` **200 in 1 970 ms** against 385.7 ms unfiltered (the threshold prefilter path); and **one real assistant turn** from the hub's own prompt *"Which products are low on stock?"* returned `POST /api/ai/chat` **201 in 23 849 ms** with `[ai] turn outcome=degraded total=23287ms providerMs=22320ms calls=2 toolMs=967ms toolRuns=1 tools=getLowStockProducts empty=0 in=5928 out=800 iterations=2`, the read-only chip **Read: getLowStockProducts** on screen — the tool that answered `TOOL_ERROR` at 131 000 products before this change — and the answer drawn as product cards, the `degraded=OUTPUT_LIMIT` mark being the free tier's 800-token cap with AI-8's shipped *"This answer hit the length limit"* copy and its **Open the full restock list** action rather than a defect of this increment. **Limits:** `lowStock` is still a full-partition pass; the location-filtered page's plan instability is described with its measurement but unfixed; the huge tenant no longer exists (the numbers were measured before its removal to restore the cluster's writes) so the device evidence is the 50 000-product tenant, while the 131 000 / 523 918-row evidence is service and tool measurement. Roadmap §27DF.
The location page's plan made unambiguous — an index for a defect that cannot be re-measured (2026-10-18; **server only — one declared index, one explicit index guard and a model comment; no route, permission, schema, dependency, mobile or environment change, and no model was called**): §27DF measured the location-filtered inventory page at **2 395 ms** on the 523 918-row tenant and named a dedicated `{ businessId, locationId, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1 }` index as the next step without taking it. **The honest shape of this increment is that the defect cannot be reproduced on the data that still exists** — the tenant that showed it was deleted to restore the cluster's writes (§27DF) and the 512 MB tier cannot hold it again — so what is recorded is a captured plan, a diagnosis and a preventive fix verified in every way available, **not a before/after speedup**. What was observed: at 523 918 rows the call took **2 395 ms** where the same query had taken **98–125 ms** earlier the same day; `explain` captured a **`SORT` winning plan** over the location's rows with **`optimizationTimeMillis: 2052`**; and **no declared key could supply both halves** of the request — `{ businessId, locationId }` carries no sort field, `{ businessId, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1 }` carries no `locationId` — so the planner was choosing between an index-ordered scan that filters and a blocking in-memory sort of every row at that location, and at that size it chose the sort. The fix is the compound key: equality first, then the complete page order, so the wanted plan is index-provided **by construction** rather than chosen by estimates that flipped as the collection grew; `{ businessId, locationId }` is a **prefix** of it and is **deliberately kept** (the same choice this model made when the page-sort index was added), so no environment has an index dropped underneath it. **Verified:** after `Model.init()` (build **1 401 ms**, index size **296.3 → 298.4 MB**) `explain` shows `LIMIT → FETCH → IXSCAN` on `businessId_1_locationId_1_updatedAt_-1__id_-1` with **no `SORT` stage** and **`optimizationTimeMillis: 0`**; `perf:golden` stayed **17/17 byte-for-byte identical** (the guard that an index can never be observed to change a result); and through the service the three locations of the 100 143-row perf tenant read **123 / 173 / 237 ms before** versus **172 / 175 / 372 ms after** — noise, and the honest reading is that **the shape was already index-ordered at 100 000 rows**, so this is prevention at the size where the planner flipped rather than a measured improvement there. `phase-index-integrity.test.mjs` gains a **★ case beside the AI-3 one** asserting the key is **declared** AND **present in the database**, because nothing in the tree named it before and one deleted line would otherwise leave every suite green. **DEVICE-VERIFIED the same day** (OPPO CPH1969, USB tunnels, Metro development build, local backend restarted with its boot instance checked against the port's owner, throwaway MANAGER **removed afterwards**, tunnels cleared, servers stopped): the Inventory screen's **Location** sheet was opened, **Main Warehouse** selected, and the filtered page answered `GET /api/inventory` **200 in 365.5 ms** with the control reading "Main Warehouse". **Limits:** the slow plan was never reproduced, so the fix is argued from the key rather than from a re-measured 2 395 ms; an index makes the desired plan dominant but does not remove plan choice, and no test here fails if the estimates shift for another shape; and the cost is an index (~2 MB on current data) built on the next boot by the same `autoIndex` path the Phase 20G/20H and AI-3 indexes took. Roadmap §27DG.
There was no backup — and the drill found the tier's ceiling (2026-10-18; **server only — two new scripts (`backup-db.mjs`, `restore-db.mjs`), one shared module (`scripts/backup/lib.mjs`), two `package.json` entries, one nine-case suite and the guide section that teaches them; no route, permission, schema, dependency, session, mobile or environment change, and no model was called**): §27DF recorded that the cluster was over its quota and that the tenant behind the 523 918-row measurements **had to be deleted to restore writes**, and reading that back with the Atlas documentation open produces the sentence this increment answers — *"Atlas backups are not available for Free clusters (formerly known as M0). You may use `mongodump`…"* — so the production database had **no recoverable copy of any kind**, and the free tier's own documented remedy was not reachable from this repository either. Now: **`npm run backup:db`** is **read-only** (one gzipped Extended-JSON file per collection, one document per line, plus `manifest.json` carrying each file's length, uncompressed size and **SHA-256**; manifest written last, so its presence means every file before it completed; it may point at production precisely because it cannot write there), and **`npm run restore:db`** is **destructive and fail-closed** — the target must be `inventoryai_test_db` or `inventoryai_dev_db` (**allowlist imported from `cleanup/lib.mjs` rather than re-typed, so the two cannot drift**), **every** file is verified against the manifest **before anything is dropped**, and the phrase is required; `--dry-run` verifies and writes nothing. **Verified by 9 cases, 9 passing** (`server/test/OPS1-backup-restore.test.mjs`, registered PURE_DB), run against the suite's **own scratch database** because the first version used `inventoryai_test_db` itself and the listing assertion exposed the hazard — `node --test` runs files in parallel, so a suite that dumps and restores a shared database drops collections other suites are using. The round trip is exact for the BSON types a plain JSON dump degrades (`ObjectId`, `Date`, `Decimal128`, nested, `null`), compared as canonical Extended JSON **with a negative control on the comparator itself**, since an equality helper that always returned true would make the file vacuous; a **byte flipped inside the gzip** is refused with the manifest's hash **and the row written just beforehand still present**, which is the property that matters — verify-all-then-drop is the order under test; a missing file, a manifest listing nothing and a backup of an empty database are each refused; a dry run writes nothing; a dump directory inside the repository is refused by name. **Then both commands were run for real against the development database (15 collections, 525 750 documents, 111 923 products, 223 227 inventory rows):** the dump wrote **11.0 MB on disk (240.5 MB to restore)**, the dry run **verified all 15 files and wrote nothing**, and the real restore put `ai_conversations` → `inventory` back — **223 227 documents in 46 s**, after the first version's 500-documents-per-round-trip serial insert managed ~250 docs/s (a 560 s window ended with `inventory` holding 140 000 of 223 227); batching is now 2 000 with four in flight, **~19× faster**, and progress prints every 50 000 documents because a nine-minute silence is indistinguishable from a hang. **The drill then found the tier's ceiling:** Atlas stopped it with *"you are over your space quota, using 520 MB of 512 MB. Writes are blocked on your cluster."* — the copy needs ~240 MB and the cluster had ~280 MB used, so **a second copy of the development dataset does not fit on this tier** — and the command reported it, **exited 1 and claimed nothing**. **Nothing was lost:** the space was freed by dropping `inventoryai_test_db` (its documented throwaway state), the development database was never touched (`dataSize 125.1 MB`, all 15 collections intact, verified), and a write probe confirmed the cluster accepts writes again before the suite was re-run. **The finding, stated plainly: the backup works at production scale and the tier cannot hold its restore** — no code change fixes that, and it is now the measured argument for the cluster upgrade (**~240 MB needed to restore, 512 MB total, and no server-side backups at all**) with the `size` line both CLIs print existing because of this run, since an 11 MB file told an operator nothing about the fit. **Limits, stated rather than glossed:** a full restore was **never observed end-to-end** on this tier for that reason (every covered case is a copy that fits); it is single-database — not `admin`, not an oplog, no substitute for continuous backups once real users exist; the dump is **not point-in-time** (reading collection by collection means a write during the dump can land in one file and not another — the manifest is internally consistent, the dataset is not transactional); and **nothing schedules it**, because a copy that depends on someone remembering is the situation this increment found. Roadmap §27DH.
The pulse an outside watcher can read (2026-10-18; **server only — one new module, one middleware line in `app.ts`, one field on the existing legacy health payload, one MIXED suite and a guide section; no new route, no permission, no schema, no dependency, no environment value, no mobile change, and no model was called**): §27DF recorded the gap without filling it — the cluster was refusing writes and four assistant tools were answering `TOOL_ERROR` **while `/api/health/ready` reported `ready` throughout**, because readiness answers *“is the process up and the database reachable”* and nothing answered *“are requests succeeding”*. Now `src/config/operationalCounters.ts` counts responses **by status class**, counts **5xx separately** with the time of the most recent one, is bounded by construction (fixed keys, integers, ≤ 8 tracked statuses plus an aggregate bucket), and is returned as a **copy** so a caller serializing it cannot reach into the process's state; **one middleware line in `app.ts` on `res.on('finish')`, registered before the first route**, so the error handler's and `notFound`'s responses are counted too; and **one field on the existing legacy `/api/health` payload** — no new route, the same exposure class and the same reason as the commit id already published there (it identifies behaviour, not people), while `/live` and `/ready` stay **unchanged** because one is a dependency-free probe and the other a gate.
