The guide's Part 7 now turns the field into an actual alert: poll `/api/health/ready`, alert on two consecutive non-200s, watch `counters.serverErrors` climbing, with **UptimeRobot** named as the smallest service that does it and a bare `curl` cron named as the no-account alternative. **Verified:** `server/test/OPS2-operational-counters.test.mjs`, **11 cases / 11 passing**, registered MIXED — the status classification **and its own negative control** (99, 600, 0, −1, 1.5, `NaN`, `undefined`, `null` and the string `'200'` all answer `other` rather than being folded into a plausible class), a **read is not traffic**, a 4xx is not a server error and does not move the error timestamp, the snapshot is a copy whose mutation leaves the live counters untouched, the published surface pinned by key list with every value asserted to be a number, one timestamp or `null` (**no path, id or message in it**), **100 distinct 5xx codes collapsing into 8 statuses plus `other` without losing a count** (100 in, 100 out), reset returning to boot state, and a source guard that the counter is installed **before** the first route; over the real app — a finished request is counted, a **404** lands in 4xx and leaves the error count alone, `/live` and `/ready` carry **no `counters` key at all** (their payload keys asserted, so the surface cannot widen by accident), and **the end-to-end case is the sequence that was invisible**: the database connection is dropped, `/api/health/ready` answers its real **503**, and the next `/api/health` read reports `serverErrors ≥ 1` with a timestamp. **Limits, stated rather than glossed:** the counters are **process-local** (four instances each report their own; a shared counter needs a shared store this project does not have — the reason B2/E3 are deferred), they **reset on restart** and carry no history, so this is *“is it failing now”* rather than *“did it fail at 02:00”*; **nothing was wired to the outside** — the service, monitor and alert recipient are the owner's to choose, so until a monitor exists this makes an outage **observable**, not **announced**, which is the whole remaining gap; and `/api/health` is **public**, as it already was, with the disclosure bounded on purpose (counts, no names) and recorded rather than left to be noticed. Roadmap §27DI.
```text
The reference data the assistant could not see — and the ids two tools required (2026-10-19; **server only — two tools in the existing table, two sentences in the system prompt, one MIXED suite and the two catalogue counts; no route, permission, schema, dependency, environment value or mobile change**): `getProductsByLocation` takes a `locationId` and `searchProducts` / `getReorderNeededProducts` take a `categoryId`, but **nothing in the tool table could turn "the Main Warehouse" or "Beverages" into one**, so the specification's own example (*"Show me inventory in the warehouse"*) was answerable only by luck — when an unrelated inventory row happened to carry the id — and *"which locations do I have?"* was not answerable at all. `listLocations` and `listCategories` close that gap, and the permissions are the interesting part: their REST counterparts (`GET /api/locations`, `GET /api/categories`) require **authentication only**, so there is no reference-read permission to mirror and none was invented — they are gated on `INVENTORY_VIEW` and `PRODUCTS_VIEW` because that is what they are *for*, and all four roles hold both, so the gate is at least as strict as the route it mirrors and narrows nobody. **Archived is excluded by default and the response says so** (`status`), because a model handed an archived location would confidently report stock "in" somewhere that cannot receive any — worse than not knowing it exists — while `status: 'ARCHIVED'` answers the member who asks about a closed one. `referenceItem` maps each row to `{id, name, description, status}` and **deliberately omits `businessId`** — the tenant is already the session's, so publishing it into a transcript buys nothing and puts a tenant identifier in a prompt. The prompt gains one sentence (rule 9, ~30 tokens per turn): resolve a location or category name to its id **before** filtering by id, never guess one. Suite `server/test/AI14-reference-tools.test.mjs`, **13 cases (5 unit + 8 DB)**; removing the ACTIVE default failed **3** cases exactly and the file was restored **byte-identically**. **TWO REAL TURNS WERE THEN RUN against the deployed model** (80 000-product perf tenant, four provider requests): *"Which locations do I have?"* answered **4 586 ms** with `tools=listLocations` alone and named the three **active** locations; *"Show me the products in Main Warehouse."* called `listLocations,getProductsByLocation` — **the model resolved the name into the id**, which is the join this increment exists for. **That run also found a defect the suites could not:** the second turn took **29 210 ms** and returned `outcome=degraded reason=OUTPUT_LIMIT out=800`, because a location with 1 000 products invited an enumeration that the 800-token cap cut off mid-list — marked correctly (AI-8/AI-13) and still a half answer for no reason. Prompt rule 3 now says to summarise a **truncated** result and give its count instead of listing rows; re-measured on the same tenant and question it returned **`outcome=ok`, `out=511`, 11 427 ms** — no partial answer and **61 % faster**, for ~35 tokens per turn. **Limits:** no device run — the screen has not shown a `listLocations` provenance chip; the two prompt sentences cost ~65 tokens on every turn; and the reference lists are capped at 50 rows, so a tenant with more locations would need the model to narrow with `q`. Roadmap §27DJ.
The instrument that measured the provider could crash, and the test threw away why (2026-10-19; **server only — one script's connection handling, one test helper and one guard case; no route, permission, schema, dependency, environment value, mobile or production-server change, and no model was called**): `npm run ai:usage` — the read-only report ISS-22's provider decision rests on — failed inside a full-suite run with the single least useful message a test can produce, `SyntaxError: Unexpected end of JSON input`, and **two defects** were behind it. The script connects to MongoDB itself and had **no connection `error` listener**; a mongoose `Connection` is an `EventEmitter`, so such an event is **thrown**, and a teardown-time `MongoNetworkError: connect ETIMEDOUT …:27017` killed the process after it had computed everything and before it printed anything — empty stdout, a stack trace on stderr, indistinguishable from *"no turns"*. **The first fix was incomplete and the next run proved it:** listeners attached around the read and detached in `finally` did not cover `mongoose.disconnect()`, which is exactly where a pending socket's timeout surfaces; they are now attached **before `connect()`** and **never removed**, because this is a one-shot report process with nothing to leak. The second defect was the harness: `cli()` returned the exit code and stderr and every assertion then did `JSON.parse(res.stdout)` **without reading either**, so a dead child and an empty database failed identically. `json()` now asserts the run first — killed, non-zero (with stderr's first line), or exit-0-but-empty — and only then parses, `dbReport()` asserts `database.status === 'OK'` separately, and the child is bounded at 120 s so a hang is *reported* rather than stalling the suite. New case `★ a dropped connection is a row in the report, never an unhandled error` pins the wiring (an `error` and a `disconnected` listener exist, **no** `.off('error')` anywhere, the listener attached **before** the first `connect()`), and re-adding the detach failed **exactly that case and only it**, restored **byte-identically**. The report was then run twice against the real cluster, both answering `database.status: OK`. **Limits:** it survives a network fault, it does not retry one; the 120 s bound is a hang-stopper, not a service level; and the `ETIMEDOUT` is this host's path to one Atlas node, not a repository defect. Roadmap §27DK.
The location a member named — one provider call instead of two (2026-10-19; **server only — three tool schemas, one resolver, one optional `exactName` filter on the reference list, one MIXED suite; no route, permission, schema, dependency, environment value or mobile change**): AI-14's measured turn *"Show me the products in Main Warehouse"* took **3 provider calls / 11 427 ms** because the first call (`listLocations`) existed only to translate a name into an id. `getInventory`, `getProductsByLocation` and `getInventoryHistory` now accept **`location`**, the exact name, alongside `locationId`. **`referenceService.list` gained `exactName`** — anchored, regex-escaped, case-insensitive after trimming — rather than reusing `q`, which searches name *and description* and, truncated to a page, could push the exact record out of view on a large tenant. Only **ACTIVE** locations of the **session's own business** resolve; a **duplicated name returns both candidates** rather than picking one, because a name is a member's word and not a key; passing both forms is refused; and **neither form is refused politely**, since an `undefined` id reaching `productService.list` would answer the whole tenant while looking like a filtered page. **Prompt rule 9 was amended**, not left contradicting the new parameter: pass the exact name to a tool that accepts one, resolve an id with `listLocations` only when it does not. `server/test/AI15-location-name.test.mjs`, **15 cases (3 unit + 12 DB)**: name and id return the **same rows and count** with the resolved id reported, case/whitespace forgiven, `getInventory` and `getInventoryHistory` narrowing identically with "all locations" as their default, **prefixes** (`Main`, `Sh`, `Twin`) and **description fragments** refused, archived refused, duplicates disambiguated, cross-tenant refused while the same name still resolves for its own tenant, and both-forms refused (and the `phase06-ai-tools` empty-call case moved from the schema to the executor — a JSON-Schema subset with no `anyOf` cannot say "one of two", so the soft refusal is asserted **along with carrying no rows**) — while `getProductsByLocation` refuses a missing location and `getInventory` keeps "all locations" as its documented default, a regression this change introduced and the suite caught before it landed (the unfiltered answer is asserted **wider** than one location's, so the case cannot pass vacuously). **The first mutation taught a lesson:** removing the anchors failed **nothing**, because the filter reads `name` only — so the suite gained the prefix case it was missing and the same mutation then failed **exactly that case**, restored **byte-identically**. **Measured on the deployed model, same question:** `tools=getProductsByLocation` alone, **10 013 ms, 2 calls** (from `listLocations,getProductsByLocation`, 3 calls). **Limits:** the **call count** is the structural result and the 1.4 s is not a speed claim (one sample of a provider whose turns range 4.6–29.2 s); the name must be exact, so "Warehouse" costs the fallback call; more than two duplicates still answer ambiguously; and categories deliberately keep their lookup call. Roadmap §27DL.
The assistant on the owner's phone — six questions, five increments, every figure checked against MongoDB (2026-10-19; **server + mobile — AI-16 … AI-20; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment-VALUE change; the deployed free-tier model and a labelled, disposable fixture tenant were used** — `SEED_CONFIRM=seed-roles npm run seed:roles` builds it, `cleanup:roles` removes it): the owner asked for the app to be exercised on hardware rather than inferred from suites, and the phone disagreed with the suites five times. **AI-16** gave `searchProducts`/`getReorderNeededProducts` an exact `category` name through AI-15's anchored `exactName` filter — and the device then corrected the increment's own premise: the first live turn still called `listCategories` first (**2 tool runs / 3 provider calls**), because rule 9's *"otherwise resolve the id"* reads as *"confirm first"*; the rule now forbids a confirming lookup and the re-run spent **1 tool run / 2 calls** for the same, correct answer (**10** low-or-out Beverages products = **3 out + 7 low**, against a ground truth of 10 = 3 + 7). **AI-17**: the small grey `02:33` in the corner of every reply was `formatTime(createdAt)` — the message's own clock time, which the owner read as a response time and asked about — and is now `formatClock12` (**`2:33 AM`**); the owner's follow-up is part of the record (the first version labelled it *"Answered"*/*"Sent"*, the owner asked for the bare time back), and the suite pins the bare 12-hour form and fails if the 24-hour one returns. **AI-18**: *"Show me the inventory in Main Warehouse"* — the specification's own example question — called `getInventory`, received **25 of 203** rows, printed all 25 and came back **`degraded` at exactly `out=800`**, cut off at row 16, even though the model itself had written *"showing the first 25 of 203"*: nothing said `items` was a WINDOW. Every paged list now reports `returned`/`hasMore` (nine tools, one `page()` helper, plus a source guard that no tool may go back to a bare `{ count, items }`), the prompt bounds an answer at **five items counting cards, list entries and table rows alike**, and `getInventory`/`getInventoryHistory` report the resolved `locationId` so an answer is checkable; the same question then answered **`ok`, `out=355`** — *"203 products"*, five rows, chip `Read: getProductsByLocation`. **AI-19**: `getInventoryHistory` returned `transactionPublic` verbatim, so every history answer put `businessId` and `userId` into the prompt and spent output retyping ledger fields; the row is now the movement (type, quantity, before, after, reason, reference, and the product/location NAMES) — same question, **`in=8173 → in=5503`**. **AI-20** raised `AI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` **800 → 1200**, **explicitly reversing §27BY** on new measurement rather than taste (three turns hit 800 exactly, two while obeying the five-item bound; a five-row movement table is ~900–1000 tokens in this model's own wording; the history answer then completed at `out=1078` in 20.7 s where the 800-cap version was cut at row 3 of 5), with §27BY's slow-path caveat carried forward **in the record** — at the 13.9–16.5 tok/s it measured, a 1200-token answer cannot finish inside the 60 s deadline and becomes a `504` instead of a marked partial answer. **Accuracy was checked, not assumed:** Main Warehouse holds **203** inventory rows and the assistant said 203; the printed rows (**32 / 1 / 0 / 7 / 609** available) and the five newest movements (**+22** Ultra Kettle 10137 313→335, **−40** Everyday Towel 10090 60→20, **+49** Pro Cable 10153 17→66, **−23** Deluxe Battery 10098 62→39, **−73** Deluxe Bottle 10094 103→30) each match the stored documents, beside a dashboard reading 203 / 46 602 / 167 in stock / 11 out / 36 low. **Verified:** server **1605 tests / 242 suites / 0 failures**, mobile **74 suites / 937 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint`/module-graph clean on both sides; the AI-16 anchors and the AI-18 source guard were each watched failing and restored byte-identically; the running app was reloaded by broadcast so the JS under test was the JS on disk. **Limits:** three questions, one model, one tenant — turns on this host range 4.6–32.3 s, so no latency number here is a claim; the model still chooses its format, so the five-item bound is followed most of the time rather than always; the chip shows internal tool names, which the owner may want to revisit *(superseded the same day — AI-21, §27DN)*; and `ISS-29`'s row is amended with the cap change and its slow-path caveat. Roadmap §27DM.

The chip a member can read — and the assistant at 50 000 products (2026-10-19; **mobile AI-21 plus a measurement pass; no server file, route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment change; the deployed free-tier model and a labelled, disposable tenant**): §27DM ended with a limit and the owner's question — the provenance chip showed server identifiers (`Read: getProductsByLocation`) — and the owner asked for labels, so **AI-21** adds `mobile/lib/toolLabels.ts`: each tool is rendered as what was **read** (`getProductsByLocation` → *Products at a location*, `getInventoryHistory` → *Stock movements*, `getReorderNeededProducts` → *Restock list*), unknown names are **humanised** rather than printed (`getWidgetStats` → *Widget stats*) so a tool added later cannot leak an identifier, the stored message keeps the real names and `restockPathForTools` still keys off them, and the suite (*8 cases*) asserts the eleven labels are **pairwise distinct**. The suite caught a real gap in the first implementation — a name with no letters (`123`) came back unchanged — fixed with a total fallback (*Your data*), not a loosened assertion. **Device evidence:** the on-screen answer at 03:39 read **`Read: Low-stock products`** where it had read `Read: getLowStockProducts`. The same turn answered the list-length question on hardware: **five items**, then *“The full list continues beyond these five items (the current page shows 20 of 36). You can view the complete low-stock report on the matching screen”* — the AI-18 bound held and now points at the screen holding the rest. **AI-3's open item is closed:** a **50 000-product** tenant (100 143 inventory rows / 100 096 transactions, seeded in 112.8 s, `cleanup:perf` removes it) was measured with **zero model calls** — the three index-backed tools stayed flat (`getInventory` **102 ms**, history **107**, search **213**) and the merged partition-scan family grew **3.6–4×** for 5× the data (`getProductsByLocation` 258 → **1 041 ms**, low 265 → 1 015, reorder 268 → 962, out 259 → 946, summary 413 → 723), worst tool **1.2 s** and every payload under the 12 000-char truncation; **two real turns over HTTP** then answered **grounded and numerically exact** — 50 000 / **7 585** low / **3 079** out in 10.4 s and **12 425 872** units in 6.7 s, each against ground truth read at run time. One artefact recorded rather than smoothed over: the first answer **stopped mid-sentence with no `degraded` marker**, because the provider reported a normal finish rather than the cap — the marker logic is right, the sentence is a free-tier model artefact. **Verified:** mobile **75 suites / 946 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint` clean. **Limits:** the 50 k ledger is synthetic, two turns are two samples on a provider that has ranged 4.6–32.3 s on this host, and a server-side rename of a tool would silently fall back to the humanised label by design. Roadmap §27DN.

The round-trips the prompt never asked the model to save (2026-10-19; **server only — AI-22: prompt rules 10–12, one wire-mapping change in `anthropicWire.ts`, one new suite; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment-value change, and no mobile file**): the owner asked his phone *"Compare my two locations, how many products are in each?"* and the answer was **exactly right** — 203 and 99, both checked against MongoDB — while the turn line read `calls=5 toolRuns=4 providerMs=50879` and the wall clock said **51.6 s**: **one full model round-trip per lookup**, ~10 s each, against a 60 s deadline. The answer was not the defect; the path to it was. `runAiChat` could already execute every call in one response, and **nothing had ever asked it to** — so rule 10 now says independent lookups go out together (with the reason: the member waits for every round-trip), rule 11 forbids naming a tool, function or internal identifier in member-facing prose (the chip was translated by AI-21; the sentence was not), and rule 12 forbids appended sections nobody asked for, after the same answer volunteered a movements summary — output tokens are generation time on the member's clock. The third leg is defensive: the Anthropic mapping sent **one `user` message per tool result** and relied on a compatible gateway merging consecutive user turns, a dependency no model had ever exercised because none had batched. Adjacent results now travel as **one** user message with N `tool_result` blocks — the canonical shape — and since Gemini's mapping already merged, the suite asserts **both wires** produce one model turn plus one user turn for the same conversation, which is the divergence guard. **Measured on the same question, tenant and model: `calls=5 → 3`, `toolRuns=4 → 3`, 51.6 s → **29.8 s** (−43 %), `out=446 → 283`, same exact answer.** `server/test/AI22-batched-lookups.test.mjs` — **11 DB-free cases** (the rules, the loop's multi-call path, ids and contents paired in the batch, the untouched single-result shape, both providers agreeing, and the boundary that a later user message is never merged into a result batch), each leg watched failing first; server **1616 tests / 245 suites / 0 failures**, mobile **75 suites / 946 tests / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint` clean. **One process finding recorded rather than tidied:** a mutation experiment reverted `ai.service.ts` to HEAD and thereby undid AI-13's payload cap, restoring `truncate(JSON.stringify(output), …)` — and the **AI-13 source guard failed the suite** (it asserts the cap call is present *and* the old shape absent, with a positive control), so the regression was repaired instead of shipping as a passing build. **Limits:** one turn on one provider whose turns range 4.6–32.3 s, so the 43 % is evidence and not a rate; batching is a model *choice* the prompt asks for and nothing enforces; the wire merge removes a risk no rejection had demonstrated; rule 11 cannot be proven from one answer; and rule 12 bounds bloat, not helpfulness. Roadmap §27DO.

The comparison one lookup can answer — and the prompt sentence that made the model use it (2026-10-19; **server only — AI-23: one service read (`inventoryService.locationBreakdown`), one tool, prompt rule 13, one harness question; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment-value change, and no mobile file**): AI-22 took the owner's two-location question from 5 provider calls to 3, and the turn still cost **29.3 s** because the member never names the locations — one call went on learning them, and **no batching instruction can merge a second lookup that cannot be phrased until the first answers**. The capability was missing, so it was added: a per-location breakdown reporting, for every location, how many **distinct** products it holds and how many units are on hand, plus business totals — one aggregate (two `$group` stages under a `$facet`), `totals.products` counting a product **once** however many shelves hold it, empty locations present with zeros, **ARCHIVED locations marked with their stock intact**, and stock whose location no longer exists summed into an `Unassigned` entry rather than dropped. The tool takes **no arguments at all** — the strictest schema in the table, so there is no field in which a `businessId` could arrive — and is gated on `DASHBOARD_VIEW`. **The first hypothesis was wrong and measured to be wrong:** with the tool in the table and a description saying it answers every location at once, a live turn on a 1 000-product tenant still called `listLocations` and then asked about one location (33 643 ms). **Prompt rule 13** — naming the tool once for the question shape it exists for, ~40 tokens per turn — is what changed the choice: the same question then answered with **`getLocationBreakdown` alone, 2 provider calls, 8 983 ms** (`toolMs=114ms toolRuns=1`, `in=4287 out=221`) and every figure correct. At **10 000 products / 20 105 rows** the tool measures **242 ms** median with a **378-character** payload, against `getInventorySummary` 212 ms / 1 582 chars. The read also reproduces a device-verified answer independently: the dev fixture reports **Main Warehouse 203 / Shop Front 99**, the figures the phone showed on 2026-10-19. `server/test/AI23-location-breakdown.test.mjs` — **10 cases** written first and watched failing, with **three mutations each failing the case it should** (totals double-counted; empty locations filtered; tenant `$match` removed) and the file restored **byte-identically** from a checksummed copy; the AI-2 invariant suite required the new read to be **added to its allow-list**, which is the guard working as designed. **Limits:** the owner's exact wording (*"my two locations"*) on a three-location tenant made the model **correctly** ask which two — the harness question was reworded, the clarification was not engineered away; one question, one model, one tenant, so the call count is structural and the wall clock is not; the prompt now names one tool, which is a recorded trade; and 50 000 products was not re-measured for this read. Roadmap §27DP.

The question's own clock — and the four copies a database blip exposed (2026-10-19; **mobile AI-24 plus one line of the chat controller; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment-value change**): the owner asked for a timestamp on their **own** messages, matching the answer's, so the interval between question and reply can be read off the screen. `MessageBubble` had always supported it — `styles.timeUser`, `testID="chat-time-user"`, the same `formatClock12` — but **nothing supplied the value**: the optimistic row was built by hand as `{ key, role, content }`, so the member's bubble had no time at all. The timestamp could not simply be copied from the stored message: a turn persists **nothing** until the provider succeeds (Phase 07E / M7), so the USER row is written *after* the answer, and two timestamps made from that are a second apart however long the member waited — the interval the feature exists for, gone, and a reloaded thread reporting zero seconds. The time is therefore taken where it is true: **on the device** when Send is pressed (for the bubble drawn while the turn runs) and **on the server** as `askedAt`, recorded before `runAiChat` and written as the USER message's explicit `createdAt` — Mongoose honours a supplied value (measured against the live schema first: 45 s in the past stored unchanged), so no schema change was needed and none was made. The reply's `userMessage` then **replaces** the optimistic bubble, which is the same single-mapping rule AI-8 applied to the answer. **Two defects were found on the phone during a real Atlas blip (two 503s), not by any test:** a failed send **left its bubble on screen** although nothing was stored, and every retry stacked another — the same question appeared **four times**; fixed by removing the bubble and returning the text to the composer on a failed (not stopped) send. The first fix then **caused** the second: matching "a user row with no server id" hit *every* leftover bubble, so the next success replaced all of them with the same stored message — four identical rows and React's *"Encountered two children with the same key"*; fixed by holding the pending row's key in a local (the only row this turn may replace) and deduping the merged list by server id, which also closes the first-turn reload race. **Verified:** `mobile/__tests__/ai24-user-message-time.test.tsx` (**8 cases**) and `server/test/AI24-message-times.test.mjs` (**3 DB cases** with a deliberately slow 350 ms fake provider, because a fast one makes both stampings land in the same millisecond and the suite would pass whatever the code did; the stored row is read back from MongoDB, and a failed turn still writes nothing) — each half proven able to fail by mutation and restored **byte-identically**; mobile **76 suites / 954 tests / 0 failures**, server **1629 / 248 / 0**, `tsc`/`eslint` clean both sides. **Device-verified:** a reloaded conversation shows the question at **3:01 PM** and the answer at **3:02 PM**, one bubble each. **Limits:** the footer is minute-resolution (AI-17's chosen format), so a 20 s turn reads 3:01 → 3:02 — a duration label would make it exact and is a separate decision; the in-flight time is the device clock and the stored one the server's; a stopped turn keeps its bubble because the server may have stored it; and the long-running server kept answering 503 after the database was reachable again and needed a restart — recorded, not changed. Roadmap §27DQ.

One provider, on purpose — the direct Claude and Gemini implementations removed (2026-10-19; **server only — AI-25: provider registry, `aiConfig`, `.env.example`, the production-config message and the suites that named the removed providers; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency or mobile change**): the owner asked whether the extra providers still earned their configuration, with simplicity, reliability and maintainability as the goal, and the answer is **remove them** — because they bought **no capability** (Claude and Gemini models are reachable **through OpenRouter** as model choices, so `AI_MODEL_OPENROUTER` is the whole change), because the "fallback" was **theoretical** (a second provider only falls back if it has its own account, key and billing and is exercised — none of which was true), and because Gemini carried a **second wire mapping and parser** plus two more environment variables in every operator's `.env`. Removed: `claudeProvider.ts`, `geminiProvider.ts` and their registry entries, `aiConfig().claudeModel`/`.geminiModel`, and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY`/`AI_MODEL_CLAUDE`/`AI_MODEL_GEMINI` from `.env.example`. **Kept, deliberately:** the `AIProvider` interface and registry (OpenRouter is one entry; the test `fake` is the other) and **`anthropicWire.ts`** — OpenRouter *is* an Anthropic-compatible gateway, so that shared mapping is the deployed path; restoring a direct provider is one factory plus one entry, and git keeps the Gemini mapping. An existing OpenRouter deployment sees **nothing** change; a `.env` still naming a removed provider gets **503 `AI_NOT_CONFIGURED`** through the route (tested) and `AI_PROVIDER` is named by the production check, whose message now offers only `openrouter` (or empty to disable). `server/test/AI25-single-provider.test.mjs` — **4 DB-free cases written first and watched failing on the unmodified tree** (`providerRequiredEnv('claude')` returned `['ANTHROPIC_API_KEY']`, and selection answered with the old missing-key sentence): openrouter is the only built-in, the removed names are refused as **unknown**, `aiConfig` no longer carries their models, and `.env.example` no longer documents their keys — a documented variable is a requirement whether or not anything reads it. Updated rather than deleted: the selection cases in `phase06-ai-provider` (including the upgrade path), `phase07d-production-config` (openrouter's settings required; removed keys planted as secrets that must not leak), and AI-22's cross-wire parity case, now an assertion on the one remaining wire with the reason in place. **Limits:** there is no code-level provider fallback any more — a deliberate trade, since the previous one had never been exercised; the model remains an OpenRouter concern and still must support tools; Gemini's mapping lives only in git history; and nothing device-level changed, so nothing was device-verified. Roadmap §27DR.

The answer a member can finish reading (2026-10-19; **server AI-26 + mobile; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency or environment change, and no extra provider call**): two complaints and one instrument decided this. The development database held **4 `OUTPUT_LIMIT` answers out of 34 and every one ended inside a Markdown table cell** (`… | Professional Towel 1`, `… | 6ad49`), so AI-8's note explained the truncation without making a half-row readable, and the phone — at 60 000 products — printed `OUT_OF_STOCK` in a Status column and `Product (name)` as a header. `trimIncompleteAnswer` (**pure, no quota, no latency**) now drops the ONE incomplete unit at the end: a cut/missing-cell/unclosed table row, or a paragraph cut back to its last finished sentence, keeping closing markdown; a complete row, sentence or **list item** is left alone (a whole item and a cut one are indistinguishable at the end of a string, and deleting a correct row to rescue a cut one is the worse failure) and with nothing whole to keep the text is returned **unchanged**. `humaniseStatusConstants` runs on **every** answer before the trim (`OUT_OF_STOCK` → **Out of stock**, word-bounded so `LOW_STOCK_PRODUCTS` survives), because a prompt is a request and this is the deterministic half. The degraded line carries `trimmed=<chars>`. **Prompt rules, each traceable to a stored or on-screen sentence:** rule 3 now **names the app's screens** and rules out the invented one (*"There is no Reorder needed screen — the restock list is the Restock screen"*), rule 4 asks for the **thousands separator** the app uses (`8,976`, not `8976`) except inside names/SKUs/dates, rule 14 bans status constants and field names in parentheses, rule 15 requires the **first sentence to state both numbers** (*"47 products need restocking; the 5 most urgent are below."*). **Mobile:** the restock link is drawn on **every stock-list answer** rather than only when the answer was truncated — the *complete* answer showing five of 8,976 was the one with no way there, and `toolsUsed` already carries the fact; a **three-column table is now a card** (`TABLE_GRID_MAX_COLUMNS` 3 → 2) because the first real grid on hardware wrapped its header mid-word (`Availabl / e QTY`) at a 2:1:1 split; and the **Restock screen states how much of itself is on screen** (`Showing 20 of 8,976 — scroll for more`, then `All 8,976 products shown`), since it pages silently. **Measured on the phone** (OPPO CPH1969, development build over Metro): on the 203-product fixture the answer read *"36 products need restocking; the 5 most urgent are below."*, drew card rows, and its link opened a screen headed **Restock** with the same 36 and the same five; on the disposable 60 000-product tenant **4 of 4 turns were grounded and numerically exact** (8,976 / 3,654 / 14,928,688 / 8,976 — every figure equal to the dashboard, every row equal to the top of the Restock list), at **10.3 / 17.1 / 22.0 / 43.0 s composed of 0.97–1.23 s of tools and the remaining 92–97 % provider time** — provider variance, not a regression. `AI26-answer-completion.test.mjs` **22 cases**, `ai26-restock-link.test.tsx` **6**, restock-footer **2** and the three-column card **1**, each written first and watched failing where the behaviour was new; **mobile 77 suites / 963 tests / 0 failures**, server green in full. **Limits, recorded:** the trim is proven by test and by the four real tails, **not by a device answer drawn after a trim** (the cap is 1 200 and the measured turns used 483–774 tokens, so nothing on the phone reached it); the separator rule arrived **after** the measured turns, so no turn has yet confirmed the model obeys it, and a deterministic separator pass was **rejected** because it would rewrite the five-digit code inside every product name; the footer is test-only because at 8,976 rows the next page loads before it is on screen long. *(Annotated 2026-10-20: the separator rule and rule 15's **total in the first sentence** are now confirmed by LIVE turns rather than by argument — `8,976` and `14,928,688` in answers at the 60 000-product tenant, and `46,602` on the phone; the trim was also seen end to end on hardware at zero quota, through a stub provider. See §27DT.)* Roadmap §27DS.
AI-27 — The Allowance Is Read Before It Is Spent (fixes the waste the 2026-10-19 device session measured; **server tooling only — `perf:turns` and `ai:usage`; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency, application behaviour or environment-value change, no model call and no quota spent by the change**): `perf:turns` is the only instrument that measures a REAL assistant turn and it is metered by a hard daily free-tier cap, which was reached mid-session on 2026-10-19 — two of its questions were then sent anyway and both answered `429 AI_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` (`calls=1`, `764 ms` then `116 ms`), so the run spent requests to learn there were none left, and `ai:usage` already read the provider's own counter (`GET /v1/key` → `free_model_daily_requests`) but could not be asked that question alone, because it requires a log file or `--db`. Now: `shortfall(usage, needed)` lives in `scripts/ai-usage.mjs` as the one judgement — a run is short only when the provider SAID a number and it is below the questions selected, so `NOT_CONFIGURED`/`UNREADABLE`/`SKIPPED`, a paid key with no counter and a `remaining: null` all return `null` and block nothing, and **exactly enough is not a shortfall** (`<`, not `<=`); `readProviderUsage` is **bounded** (aborts at 15 s and reports `no answer within 15000 ms`, where it previously had no timeout and could hold a report open indefinitely); `perf:turns` reads the allowance BEFORE the first turn, prints the numbers beside the selected count, and refuses with **exit 2** when the shortfall is known — **before a session is minted**, so a refused run leaves no row — naming the remaining count, the limit and the window (`00:00 UTC`), with `--force` as the escape, and `--only=` filtering moved ahead of it because the refusal is a function of the questions actually selected; and `npm run ai:usage -- --provider-only` answers the allowance alone with no log, no database, no model and no quota, which is the form it always should have had, with `DEFAULT_BASE_URL` exported rather than copied so the probe and the report cannot ask two different hosts about one account. `allowanceLine(usage)` is the one sentence both printers use — a failure, a paid key and a number can never share a line — and it exists because the first preflight printed `${status} — ${detail}` unconditionally, so the LIVE stub run printed `[turns] allowance: OK — undefined`, a bug found by running it rather than by the suite that had just passed. Guarded by **12 cases** in `server/test/AI27-allowance-preflight.test.mjs` (unit only: the spent day, one short of three, exactly enough allowed, three failed readings that must not refuse, the paid key, `remaining: null`, zero questions, the real body mapping with the masked `label` never becoming a value, one sentence for every reading (with a case that forbids the string `undefined` reaching the output), an **abort-aware hanging fetch** that must settle at its bound, the probe's own source ordering/exit/message, and the CLI as a **child process** with an empty key: exit 0, `source=not requested (--provider-only)`, `provider.status=NOT_CONFIGURED`); the guard was **watched failing twice** — `>=`→`>` broke the boundary case and `exit(2)`→`exit(0)` broke the wiring case, 2 failures and exactly those, both files then restored **byte-identical** and green. **Live:** `npm run ai:usage -- --provider-only` printed `50 used of 50 · 0 remaining today (UTC day; resets 00:00 UTC)`, and `npm run perf:turns -- --only=1,2` refused with exit 2 and that sentence — no request reached the provider, no session row created. Server `tsc`/`eslint` clean; **1666 tests / 254 suites / 0 failures**. **The trim was also exercised end to end on the phone at ZERO provider quota**, because the allowance is spent until 00:00 UTC: the provider was replaced by a local stub answering in the Anthropic wire shape with `stop_reason: max_tokens` and a table cut inside its last cell (the shape of all four stored `OUTPUT_LIMIT` answers) — everything after the provider real. Through the real route and database the turn reported `degraded=OUTPUT_LIMIT` and `trimmed=43`, the cut row gone and the statuses humanised; on the OPPO the two COMPLETE rows drew as cards with no pipe, no separator row and no `OUT_OF_STOCK` on screen, the cut row ABSENT, and the length note still shown — the composition §27DS left unobserved. Limits recorded rather than smoothed: the check is a **floor, not a budget** (a turn with tool rounds costs 1–3 requests, so `remaining >= questions` means the run CAN fit, and a run can still meet a 429 mid-way — the conservative 2–3 multiplier was rejected because it would refuse runs that would have succeeded); the reading is the **account's**, so a model-specific or per-minute limit is invisible; and a **stale** provider counter refuses a run that would have worked, which `--force` exists for. Roadmap §27DT.

DB-1 — A Boot That Survives a Slow Cluster (fixes a defect observed **three times in one afternoon**; **server only — `src/config/db.ts` and one message in `src/config/startup.ts`; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency, environment value or mobile change**): `connectDatabase` made **one** attempt with a 5 s `serverSelectionTimeoutMS`, and mongoose does not retry a **rejected** initial connect — so on 2026-10-19, when this Atlas cluster took **15.8 s** to select (a figure the repository already had: `scripts/ai-usage.mjs` documents it as why it stopped overriding the bound), **three separate `npm start` runs** died on the 5 s bound while a raw connect seconds earlier had succeeded, leaving an API that answered **503 to every database request** until somebody restarted it — and the log's *"Restart once MongoDB is reachable"* was an instruction to the operator, not behaviour of the process. Now **`connectBudget({ nodeEnv, testMode })`** states the budget as data: development gets **3 attempts** with a **30 s** selection bound and a 3 s pause, while **production, test and an unset `NODE_ENV` keep one attempt and 5 s** — production is fail-fast by contract (exit non-zero, Render restarts) and a longer wait there would only delay the restart, so the bound is raised only where the failure mode is a dead development API. **`TEST_MODE` is part of that decision and the first version got it wrong:** the runner sets `TEST_MODE` and leaves `NODE_ENV` unset, so a test process reports `development` and would have taken 3 × 30 s **inside** the suites' own `TEST_DB_MAX_ATTEMPTS` loop — caught by a case asserting a test process is never development. Every attempt names itself (`connection attempt 2/3 failed (…)`) so a boot log separates a slow socket from a dead cluster, and the failure message now says the attempts were exhausted before advising the restart; the URI, the resolved `dbName` (environment separation), `bufferTimeoutMS: 1500` and the reconnect monitoring are untouched. Guarded by **7 cases** in `server/test/DB1-boot-retry.test.mjs` (unit, injected connect: the budget for every environment string, the shipped constants 3 / 30 000 / 5 000, recovery on the second attempt, numbered log lines with **no connection string** in any of them, the **last** error reaching the caller, a real 40 ms pause, the resolved `dbName`/`bufferTimeoutMS` reaching the driver, and — only in a genuine test process — that the default stays **one** attempt); the guard was **watched failing** by removing the test exclusion and setting the non-development budget to 3 in one mutation, then restored **byte-identical** and re-ran **7/7**; and a **real boot with the database unreachable** logged attempts `1/3`, `2/3`, `3/3` three seconds apart, printed the new message, and still listened for diagnostics. Server **1673 tests / 255 suites / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint` clean. Limits recorded: this does **not** make the process self-healing (after three attempts it still needs the restart the message names, and a background reconnect loop was **not** added — it would be a second connection lifecycle beside the driver's own monitoring, for a development-only failure); 15.8 s is **one** measurement, so a cluster unreachable for longer still needs the restart; and the attempts surface as three `MongoDB connection lost` lines from the driver's own events, which is noise rather than behaviour. Roadmap §27DU.

PREVIEW-1 — The Preview Build Measured Against Production (**verification only — no code, route, permission, schema, dependency or environment-value change**): the owner asked for a **preview APK** to test the shipped work in a production-like environment before a release, so EAS build **`4b9a79dd-29db-42a7-bab6-c1162e9b5a8d`** (**`preview`** profile, commit **`dfd2c62`**, `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=https://ai-inventory-l1mi.onrender.com`) was installed **over** the development build with `adb install -r` — it succeeded without an uninstall because the two profiles share the same EAS keystore, so app data survived. On the OPPO CPH1969: the app launched, **rejected the stored session** (`Loading your workspace…` → Login — the token belonged to the development server, whose session row production has never had), signed in as the owner **at the owner's keyboard with no password shared with a tool**, rendered the dashboard, showed **`No conversations yet`** (no development conversations leaked into the production list) and answered one **real production turn** — *"How many total units do we have?"* → *"Your inventory currently shows **0 units** on hand across all locations."*, tool label `Read: Inventory summary`, bold rendered as bold by AI-4's renderer. **The finding that bounds it: the production tenant is empty** (every dashboard count `0`) — the `46,602` units from the earlier device runs were the **development** database's demo data for the same business — so **AI-26's restock link and AI-27's trimmed answer could not be re-run** on this build (they stay device-verified against the local server, §27DS/§27DT), and no demo data was seeded into production without being asked. Also confirmed live: production `/api/health` reports **commit `52113e5`** (the §27DU merge) with `db: connected`, `0` server errors and `232` responses, so the boot-budget change is deployed and production still boots fail-fast; and the provider allowance read **34 of 50 left / used 16** against **used 8** at the §27DT reading, which is the check that the key in Render is the intended account rather than the spent one — the production turn spent against the **same** key this repository holds. One observation recorded, not fixed: a persisted **unverified** Firebase identity put the **Verify-your-email** screen in the auth stack while signed out, and its *"Wrong account? Sign out"* escape returned to a clean Login. Roadmap §27DV.

DEMO-1 — The Demo Tenant a Preview Build Can Be Checked Against (**server tooling and documentation only — `scripts/demo/fixture.mjs`, `scripts/demo/lib.mjs`, `scripts/seed-demo-tenant.mjs`, `scripts/cleanup-demo-tenant.mjs`, two npm scripts, one test file, two guides; no route, permission, schema, session, dependency, environment value or mobile change**): §27DV ended with a production tenant that held nothing, so an assistant answer could be read but not checked. A generated dataset cannot fix that — nobody can say what its total should be — so this is a **hand-written fixture**: **6 products** (one deliberately with **no** inventory row, so "missing row is zero" is exercised), **3 locations**, **7 inventory rows**, **9 ledger rows**, **112 units**, every figure stated by the seeder and **re-read and compared before it reports success** (`verifyDemoTenant`, 15 checks). `assertDemoTarget` requires the database to be **named** on the command line and to equal the one `config/env.ts` resolved, **refuses the test database**, and requires its own confirmation phrase; `planSeed` is pure and **refuses** an address that already belongs to a different business, and a half-state, rather than repairing either; `removeDemoTenant` needs **both** fingerprints (exact business name and owner email) to agree on one businessId, then re-counts leftovers and requires **0**. The two deliberate departures from every other seeder: it **may run against production** (the preview APK can reach nothing else — and a production database is refused from a development process by config), and the teardown **never deletes the Firebase identity** (§27BB/F-50's lockout is order-dependent; an identity without a MongoDB user is harmless and re-seeding reuses it). Verified: **12 DB-free cases**, the guard **watched failing** (one movement's `newQuantity` mutated → 11/1) then restored **byte-identical** and 12/12; a **local rehearsal** seeded, matched 15/15, cleaned to **leftovers 0** and answered *"no demo tenant exists — nothing to remove"* on a second run; **production seeded 15/15** (`NODE_ENV=production MONGODB_DB_NAME=inventoryai_prod_db`, `--database=inventoryai_prod_db`); server **1685 tests / 255 suites / 0 failures**, `tsc`/`eslint`/both module-graph checks/secret guard clean. **The device run on the preview APK against production** matched the fixture on screen — dashboard **3 / 3 / 1 / 6 / 112**; *"How is my inventory doing?"* answered **6 products, 112 units, 3 / 3 / 1** plus the five newest seeded movements; *"How many units at each location?"* answered **Warehouse 75 · Shop 30 · Van 7** in one `Read: Stock by location` call (AI-23); *"What needs restocking?"* listed **Desk Lamp out / Coffee Beans low / Paper Cups low** and its AI-26 **Open the full restock list** link opened the Restock screen with the same three and *"All 3 products shown"*; a 1000-word request **hit the cap** and showed AI-26's warning; **Stop** 1.5 s into a turn replaced the thinking bubble at once with AI-9's sentence and nothing arrived after 12 s. Allowance **16 → 26** requests across six turns. The demo tenant was then **removed in the same session** (leftovers 0), the phone signed out to Login, and only the Firebase identity remains by design; `docs/guides/4-test-accounts-and-mock-data.md` §F documents creation, the expected numbers and removal. Limits: the abandoned turn's **server-side** `AI_ABANDONED` is not observable from this host (measured in §27CM); the capped answer ends mid-clause, which is what the warning is for, since AI-27's trim only removes a structurally broken trailing row; a 6-product tenant says nothing about scale (§27BG/§27DT untouched). Roadmap §27DW.

The Stop, measured again — and two stale claims corrected (2026-10-17; **documentation only — no production file, route, permission, schema, test, dependency or environment change, and no suite re-run for it**): AI-9's Stop against a held stub was measured a second time independently (§27CW) — `[ai] turn outcome=AI_ABANDONED total=6539ms … calls=1` with the stub logging *"caller had already gone away; nothing sent"* 20.0 s before its held answer was due, and the hub listing **one** conversation (the earlier successful stub turn) and **none** for the stopped one, so an aborted turn leaves no conversation and no orphan USER message — read from the member's own list. **The durable part is not the measurement but the correction:** §27CM had already measured this that morning, and **two claims were left saying otherwise** — the ISS-21 row (*"only AI-1's abandoned-turn cancellation remains unmeasured on hardware"*) and the AI-1 entry (*"Still unverified on hardware"*) — so the instruction file sent readers to a gap that no longer existed, while an annotation two entries later recorded the measurement. Both are corrected where they stand; the one path that is genuinely still unprovoked is a stop landing **after** the model had already finished (§27CM's own limits). **Also recorded, because it cost real spend:** four stale `node src/index.ts` processes were alive on this laptop and **Windows let a new server bind `:4000` while the old one kept serving** — it printed `listening on http://localhost:4000` and `/api/health/ready` answered `ready`, so an `OPENROUTER_BASE_URL` override looked like it had been ignored, and two turns reached the **deployed free tier** instead of the local stub (`outcome=AI_OUTPUT_LIMIT total=22068ms in=5265 out=800` and `outcome=degraded total=41442ms providerMs=41364ms in=5141 out=800`, both with nothing usable at the 800-token cap, rendering AI-10's copy on the phone). The check that settles it is **the owner PID on the port**, not readiness or a startup banner: `netstat -ano | grep '0.0.0.0:4000'`. Both traps and the dev-client's floating `Tools` overlay are now in the device guide §9b. Roadmap §27CW.
```

### Workflow simplification (2026-10-09) — the owner asked for a lighter process

The GitHub flow is deliberately minimal now: **branch → commit → push → pull request → review →
merge**, with one `ci` workflow (jobs `server`, `mobile`, `secret-guard`) that runs in about three
minutes and gates nothing — `main` cannot be protected on a private repository at this plan level,
and that is accepted.

**Two workflows were removed** on the owner's decision, to keep the setup small: `main-guard.yml`
(it flagged a push to `main` outside a pull request) and `access-guard.yml` (a daily monitor of
repository visibility and the access list, which had run green).Nothing replaces them unattended; the access check is now the manual `node scripts/audit-access.mjs`, and the deferred-upgrade gates are one read-only command, `node scripts/check-upgrade-watches.mjs` (roadmap §27AQ — outside CI on purpose, since it needs the network). `ci.yml`,
`scripts/check-secrets.mjs` and the pinned action SHAs are kept.

**Dependency policy:** Dependabot queues **minor and patch updates only**, grouped per package,
at most two open PRs per package, weekly — major versions are taken deliberately and are tracked
in issue #32 rather than as standing red PRs. Security updates are exempt from all of that and
still arrive on their own. **Every package the installed Expo SDK pins** is additionally excluded from
version updates — the rule is mechanical: if it is a key in `expo/bundledNativeModules.json`, Expo owns
its version and Dependabot cannot know it (all **21** of the 30 packages the mobile manifest declares,
dependencies and devDependencies both, since **2026-10-12** — the earlier figure of 17 missed the two
SDK-pinned devDependencies, `eslint-config-expo` and `jest-expo`, and the completeness of that list is now
held by `mobile/__tests__/expo-sdk-pins.test.ts` rather than by hand). These move only with an Expo SDK
upgrade via `npx expo install --fix` (roadmap §27AH/§27AL; ISS-16, ISS-17). That command was **blocked** on
the owner's machine by `allow-scripts=9router` in the user-level `~/.npmrc` — npm 11.19.1 rejects the flag
form Expo's installer passes, and `expo install` writes `package.json` before the install it then fails on —
and is **fixed as of 2026-10-12**, the line having been removed (verified: `npx expo install --check` reports
"Dependencies are up to date"). Note separately that npm 11.19.1 gates **dependency install scripts by
default**, so the `npm warn install-scripts` notice is not a local misconfiguration (roadmap §27AY, findings
**F-41** and **F-42**; this host also runs Node 26 while `.nvmrc` pins 24 — **F-43**). The four that
stay open are
`@react-native-firebase/app`, `@react-native-firebase/auth`, `@react-native-google-signin/google-signin`
and `expo` itself, whose versions this project chooses deliberately.

**Device measurements on 2026-10-15, and one correction of the record** (roadmap **§27CE**) — no production behaviour
change: the diff is 11 comment lines and the one experiment it describes was measured and then **reverted**. Two open
device items are settled as far as this hardware allows. **ISS-25's last unmeasured shape is closed on hardware:** the
stored six-column table renders as **one card per row** — title **647 px** (the full bubble), labels **288 px**, values
**359 px**, all three rows carrying all five lines, and **zero** pipes, rules, `**`, `###` or `\frac` anywhere in the
dump. **ISS-13's device half is measured and splits in two:** the artifact `Share PDF` writes is
`/MediaBox [0 0 594.95996 339.12]` — exactly the 595×340 label page — while the print dialog's sheet is **612×792**
(US Letter) and **is not the app's to set**, because expo-print's Android module builds the print job from `orientation`
and margins only (`UNKNOWN_PORTRAIT` = "the service or the user decides"), which is also why the eight guard cases can
pin the arguments and never the page. The obvious remedy — generate the label PDF and print *that* file — was tried on
the device and produced the same Letter sheet, so it was **reverted rather than shipped**; a label printer wants the
shared PDF, or a media size chosen in the dialog. **The correction matters more than either measurement:** an earlier
attempt in this session explained "zero requests reached the local backend" as the installed build talking to the
deployed backend, and that was **wrong** — `adb shell uiautomator dump` was being read back through paths Git Bash and
Node disagree about (`/sdcard/…` mangled; Node's `/tmp` is `C:\tmp`, the shell's is `AppData\Local\Temp`), so every
"screen" was a **stale file** and the app was in fact sitting on the **Expo dev launcher**. Two rules this leaves:
a device reading is evidence only once a **reload is proven** (Metro `Bundled … (1 module)`), since the first attempt at
the experiment silently measured the old code; and an absence of evidence is never evidence about the system until the
instrument is known good.

**The server minor-and-patch group was taken on 2026-10-15** — `express` 5.2.1 → 5.3.0, `dotenv` 18.0.7 →
18.0.8, `mongoose` 9.11.1 → 9.11.2, `@types/node` 26.6.4 → 26.6.5 and `typescript-eslint` 8.71.1 →
8.71.2 — verified green (`tsc`, `eslint`, both module-graph checks, the full suite, `npm ci` and the boot
smoke) and recorded in roadmap **§27CC**. It is **not** a security fix and is not described as one: the CVEs
express 5.3.0's release notes name were **already closed by the lockfile** (`qs` 6.16.0 and `proxy-addr`
2.0.8 were already resolved), so what moved is the *declared* floor. **Nothing was taken in `mobile/`** —
§27CA records why: three of its five advisories have **no patched release published upstream at all**, and
the one patch that exists breaks its own consumer. The runtime floor is unchanged (express 5.3.0 requires
Node `>= 18`, below this project's `>= 24`), and the install step is unchanged (`npm ci` ⇒ exit 0, the
lockfile being in sync being the thing that check proves).

**Records are proportional:** a security, architecture, authorization, schema, route,
dependency-policy or phase-level change gets a dated record; a routine feature or fix is
documented by its pull request description, its tests and the code. Do not restate what the
roadmap already records. Nothing above changes what must be verified — never claim a check you
did not run.

### Detailed history lives outside this file

The prose record of every phase and increment listed above is kept in
`docs/PHASE_HISTORY.md`, so it does not load into every session. The authoritative records are
`docs/DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md` (per-phase, with evidence) and
`docs/AUTH_SECURITY_MASTER_CHECKLIST.md` (security-relevant work). **Current open work, known issues and the next-step order live in `docs/DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md` §27AH** — read that section at the start of a session instead of re-auditing the repository.

### Records are proportional to the change

- A **security, architecture, authorization, schema, route, dependency-policy or phase-level**
  change gets a dated record with its evidence in the roadmap and, when it is security-relevant,
  the checklist.
- A **routine feature or fix** is documented by its pull request description, its tests, and the
  code. Do not open a second parallel narrative for it, and do not restate what the roadmap
  already records.
- Never claim verification that was not performed, and keep failures and limitations visible.

When instructed to work on a specific phase:

- Implement that phase only.
- Do not jump ahead.
- Do not implement future features without explicit approval.
- Do not silently expand scope.

If a future feature is required to properly implement the current phase, explain the dependency before making major changes.

---

# 8. Code Quality

Prefer:

- Clear naming
- Small focused modules
- Reusable services
- Separation of concerns
- Consistent error handling
- Input validation
- Appropriate logging
- Testable business logic

Avoid:

- Duplicate logic
- Giant files
- Unnecessary abstractions
- Dead code
- Hardcoded configuration
- Magic values
- Unused dependencies

---

# 9. Backend Rules

Backend responsibilities include:

- Authentication
- Authorization
- Validation
- Business logic
- Inventory calculations
- Database operations
- Audit logging
- AI tool execution

Controllers should not contain excessive business logic.

Keep business logic in appropriate services/modules.

Database access should remain organized and maintainable.

---

# 10. Mobile Rules

The mobile application communicates with the backend through the API.

Do not place business-critical authorization logic only in the mobile app.

The mobile application should:

- Handle loading states
- Handle errors
- Handle empty states
- Validate basic user input
- Provide clear feedback
- Avoid unnecessary API calls
- Use efficient list rendering

Mobile UX should remain simple and practical.

---

# 11. AI Rules

The AI assistant must use controlled tools.

Initial tools may include:

```text
searchProducts
getProduct
getInventory
getLowStockProducts
getOutOfStockProducts
getInventorySummary
getInventoryHistory
getProductsByLocation
getReorderNeededProducts
listLocations
listCategories
```

The last two are reference lookups (AI-14): `getProductsByLocation` and the
category filters take IDs, and these are the only way to turn a name a member
said into one. They read the same records as `GET /api/locations` and
`GET /api/categories`, which are authentication-only, so they are gated on the
read permission of the capability they serve and can narrow nobody.

AI tools must receive authenticated user context.

Every tool must enforce:

```text
User
 ↓
Authentication
 ↓
Role Authorization
 ↓
Business Isolation
 ↓
Database Query
```

Never allow the AI model to generate arbitrary MongoDB queries.

Never trust an AI-supplied `businessId`.

Never allow AI-generated text to override backend authorization.

---

# 12. AI Accuracy

The AI must not invent database facts.

For inventory quantities, products, stock levels, locations, and history:

- Retrieve authoritative information from backend tools.
- Base the response on tool results.
- Clearly state when information is unavailable.
- Do not guess missing inventory information.

---

# 13. Testing

After implementing a feature, run appropriate checks and tests.

Critical areas include:

- Authentication
- Authorization
- Business isolation
- Product operations
- Inventory operations
- Stock-in
- Stock-out
- Negative stock prevention
- Transaction creation
- Dashboard calculations
- AI tool authorization

Do not claim a feature works without verifying it.

---

# 14. Verification Requirement

After implementation:

1. Run relevant tests.
2. Run lint/type/syntax checks when available.
3. Verify the affected functionality.
4. Review for security issues.
5. Check that unrelated functionality was not unnecessarily changed.
6. Fix problems introduced by the current task.

If something cannot be verified, explicitly report it.

---

# 15. Documentation

Update documentation when implementation changes an architectural or functional decision.

Primary specification:

`docs/SYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md`

Do not silently contradict the specification.

If an intentional architecture change is required:

1. Explain the conflict.
2. Propose the change.
3. Obtain approval before making a major architectural change.

---

# 16. Git

Use meaningful commits.

Examples:

```text
feat: add product model
feat: implement stock in
feat: implement stock out
fix: prevent negative inventory
feat: add inventory history
feat: add read-only AI tools
test: add inventory authorization tests
```

Never commit:

```text
.env
API keys
MongoDB credentials
Private certificates
```

### Inventory AI — Project Identity & Git Attribution

The project identity belongs to Jeffern. Claude Code is only an implementation tool and must NOT be represented as a project author, contributor, co-author, or attribution identity.

Canonical project identity:

* Project: Inventory AI
* Owner: Jeffern
* GitHub display name: Jeffernzxc
* GitHub username: nreffejzxc
* Git identity: jeffernzxc
* Commit email: jefferndullamalinaozxc@gmail.com
* Implementation: Jeffern
* Attribution: Jeffern / Jeffernzxc only

#### Git commit rules

These rules are **tool-agnostic and forward-binding**: they name Claude Code and Codebuff only as
instances. Any AI agent, coding tool, bot or automation is covered, and so is any tool introduced later.

1. Do NOT add a co-author trailer for an AI agent, a coding tool, a bot or automation. Forbidden
   examples, by name:
   `Co-Authored-By: Claude Code <noreply@anthropic.com>`,
   `Co-Authored-By: Codebuff <noreply@codebuff.com>`.

2. Do NOT add a generated-by tag, signature line or automated footer to a commit body or trailer.
   Forbidden examples, by name: `🤖 Generated with Codebuff`, `Generated with Codebuff`,
   `Generated with Claude Code`, `Generated by Claude`, `Made with …`, and any emoji-prefixed
   attribution line.

3. A normal commit message is the Inventory AI change and nothing else — subject, optional body
   explaining *why*, and no attribution of any kind. **The only identity that may appear anywhere in a
   commit, branch, pull request, changelog or release note is Jeffern's own name and the commit email
   above.**

4. Do NOT identify Claude Code, Anthropic, Codebuff or any other tool as the author, co-author,
   contributor, implementer or owner of Inventory AI. Implementing, modifying, testing, debugging and
   documenting the project does not make a tool an author or contributor.

5. Git author **and** committer identity must be the repository's configured Jeffern/jeffernzxc
   identity. Verify with `git config user.name` / `git config user.email` before creating a commit; the
   two must be `jeffernzxc` and `jefferndullamalinaozxc@gmail.com`. If either differs, **report the
   mismatch** — never silently change Git identity.

6. Before creating any commit, read back what you are about to record —
   `git log -1 --format=%B` on the staged commit, or the message you are about to pass — and confirm it
   carries no trailer other than the owner's own. A trailer that was never added cannot be reviewed
   away later.

7. **Nothing in this repository may add a trailer automatically.** No `commit-msg` or
   `prepare-commit-msg` hook, no commit template (`commit.template`), no alias and no script may append
   one. The unwanted trailers found in pre-2026-10-11 history came from a coding agent's own
   commit-message template — not from Git — so that template must not be reintroduced.

8. This rule applies to all future commits, branches, pull requests, pull request descriptions, reviews,
   changelogs, release notes and project documentation, unless Jeffern explicitly overrides it for one
   specific case.

9. Rewriting history stays forbidden **except on Jeffern's explicit request.** Jeffern requested it once,
   on 2026-10-11, to remove the already-published attribution described in rule 1 and rule 2; that rewrite
   is recorded in `docs/DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md` §27AS. That single request does not stand as standing
   permission, and no further history rewrite may be started without a new, explicit request.

10. When a history rewrite is authorised, it must be **message-only** (file contents and trees untouched,
    proved by comparing tree hashes before and after), must preserve every commit's work, must be
    reported with its impact on branches, pull requests and deployments, and must use
    `--force-with-lease` — never an unguarded force push.

---

# 17. Scope Protection

Do NOT implement the following unless explicitly requested:

- Payments
- Subscriptions
- SaaS billing
- Full offline synchronization
- AI write actions
- AI forecasting
- Demand prediction
- RAG
- Vector Search
- Advanced supplier management
- Automated purchasing
- Advanced analytics

These belong to future versions.

---

# 18. Working Style

When given a task, follow this sequence:

```text
READ
  ↓
INSPECT
  ↓
PLAN
  ↓
IMPLEMENT
  ↓
TEST
  ↓
VERIFY
  ↓
DOCUMENT
  ↓
REPORT
```

Do not rush directly into implementation.

Before making substantial changes, understand the existing project.

---

# 19. Completion Report

After completing a task, report:

### Implemented

What was changed.

### Files Changed

Important files created or modified.

### Verification

Tests/checks that were executed and their results.

### Issues

Any remaining problems or limitations.

### Next Step

The recommended next development step.

Keep reports concise and factual.

---

# 20. Final Rule

When uncertain about implementation details, prioritize:

1. `docs/SYSTEM_SPECIFICATION.md`
2. Existing project architecture
3. Security
4. Data integrity
5. Maintainability
6. Simplicity

Never sacrifice security, authorization, business isolation, or inventory consistency for convenience.
